### Aiven Free Plan

- **Aiven Free Tier (always-free managed PostgreSQL, MySQL, Kafka, OpenSearch, Valkey)** (high) — Aiven's always-free tier covers 5 services at $0/month: PostgreSQL (1GB storage, 1GB RAM, 1 CPU/VM, single node, network egress included), MySQL (1GB storage, 1GB RAM, network egress included), Apache Kafka (up to 250 KiB/s combined produce+consume, max 5 topics x 2 partitions, up to 3-day retention, free Karapace Schema Registry + REST proxy, only ONE free Kafka service per organization, no Kafka Connect/MirrorMaker 2/integrations/tiered storage), OpenSearch (4GB RAM, 20GB persistent storage, up to 20 shards, single node, k-NN/vector enabled by default), and Valkey (1GB RAM, network egress included). It is a permanent free tier, not a trial: no expiry, no time limit, no credit card required. Free services are automatically powered off during extended inactivity and can be reactivated anytime; Aiven reserves the right to shut down services that violate acceptable use or are unused for extended periods. No SLA; support is documentation-only. No specific cloud/region can be chosen (geographical area only, e.g. Europe/NA/APAC).
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone — no .edu email, no age cap, no company verification, no region-of-residence restriction, and no credit card. Free of any trial or time limit. The only implicit caps: one free Kafka service per organization, and per-service resource caps listed above. ClickHouse has NO free plan.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up free at aiven.io (no credit card, no trial form), open the Aiven Console, create a service (PostgreSQL/MySQL/Kafka/OpenSearch/Valkey) and select the free plan — service is live in minutes. Startup credits: apply via the form at aiven.io/startups (Typeform), acceptance ~2 business days, 15-min kickoff call, then apply the credit code to your Aiven account.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry or renewal — the free tier persists indefinitely within limits; services power off on prolonged inactivity but can be reactivated at any time. Aiven can discontinue/reduce the program (they did reduce storage in 2025).
  - *Gotchas:* Not a trial, but: (1) idle services auto-power-off, so no uptime SLA and no production use; (2) since 15 May 2025, PostgreSQL/MySQL free storage is only 1GB (was 5GB) — a full 5GB-database won't fit; (3) network egress is included only up to the free tier's stated network allowance — the free-tier page labels PG/MySQL/Valkey as 'network costs' included, verify on the plan page if your traffic is heavy; (4) you cannot pick a specific cloud/region on free plans, so cross-cloud peering/VPC features are unavailable; (5) no connection pooling or service integrations on free PostgreSQL; (6) the paid upgrade path is the new $5/month Developer tier (launched Nov–Dec 2025) with 8GB storage and no auto power-off; (7) one-off prize competitions (Dec 2025 $5,000 free-Kafka competition closed 31 Jan 2026; $1,000 free-tier competition closed 17 Apr 2026) are marketing campaigns, not recurring credits.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/free-tier;https://aiven.io/pricing;https://aiven.io/free-postgresql-database;https://aiven.io/free-opensearch;https://aiven.io/changelog/6d8b5811-882e-4a87-a346-66fbca7ed709
- **Aiven for Startups (formerly 'Cluster') — up to $100,000 platform credits over 12 months** (high) — Aiven for Startups (the program formerly called 'Aiven Cluster', revamped Aug 2023) grants from $12,000 up to $100,000 in Aiven platform credits usable over 12 months across all managed services (Kafka, PostgreSQL, MySQL, OpenSearch, ClickHouse, Valkey, etc.), plus startup-Slack community, technical guidance/office hours from Aiven solution architects, promotion on Aiven channels, and virtual events. Partner-marketplace listings (e.g. Stand Together Ventures, Aug 2026) state explicit stage-based tiers: $12K pre-seed, $30K seed, $75K Series A, $100K Series B, with the option to upgrade tier as the company grows; the $12K tier also exists for startups with no partner affiliation. Credits are valid 12 months; no payment commitment after they expire.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup founded no more than 7 years ago; external funding raised from pre-seed up to and including Series B; application with company website + business email (personal emails rejected as verification); preferred affiliation with an Aiven ecosystem partner (VC, accelerator, incubator — e.g. Techstars, Digital Ocean Hatch which offers a $10K variant, Aalto Startup Center, SMU iie with $30K). Excluded: agencies, consulting firms, NGOs, and educational institutions; applicants must be a new Aiven customer or a paying customer with monthly invoice under $400, and must not have previously received startup credits. No .edu requirement (it is a company program, not a student one).
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the Aiven for Startups form (aiven.io/startups, Typeform: aiven-startups.typeform.com/to/STphOA6R); select your VC/accelerator affiliation if you have one; on acceptance (~2 business days) you get a welcome email, book a 15-min kick-off call with the Startup Program Manager, and receive a credit code; sign up on Aiven, apply the code, and spend credits on any managed service for 12 months.
  - *Expiry:* Credits must be used within 12 months of receipt; no auto-renewal. Program has been running since Nov 2021 (launched as Cluster), revamped Aug 2023 (funding cap raised Series A→B, age cap 5→7 years, new $12K tier), and is branded 'Aiven for Startups' as of 2026.
  - *Gotchas:* The official page says amounts 'vary based on your startup's credit tier' without publishing the per-stage numbers — the $12K/$30K/$75K/$100K mapping comes from partner listings, not the main page. Credits are not cash: they offset Aiven service spend only, and unspent credits lapse after 12 months. Approval is discretionary ('apply anyway' is encouraged but not guaranteed). A partner (VC/accelerator/incubator) affiliation is effectively expected for the higher tiers. The Digital Ocean Hatch variant caps at $10,000 even though the headline is $100K. Verify current form/eligibility in the Typeform at application time.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/startups;https://aiven.io/press/aiven-unveils-revamped-cluster-startup-program;https://aiven.io/guide-for-startups
- **No dedicated student/education program (negative finding)** (medium) — No dedicated education program, .edu license, student discount, or student credits program was found in Aiven's official pages or press releases as of 2026-10-22. Aiven supports students indirectly: (1) the always-free tier is explicitly marketed for 'learning databases & streaming' and is open to any individual — a student with a personal email can get free managed PostgreSQL/MySQL/Kafka/OpenSearch/Valkey with no credit card; (2) free online 'Aiven Developer Workshops' (aiven.io/developer-workshops) are open education events; (3) universities (Aalto Startup Center, SMU iie) partner on the STARTUP program side, where their incubatee startups (companies, not students) get $30K–$100K credits. Notably, the startup program explicitly EXCLUDES educational institutions as applicants. Confidence is medium rather than high because the web-search budget for this session was exhausted before a final confirming sweep of aiven.io/education-style URLs could run.
  - *Eligibility:* Not applicable — no student/education offer exists to qualify for. (Students qualify for the general free tier by simply having an Aiven account; no .edu email required or beneficial.)
  - *How to claim:* No application or license step — students simply use the always-free tier (aiven.io/free-tier, no credit card). Free Aiven Developer Workshops (aiven.io/developer-workshops) are open, free training sessions on PostgreSQL/Kafka/OpenSearch.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse university partner listings (Aalto, SMU) — those are startup-company offers for incubatees, not student licenses; the startup program's terms bar educational institutions. If Aiven ever launches an education program it would likely surface at aiven.io/startups or the press room; check the free-tier page, not a nonexistent '/education' page.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/free-tier;https://aiven.io/developer-workshops;https://startupcenter.aalto.fi/services-for-our-network/startup-growth-tools
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes that materially altered the free landscape: (1) 15 May 2025 — free-plan storage for PostgreSQL and MySQL reduced from 5GB to 1GB for all new and existing free services (changelog aiven.io/changelog/6ad6c429-6c1c-4418-abfb-2ca82f927414); no data loss, connections unchanged, but hobbyists needing more must move to paid 'hobbyist/Developer' plans. (2) Nov 12 / Dec 10 2025 — a new $5/month Developer tier launched for PostgreSQL and MySQL (1 CPU, 1GB RAM, 8GB storage, Basic support, services never auto-power-off, region area but not cloud selection); one changelog dated Nov 12 2025 says the Postgres Developer plan starts 'at $8 USD' while the Aiven blog states $5 — treat $5/month as the advertised starting price and verify in-console. (3) Dec 10 2025 — the Kafka free tier itself is new (previously Kafka had no $0 plan); before that date the free tier was PostgreSQL/MySQL/OpenSearch/Valkey only. (4) The startup program rebranded from 'Aiven Cluster Startup Program' to 'Aiven for Startups' (revamp in Aug 2023: age cap 5→7 years, funding cap Series A→B, new $12K non-partner tier; current form is aiven.io/startups). (5) One-off prize competitions, not recurring offers: $5,000 Free Kafka competition (closed 31 Jan 2026) and $1,000 Free Tier competition (closed 17 Apr 2026, winner announced 27 Apr 2026). Nothing was shut down in this window; the only reductions were the 2025 free-tier storage cut and Kafka's free-service-per-org limit (1).

### Aiven Startup Program

- **Aiven Free Tier ($0/month, perpetual)** (high) — Standing $0/month free plan, no time limit, no credit card or payment details required. One free single-node service per type per account. Caps (network/egress costs still apply): PostgreSQL 1GB storage / 1GB RAM; MySQL 1GB storage / 1GB RAM; Valkey 1GB RAM; Apache Kafka up to 250 kb/s IN and OUT with retention up to 3 days; OpenSearch 20GB storage / 4GB RAM / up to 20 shards. Free services are single-node (multi-node HA is paid-only), no integrations or connection pooling. Aiven may shut down services that violate acceptable-use or stay unused an extended period.
  - *Eligibility:* Any user — no .edu, no company email, no funding/age cap, no credit card. Open to students and individuals.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Aiven account at console.aiven.io/signup (no payment details required), then deploy the desired service (ready in minutes). Upgrade to paid is one-click with no data migration/downtime.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry / no trial clock. 'Always free.' Aiven reserves the right to shut down unused or policy-violating services.
  - *Gotchas:* Capped resources, not for production; network/egress costs are not included; one free instance per service type; deleting a service deletes its data; Kafka retention max 3 days on free tier.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/free-tier
- **30-day free trial / new-account credits** (medium) — Pricing FAQ offers a 'free 30-day trial' when you need more memory or advanced features. The Developer-tier page states new accounts get '$300 in free credits' and 'no credit card required.' The two pages describe the offer differently (the $300 figure appears only on the Developer-tier page, not on pricing), so treat the exact credit amount as unconfirmed.
  - *Eligibility:* New Aiven accounts; no .edu requirement; no credit card required per the Developer-tier page.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at console.aiven.io/signup and start a trial / pick a plan beyond the free tier. No card needed to start.
  - *Expiry:* 30-day window for the trial; free credits are new-account promotional credits. End-of-trial outcome (downgrade vs auto-charge) not stated on the page — consult docs; auto-billing behavior not confirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs free-tier distinction: the 30-day trial is for higher-memory/advanced features, not the perpetual free plan. $300 figure not corroborated on the pricing page, so verify in-console before relying on it. Whether a card is required after trial to continue is not confirmed.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/pricing
- **Aiven Startup Program (startup-credits)** (high) — Up to $100,000 in Aiven credits over a 12-month period (tiers from $12,000 up to $100,000), tiered by the startup's stage of growth and funding. Credits expire if unused by the end of the 12-month program. Plus perks: 1:1 technical expert mentoring/office hours, Aiven startup Slack community, blog/social/event feature opportunities, exclusive early-stage virtual events; perks are unlocked in levels based on activity/usage. Aiven may extend credits (not guaranteed, needs strong growth reasoning) and reserves the right to cancel on T&C breach/misuse. No credit card or payment requirement is stated on the page.
  - *Eligibility:* Company founded no more than 7 years ago; external funding from pre-seed through Series B; must apply with a company/business email; proof of affiliation with a VC investor and/or an Aiven ecosystem partner (or an affiliated accelerator/incubator/co-working space) helps. Agencies, consulting firms, NGOs, and educational institutions are explicitly excluded. Existing Aiven customers with a monthly invoice under $400 may still qualify case-by-case. .edu email is NOT required.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the application via the Typeform on the startups page (primary: aiven-startups.typeform.com/to/STphOA6R; secondary: form.typeform.com/to/BqNrcMLJ). Reviewed on a rolling monthly basis, ~14 days for review. Once accepted, reserve a required personal kickoff session.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 12 months and expire unused; one-time only ('you can only receive Startup credits once'). No program-wide sunset date is published.
  - *Gotchas:* You can only receive the startup credits once. Perks/credits are level-gated on activity. One-time + 12-month expiry means no renewal. No sunset date stated, so confirm still-accepting status at the Typeform.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/startups
- **Education license / .edu student program** (high) — No dedicated education or student program exists. aiven.io/education returns HTTP 404. The free tier and Developer-tier FAQ mention students/solo developers/side projects as an audience, but there is no separate .edu-verified education license, no academic discount, and no student-only offer.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no such offer found. Students are covered only via the general Free Tier (no .edu, no card).
  - *How to claim:* N/A — nothing to claim; use the general Free Tier for student projects.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Do not assume an education license; verify only against aiven.io/free-tier and aiven.io/developer-tier.
  - *Source:* https://aiven.io/education
- **2025–26 change:** No free offer was sunset in 2025–2026. The aiven.io/education URL 404s (no dedicated education/.edu license has ever been offered; students use the general Free Tier). A dedicated /startup-program and /blog/aiven-startup-program URLs 404; the live startup program lives at aiven.io/startups and is currently active (Typeform applications open). WebSearch budget was exhausted this session, so 2025–2026 change claims could not be verified via search; findings above were read directly from the live official pages (aiven.io/free-tier, aiven.io/pricing, aiven.io/developer-tier, aiven.io/startups) today, so current-state detail is high-confidence. The $300 new-account credit figure and the 30-day trial end-of-trial behavior (auto-charge vs downgrade vs data deletion) are not fully corroborated on the official pages — verify in-console before relying on them.

### AWS (Amazon Web Services)

- **AWS Free plan (new-account credits: $100 base + up to $100 earned, 6-month window)** (high) — As of 2026 AWS replaced the classic 12-month per-service free tier with a Free plan vs Paid plan model. New accounts are auto-enrolled in the Free plan and get $100 in AWS credits immediately, plus up to $100 more earned by completing 'Explore AWS' activities (up to $200 total). The Free plan runs at no cost for up to 6 months or until credits run out, whichever comes first, and the account closes automatically. Base credits expire 6 months from account creation; activity-earned credits expire 12 months; if you upgrade to the Paid plan remaining credits apply to bills and expire 12 months after account creation. 30+ 'always free' services with monthly caps remain on both plans. A payment method is NOT required to sign up for most new customers (a major change from the old credit-card-required model). Credits are USD, non-transferable between accounts, and can offset usage on eligible services including Amazon Bedrock model invocations and AI/ML workloads. Featured Bedrock access on the Free plan; Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is listed as a Paid-plan-exclusive service.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new AWS account (personal or business). No .edu requirement, no funding criteria, no credit card required for most customers. Free plan restricts access to a subset of services; Paid plan allows a monthly spend limit starting at $20.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a new AWS account (auto-enrolls in Free plan, no payment method needed for most customers) -> 2) $100 credits are applied on signup -> 3) Complete Explore AWS activities in the console to earn up to $100 more -> 4) Use credits on eligible services incl. Bedrock within the 6-month window -> 5) Optionally upgrade to Paid plan; remaining credits roll onto invoices until 12-month expiry.
  - *Expiry:* Base $100 credits: 6 months from account creation. Earned up to $100: 12 months from account creation. Free plan itself auto-closes 6 months after account open or when credits are exhausted. No renewal or extension; credits cannot be transferred.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free confusion: this is a 6-month credit window, NOT the old 12-month tier. Once upgraded to the Paid plan you CANNOT downgrade back to Free. Legacy per-service allowances (EC2 750 hrs, S3 5 GB, Bedrock free-token lists) no longer appear in the new structure. Data transfer/egress and some advanced features may be excluded or Paid-plan-only. The old Bedrock 12-month free-token offer is gone from the free-tier FAQ and docs (see sunset notes). Credits only offset eligible services - check the Free Tier Categories list.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/(alsohttps://aws.amazon.com/free/)
- **AWS Activate Founders tier (self-serve; $1,000 start, up to $5,000)** (high) — The self-serve AWS Activate tier. $1,000 in AWS Activate Credits to start; select startups may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000 over time. Credits (USD, subject to AWS Promotional Credit T&Cs) can offset 200+ eligible AWS services including third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock (Anthropic Claude, OpenAI, Mistral AI, Meta, etc.) via a single API. Application is reviewed within 5-10 business days.
  - *Eligibility:* Self-funded / bootstrapped (no VC or accelerator backing needed). Must be: new to AWS Activate Credits, have a fully functioning company website, founded in the past 10 years, Pre-Series B, and have an AWS account on a Paid Tier plan (note: the new paid-plan requirement means the free-plan-only account must be upgraded). No .edu requirement. Business email matching the startup domain is required for the application.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create/verify an AWS Builder ID (personal email) -> 2) Complete the one-time AWS Activate profile with your business email -> 3) Choose the Founders tier -> 4) Provide startup details (product, funding stage, company profile) -> 5) Link an AWS account (on Paid plan) and submit -> 6) Hear back in 5-10 business days. Apply at https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ ('Apply here' / 'Join now' on https://aws.amazon.com/startups/).
  - *Expiry:* Promotional credits: no public per-tier expiry stated on the credits page today; AWS has said Activate credits generally expire in 1-3 years depending on package (the $200K-class credits are on 3-year expiry per the 2024 announcement). Credits are in USD, subject to Promotional Credit T&Cs, non-transferable.
  - *Gotchas:* Only $1,000 is guaranteed; the $5,000 extension is selective. 'New to Activate Credits' - you cannot re-claim unless you are requesting more than previously received. Egress/data-transfer and some fees may be excluded from promotional credits (check the credits FAQ 'What is not covered'). Requiring a Paid-plan AWS account means a card/payment setup may be needed at the account level even though the Free plan itself needs no payment method.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Activate Portfolio tier (provider-backed; up to $200,000)** (high) — Up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits (the tier ceiling was $100,000 until the June 2024 doubling for Series A-stage startups; the credits page now lists a single Portfolio tier up to $200,000). Usable on 200+ services including third-party Bedrock models (Anthropic, OpenAI, Mistral, Meta, etc.), and can also be applied to Developer/Business AWS Support Plans. Program has provided $8B+ in credits to 350,000+ startups since 2013.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series B (most recent funding round within the last 12 months, if applicable), founded in the past 10 years, fully functioning company website, AWS account on a Paid Tier plan, new to Activate Credits or requesting more than previously received, AND an Organization ID (Org ID) from an AWS Activate Provider (VC firm, accelerator, incubator or angel group in the AWS network - e.g. YC, Sequoia, a16z, Greylock). Apply within 12 months of your most recent funding date.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Confirm your VC/accelerator/angel is an AWS Activate Provider and obtain their Org ID -> 2) Create AWS Builder ID + complete Activate profile with business email -> 3) Choose Portfolio tier and enter the provider's Org ID (or use a direct provider invite) -> 4) Link AWS account and submit at https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ -> 5) Reviewed in 5-10 business days; approved credits are added to your AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* The $200K credit package expires 3 years from issuance (per the June 2024 change; previously the $100K package expired in 1 year). USD, subject to Promotional Credit T&Cs, non-transferable.
  - *Gotchas:* You cannot apply yourself without a provider's Org ID or invite - a consulting partner cannot file it for you. 'Within 12 months of your latest funding date' is a hard window. Credits cannot be sold or transferred; check the 'not covered' list (data transfer and some support fees historically excluded, though Support Plan application is permitted). The old ~$100K ceiling is outdated in most third-party guides.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Credits for AI Startups (invite-only; $200,000+)** (high) — A new tier launched with the 2026 credits-page redesign: $200,000+ in AWS Credits for AI startups 'ready to scale' beyond the Activate tiers. No open application exists - it is invite-only and accessed by talking to your AWS Account Manager. Additional credits beyond Activate are explicitly marketed for AI startups building on Amazon Bedrock (funding models from Anthropic, OpenAI, Mistral, Meta, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* Startups that have already gone through Activate Portfolio and are ready to scale (AI-focused). No public age/funding/.edu criteria published; eligibility is determined case-by-case by the account team. No credit-card specifics published (linked to the existing AWS account).
  - *How to claim:* No self-serve claim. 1) Be an AWS Activate participant (Portfolio tier) -> 2) Contact your AWS Account Manager (or use 'If you need additional support, contact us' on https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/) -> 3) AWS reviews and issues credits to your AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* Not published today; by analogy with the $200K Activate package, 3-year expiry is expected but unconfirmed. USD, promotional T&Cs apply.
  - *Gotchas:* Invite-only means no guaranteed outcome or timeline; it is not a free product - it is a promotional credit negotiation. Amounts are not fixed ('$200,000+' is a floor, not a promise).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Kiro Startup Credits (up to a year of Kiro Pro+ credits; Starter/Growth/Scale tiers)** (high) — New AWS Kiro agentic-IDE credit offer (linked from the AWS credits page: 'Start building on day one... Start with Kiro Startup Credits, then grow into Activate Credits'). Up to one year's worth of Kiro Pro+ credits in three tiers by team size: Starter (up to 2 Kiro users), Growth (up to 10 users), Scale (up to 30 users). Credits expire 1 year after issuance to your AWS account; they cannot be refunded, re-issued, transferred or sold. Separately, Kiro main offers a $20 credit when you first sign up for a paid Kiro plan via social login or Builder ID.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage through Series A startups, global. Requires an official business email on the startup's own domain AND an AWS Account ID using a matching email. EXCLUDED REGIONS: Argentina, Belarus, Brazil, China/GCR, Cuba, France, Germany, Iran, Italy, Mexico, North Korea, Poland, Russia, Spain, Syria, Crimea, DNR, LNR, UAE. Startups with active AWS Activate Credits are NOT eligible for this promotion (though Activate credits can still be applied toward Kiro). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in with your AWS account -> 2) Apply via 'Apply for startup credits' (routes to https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/kiro) -> 3) Rolling review; decision by email -> 4) Approved credits are automatically added to your AWS account and activate immediately (no deferral) -> 5) To use them, log into Kiro via AWS IAM Identity Center (Kiro Enterprise) - social login (GitHub/Google) and Builder ID do NOT work with startup credits. After credits run out, standard Kiro Pro+ billing applies.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 1 year from date of issuance to the AWS account. One application per startup at a time; re-application allowed only if eligibility status changed after a rejection. After 12 months of credit consumption you move to paid Kiro Pro+.
  - *Gotchas:* The excluded-region list is long (much of the EU/MEA/APAC). Activation cannot be deferred, so issuance date = countdown start. Company-level credit pool shared only among authorized users of associated accounts. It is mutually exclusive with holding active AWS Activate Credits.
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/startups/
- **Kiro Student Offer (1,000 credits/month free for 12 months)** (high) — Kiro's student program: 1,000 Kiro credits per month at no cost for one full year, explicitly 'no credit card required'. After the year, the account auto-downgrades to the public Kiro free tier (projects/specs stay on your machine and repos; no discounted paid plan for students). 1,000 is the Kiro credit unit for model usage (Kiro Pro+ is its paid tier).
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled student at one of ~150+ listed eligible universities (US-centric: MIT, CMU, NYU, Georgia Tech, ASU, etc., plus international). Account must use a university-affiliated email (.edu, .ca or equivalent) via Google, GitHub, or AWS Builder ID. Must be at least 18 years old. If your school isn't listed you can submit your university email through a 'Don't see your school listed?' form and Kiro may reach out later.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create or log into a Kiro account using your university email -> 2) On the account usage page, a banner appears if eligible; click verify and complete a SheerID check (first/last name, DOB, university; sometimes student ID or enrollment letter) -> 3) Once verified, the 1,000 credits/month activate automatically.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from verification, then auto-downgrade to the public free tier. Credits do NOT roll over between monthly cycles; hitting the 1,000 cap means waiting for the next cycle; no add-on credit purchases on the student tier.
  - *Gotchas:* No rollover of unused monthly credits; the ~150-university list is the real gate (many schools not included); SheerID can delay activation; after year one there is no student discount - only the base free tier. This is Kiro (agentic IDE credits for coding with frontier models, incl. Claude-class models), NOT Amazon Bedrock API access - for direct Bedrock API use, the AWS Free plan credits or Activate credits are the path.
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/students/
- **AWS Academy (education license - free courses, not cloud credits)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — AWS Academy is AWS's education program (successor to AWS Educate, renamed ~2021) giving students and educators free structured AWS courses, hands-on labs via partner platforms, and discount pathways toward certification. It is a training/education license: it does NOT include AWS credits, Bedrock access, or LLM free tiers - students who want actual Bedrock usage must use the AWS Free plan, Activate, or Kiro offers above. (Could not be re-verified live today: https://aws.amazon.com/academy/ returned 404 and the training sub-page was unreachable; details from general knowledge.)
  - *Eligibility:* Open to students and teachers at partner institutions; typically .edu/institutional affiliation or partnership enrollment (e.g., via AWS Academy Accelerate Your Career or institutional programs). No credit card needed for the free learning content.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Enroll through your institution's AWS Academy partnership or an open course on the AWS Academy/Skill Builder platform -> 2) Complete free courses -> 3) Certifications and advanced access are paid or partner-subsidized.
  - *Expiry:* N/A - ongoing educational access; no credit expiry because no credits are granted.
  - *Gotchas:* Not an LLM offer at all - it is courses/labs. Do not confuse with free Bedrock or Kiro student credits. Institutional access rules vary by country/partner.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/academy/
- **AWS Free Account (new credit-based Free plan) — the restructured AWS Free Tier** (high) — New AWS accounts automatically enter a credit-based Free plan: $100 USD credits at sign-up + up to $100 more earned by activating foundational services = up to $200 total, spendable across 90+ services. Free plan runs up to 6 months (account auto-closes at 6 months or when credits run out, whichever is first). 30+ services also have recurring 'Always Free' monthly limits on both plans. On the Paid-plan path, the same credits are valid for up to 12 months from account creation. Free-plan accounts are migrated from the legacy usage-based 12-month Free Tier for accounts created on/after July 15, 2025; a new sign-up experience launched October 2026 (older accounts still use the legacy credits console view).
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (existing/previous account holders excluded). No .edu requirement; open to students and startups alike. Payment method NOT required to sign up for most new customers, but AWS may request a payment method or extra info to verify identity. Unavailable in China (Beijing and Ningxia) regions and AWS GovCloud (US) (with a note that some services such as Lambda are exceptions in GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a new AWS (Builder ID) account via the registration portal — Free plan is selected automatically; 2) use the $100 signup credit, then earn up to $100 more by activating foundational services (credits appear in billing console within ~30 min); 3) optionally upgrade to Paid (remaining credits become bill credits expiring 12 months post-creation).
  - *Expiry:* Free-plan credits: 6 months from account opening or exhaustion, whichever first; account then suspends, data retained 90 days, then account and data permanently erased if not upgraded. Paid-path credits: 12 months from account creation. No renewal; downgrading Paid back to Free is not allowed.
  - *Gotchas:* Legacy 12-month per-service Free Tier no longer exists for new accounts. Activating 'advanced features' is irreversible and voids remaining earnable credits; joining an AWS Organization or deploying a Control Tower landing zone immediately voids remaining earnable credits. Paid-only services are excluded from the Free plan. After 6 months the account closes itself — you lose access to resources/data unless you upgrade within the 90-day grace window. Egress/data transfer and overages beyond limits are paid.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/(plushttps://aws.amazon.com/free/)
- **AWS Activate — Founders tier (self-funded/bootsrap startups)** (high) — $1,000 in AWS Activate credits guaranteed; select participants may qualify for up to $5,000 over time. Credits apply to 200+ AWS services (compute, RDS, DynamoDB, S3, CloudFront) and Amazon Bedrock, so founders can evaluate/customize/deploy third-party models from OpenAI, Mistral AI, Anthropic, and Meta. Excluded: Mechanical Turk, AWS Managed Services, AWS Professional Services, AWS Training & Certification. Credits apply monthly to new usage, never retroactively. Application processed within 5-10 business days.
  - *Eligibility:* Self-funded/bootsrap startup (no VC backing needed), founded within the last 10 years, pre-Series B, AWS account on the Paid plan, either new to Activate credits or requesting more than previously received. No .edu or institutional requirement; professional email required for the Builder ID.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an AWS Builder ID with a professional email; 2) select the Founders tier at /startups/join?destination=/credits/apply; 3) provide product, funding-stage and company-profile details; 4) link your AWS account to the Builder ID and submit; decisions in 5-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Credits 'usually expire within 1-2 years depending on the package' (official FAQ wording). No renewal, but you may reapply if you request a higher amount than previously approved and haven't hit the lifetime credit cap.
  - *Gotchas:* The extra $4,000 (up to $5,000) is discretionary ('select participants'), not guaranteed. Requires a Paid-plan AWS account, so expect a billing relationship even if credits cover usage. Credits don't cover AWS Professional/Managed Services or certification fees. $1,000-5,000 amounts are small vs the Portfolio tier — check provider-backed tracks first if you have accelerator/VC contact.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/(tiers)andhttps://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/everything-you-need-to-know-about-aws-activate-credits/(expiry/Bedrockdetails)
- **AWS Activate — Portfolio tier (VC/accelerator/angel-funded startups)** (high) — Up to $200,000 in AWS Activate credits for VC/accelerator/angel-backed startups, claimed with an Organization ID issued by the participating provider (network includes Andreessen Horowitz, Brex, Carta, Greylock, Sequoia, Y Combinator; 'thousands' of providers). Providers structure multi-step offers (FAQ example: $25K at pre-seed stepping up to $100K at seed; you receive the difference between tiers). Credits usable on 200+ services including Amazon Bedrock third-party models (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta).
  - *Eligibility:* Backed by an Activate Provider (VC, accelerator, angel investor, or enabling organization); founded within the last 10 years; pre-Series B (the /free/offers/ hub phrases it as 'up to and including Pre-Seed, Angel, Seed, Debt Finance, and Series A'); must apply within 12 months of the most recent funding date; affiliation with the provider must still be active; AWS account on Paid plan; new to Activate or requesting more than previously received.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get the provider's Organization ID from your VC/accelerator; 2) apply at /startups/join?destination=/credits/apply selecting Portfolio tier and entering the Org ID; 3) submit company/funding details and link your AWS account; 4) response in 5-10 business days (offers hub says 7-10). Alternatively the provider portal can claim on your behalf.
  - *Expiry:* Per package, credits usually expire within 1-2 years (official FAQ). Reapply for a higher tier amount if you've raised further, as long as you're under the lifetime cap and still affiliated; you receive the difference between the old and new tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Without an active provider relationship you cannot access this tier (fall back to Founders). The /free/offers/ hub still shows the Activate range as '$1,000 to $100,000', contradicting the credits page/FAQ's $200,000 portfolio ceiling — the offers hub appears stale. Credits don't cover excluded services (Professional/Managed Services, Training & Certification) and never retroactively apply to past bills. Applying outside the 12-month post-funding window is not allowed.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/andhttps://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/everything-you-need-to-know-about-aws-activate-credits/
- **AWS Credits for AI Startups (invite-only, above the $200K Portfolio cap)** (high) — $200,000+ in additional AWS credits, positioned for 'startups ready to scale post Activate Portfolio' — i.e., AI startups that have exhausted the Portfolio ceiling. Official FAQ wording: 'Additional AWS Credits above $200,000 are available for AI startups ready to scale beyond Activate's tiers.' Invite only; no public dollar figure beyond '200,000+'.
  - *Eligibility:* Invite-only; AI-focused startups already at/above the $200,000 Activate Portfolio ceiling (or approaching it). AWS reaches out; existing customers work through their Account Manager. No public self-serve application.
  - *How to claim:* No public claim path. Routes: (a) AWS outreach/invitation; (b) ask your AWS Account Manager or Activate provider about additional AI-startup credits; (c) the /startups/credits/ page routes AI-tier interest to the account-manager channel.
  - *Expiry:* Governed by the AWS Promotional Credit T&C; per the Activate FAQ, Activate credits generally burn down over 1-2 years depending on package. Exact expiry for the AI tier is not published.
  - *Gotchas:* Amount, eligibility thresholds and even the program's public name are not itemized on the credits page — treat specifics as account-manager-mediated. Do not confuse with Kiro credits or the Founders/Portfolio tiers. Credits still exclude AWS Professional/Managed Services.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/('AWSCreditsforAIStartups'card)andFAQhttps://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/everything-you-need-to-know-about-aws-activate-credits/
- **Kiro startup credits (1 year of Kiro Pro+ IDE access)** (high) — Up to 12 months of Kiro Pro+ credits (AWS's agentic AI IDE) in three team tiers: Starter (up to 2 Kiro users), Growth (up to 10 users), Scale (up to 30 users). No USD figure published. Issued at the company level, so authorized users on associated AWS accounts can share it. Once exhausted, the account automatically switches to standard Kiro Pro+ billing.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage to Series A startups; a business email matching your company domain AND a matching AWS account ID. Startups with ACTIVE AWS Activate credits are NOT eligible (though existing Activate credits can be spent on Kiro). Regionally limited: available in most countries where AWS billing works, excluding Argentina, Belarus, Brazil, China regions, Cuba, France, Germany, Iran, Italy, Mexico, North Korea, Poland, Russia, Spain, Syria, Crimea/DNR/LNR, and UAE.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the AWS Startups portal at kiro.dev/startups (rolling review, email notification). On approval credits are automatically added to your AWS account; to use them you must log into Kiro via AWS IAM Identity Center (Kiro Enterprise) — GitHub/Google social login and AWS Builder ID will not work.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 1 year after issuance. One application per company; a rejected applicant whose eligibility later changes may reapply. No refund, reissue, transfer, or sale.
  - *Gotchas:* Marked 'for a limited time only' with no published end date. Choosing a plan above Pro+ or exceeding your tier's user cap triggers charges. If you're mid-Activate-credits, this offer is off the table (but your Activate credits already cover Kiro, so no loss).
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/startups/
- **Amazon RDS free database (legacy 12-month tier for accounts created before July 15, 2025)** (high) — For legacy accounts: up to 750 hours/month of a single-AZ instance on select database classes, across MySQL, MariaDB, PostgreSQL, or SQL Server (Express Edition only), plus 20 GB General Purpose SSD (gp2) storage and 20 GB automated backup storage per month, for up to 12 months. Usage aggregates across instance types. Accounts created on/after July 15, 2025 instead get the credit-based Free/Paid plans ($100 + up to $100) in place of this 12-month free database.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS accounts created before July 15, 2025 (or existing legacy accounts still within their 12-month window). Not available in AWS GovCloud (US) or China (Beijing) regions. No extra eligibility gate beyond a new account.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a new (pre-cutover) AWS account; 2) launch an eligible single-AZ RDS instance in an eligible engine; 3) stay within 750 instance-hours + 20 GB storage + 20 GB automated backup per month for 12 months. Nothing to activate — it applies automatically.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation. After that, standard on-demand pricing. For post-cutover accounts, the equivalent benefit is the $100-200 account credit instead.
  - *Gotchas:* Single-AZ only (Multi-AZ is paid); SQL Server Express only for that engine; cross-AZ VPC traffic (EC2 in a different AZ than RDS) costs $0.01/GB in/out; backup storage beyond 20 GB is paid. Manual snapshots beyond the automated-backup allowance are charged. Don't rely on it: new accounts after July 15, 2025 cannot get this tier.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/rds/pricing/(FreeDatabasesection)
- **Amazon DynamoDB free tier (ongoing per-region allowances + account credits)** (high) — Current per-Region, per-payer-account monthly free allowances: 25 WCUs + 25 RCUs, 25 GB of data storage, 25 rWCUs for global tables deployed across two Regions, 2.5 million DynamoDB Streams read requests, and 1 GB data transfer out (15 GB during your first 12 months, aggregated across AWS services). The page notes it is 'enough for about 200M requests/month' at small item sizes. New accounts can also get up to $200 in AWS Free Tier credits to 'experience the full set of DynamoDB features for up to 6 months'.
  - *Eligibility:* Per-Region monthly allowances apply to all accounts (Standard table class, provisioned capacity model). The $200/6-month credits require a new AWS account (new-customer Free/Paid plan enrollment).
  - *How to claim:* Allowances are automatic per Region — no activation. The $200 credits come automatically when creating a new AWS account on the Free plan; apply at signup.
  - *Expiry:* Allowance is recurring monthly (no published sunset; legacy 12-month wording no longer appears on the page — it now reads as ongoing per-Region benefits tied to the new free-tier structure). Credits: 6-month window on the Free plan.
  - *Gotchas:* Applies to provisioned capacity + Standard table class only; on-demand capacity workloads pay standard rates. Data transfer-out beyond 1 GB/month (15 GB in year one) is paid. Free allowances are per Region, so multi-Region deployments multiply costs.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/pricing/
- **Amazon Redshift free trial (Serverless $300 credit / 90 days; provisioned 2-month trial)** (high) — Serverless: new users are eligible for a $300 credit with a 90-day expiration toward compute and storage use. In Regions where Serverless is not available, customers can start a free trial for provisioned clusters that expires after two months or when usage exceeds 750 hours per month, after which you must shut down or pay the standard on-demand rate.
  - *Eligibility:* New Redshift users/accounts. No funding or .edu requirements; the trial attaches to a new-customer account.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Activate Redshift Serverless in the console — the $300 credit applies automatically; or in a non-Serverless region, launch a provisioned cluster free for the trial window. No separate application.
  - *Expiry:* Serverless credit: 90 days from issuance. Provisioned trial: 2 months or 750 cluster-hours/month, whichever comes first, then standard on-demand pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* The legacy RA3/RA2 developer free tier (1-node, 12 months) and 30-day standard tier are no longer shown on the current pricing page — the trial structure changed in the 2025-2026 credit-model restructure. The page does not publish node size/warehouse size for the provisioned trial; verify in console. After trial, standard on-demand rates apply immediately.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/redshift/pricing/
- **AWS Glue Data Catalog free tier (recurring, data-platform front)** (high) — First million metadata objects stored are free and the first million catalog requests per month are free, every month (ongoing allowance, not a 12-month grant). Lake Formation permissions are also 'provided at no charge', and Lake Formation example billing shows $0 catalog/permissions usage inside the free tier. Pricing varies by Region per the regional product-services table.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS accounts; per-Region recurring allowance; no funding or .edu requirements.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic — no activation. Using the Glue Data Catalog / Lake Formation permissions under the allowances simply bills $0; the first million objects + first million requests per month are deducted first.
  - *Expiry:* No published sunset; allowance recurs monthly. (The legacy '1 million source records processed per month' Glue ETL free allowance no longer appears on the current Glue pricing page — ETL/crawler/development-endpoint work is billed per DPU-hour, so that recurring ETL freebie is effectively gone from the published pricing.)
  - *Gotchas:* Compute (ETL jobs, crawlers, DataBrew, development endpoints) is paid per DPU-hour — 'catalog free' does not make pipeline runs free. Data transfer and storage (S3) remain separate paid items. Verify Region-specific availability.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/glue/pricing/andhttps://aws.amazon.com/lake-formation/pricing/
- **AWS Educate (free education license/program for students, 13+)** (high) — Completely free self-paced cloud training and hands-on labs (S3, EC2, VPC, RDS, compute, storage, networking, databases, cloud operations), plus shareable digital badges, a job board (18+), and the AWS Emerging Talent community. Coverage: learners as young as 13; open to any individual regardless of education stage, technical experience, or career — not restricted to students/educators or accredited institutions.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone 13+ can register with just an email address — no .edu email, no institutional enrollment required. Job board access requires 18+. No credit card needed to register (the 'no credit card' label refers to registration, not cloud credits — the program bundles no AWS usage credits).
  - *How to claim:* Register at awseducate.com (learner registration link carries promo code 'EducateLP'), sign in, then take courses and complete labs at no cost.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry for the program; badges are permanent. Course catalogs evolve over time; labs run in AWS-provisioned lab environments, not your account.
  - *Gotchas:* No cloud usage credits, no exam vouchers, and no certification discounts are published on the page — do not expect a free Cloud Practitioner exam. Labs are scoped sandbox environments, not your own AWS account. Employer/recruiter features are a separate registration.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Skill Builder free digital courses** (medium) — 600+ free digital courses (the startups AI hub cites 900+ free courses in 11 languages) curated by AWS, self-paced and at no cost; paid Skill Builder subscriptions unlock additional lab experiences on top of the free catalog. Free training events (Twitch/virtual) also advertised at no cost.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone with a free AWS Builder ID / Skill Builder account — students, educators, or self-learners; no .edu requirement, no funding requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account at skillbuilder.aws (or via the Builder ID), browse the free digital catalog, take courses; events via /training/events/.
  - *Expiry:* Course access has no published expiry; free vs paid boundary changes with subscription offerings.
  - *Gotchas:* Certifications themselves are NOT free (exam fees still apply; the AI hub's 'Certified AI Business Strategist' cert is sold separately). Paid lab tiers cost extra. Free courses refresh/retire, so the exact count (600 vs 900) varies by page and date.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/(and/startups/ai-for-startups/citing900+)/https://skillbuilder.aws/
- **AWS Impact Bootcamps (with Techstars): up to $5,000 in AWS Activate credits** (high) — Two-day intensive bootcamp for underrepresented startup founders, run with Techstars in six cities: Atlanta (Sep 8-9, 2025), New York (Sep 15-16, 2025), Mexico City (Oct 1-2, 2025), London (Oct 16-17, 2025), Tokyo (Mar 26-27, 2026; apply by Feb 27, 2026) and Berlin (Apr 16-17, 2026; apply by Mar 13, 2026). Selected startups get access to up to $5,000 in AWS Activate credits to build, train, and launch products, plus consideration for Techstars Founder Catalyst / accelerator and AWS startup programs. Up to 50 founders per city, ~300 founders annually. Workshops: fundraising mindset, customer discovery, go-to-market, AI product optimization, SAFE/convertible notes, pitch practice. Launched Jul 30, 2025, building on AWS's 2022 $30M commitment to Black, Women, Latino, and LGBTQIA+ founders.
  - *Eligibility:* Self-funded, angel-funded, or early-stage startups (press release: raised up to Series A) with a goal to scale or prepare to raise funds; at least one founding/exec team member must identify with a historically underrepresented community (Black, women, Latino/Latinx, Indigenous, LGBTQIA+, people with disabilities) OR be engaged in work supporting such communities. In-person attendance at the two-day event. No .edu email required; no credit card required for participation; global (any region, in-person at the city stop).
  - *How to claim:* All 2025-2026 cohort application windows are closed (Tokyo closed Feb 27, 2026; Berlin closed Mar 13, 2026); as of 2026-10-22 no open application window is posted on the official page, so the $5,000 offer is not currently claimable - watch for new city cohorts. To claim the $5K: apply to a bootcamp before its deadline, attend, then apply for the AWS Activate credit through the AWS Startups portal (startups.aws.com); review takes ~5-10 business days and the credit is issued to a good-standing AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* Not a rolling offer: cohort-based. 2025 (US/EU/LatAm) and 2026 (Tokyo, Berlin) cohorts have concluded; future cohorts to be announced (the learn page said more locations would be announced in 2026). Credits, once issued, follow AWS Activate terms (page did not state a burn period today).
  - *Gotchas:* Press release states $5,000 is given; the landing page says 'up to $5K', so awards are at AWS discretion. The bootcamp page currently returns HTTP 500 at the server level even though its content payload is intact. 'AWS Activate Credits' are promotional credits subject to AWS's standard rules: startup <10 years old, funding stage <= Series A, most recent funding round within 12 months of application, application email domain must match the AWS account domain, free email providers rejected, website must be live, and AWS may decline applications in its sole discretion; availability can be limited by country/region.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-impact-bootcamps(servingHTTP500;fullcontentrecoveredfromitsembeddedNext.jspayload;cross-checkedwiththeJul30,2025pressreleasehttps://press.aboutamazon.com/aws/2025/7/aws-and-techstars-launch-impact-bootcamps-to-support-underrepresented-founders-globallyandhttps://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/what-to-expect-from-the-aws-impact-bootcamps/)
- **AWS Founder Retreat (bootcamp capstone): up to $50,000 unrestricted grant + up to $15,000 extra AWS credits** (high) — Invitation-only weekend capstone for 'best-in-class' bootcamp participants: in-person investor meetings and a chance to win up to $50,000 in unrestricted grant funding plus an extra $15,000 in AWS promotional credits, per cohort (up to 4 winners per press-release math; actual 2025 event: 12 finalists from the Atlanta, NYC, London and Mexico City bootcamps attended a retreat at Ritz-Carlton Half Moon Bay, culminating in a pitch competition for US$100,000 in grants with three recipients - Pilou, FoodStream Network, SyncPop). Includes one-to-one mentoring with AWS leaders and investors.
  - *Eligibility:* Invite-only; top-performing AWS Impact Bootcamp participants (hand-picked from hundreds of global participants). Underlying bootcamp eligibility applies (underrepresented early-stage founders, self-funded/angel/early-stage, goal to scale or raise).
  - *How to claim:* Cannot apply directly. Path: apply to an AWS Impact Bootcamp -> be selected as a top participant -> receive the Founder Retreat invitation. Currently no open path (all bootcamp windows closed as of 2026-10-22).
  - *Expiry:* Event-based; one per program cycle. The Tokyo/Berlin 2026 cohorts will presumably feed a 2026/2027 retreat.
  - *Gotchas:* The 'up to $50,000' grant is per capstone event, not per winner; the 2025 cohort split a US$100,000 pool among 3 winners (i.e., ~$33K each, not $50K). Winners are also 'considered' for Techstars programs, not guaranteed. In-person, US-based venue in 2025.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/learn/fresh-ideas-and-new-connections-at-the-aws-founders-retreatandhttps://press.aboutamazon.com/aws/2025/7/aws-and-techstars-launch-impact-bootcamps-to-support-underrepresented-founders-globally
- **AWS Activate Program (flagship startup credit program; the VC/accelerator-funded front): up to $100,000 - $200,000 in AWS credits** (high) — AWS Startups portal (read live 2026-10-22): the join page advertises 'Apply for up to $200,000 in Activate Credits' and the credits widget says 'Eligible startups can receive up to $100,000 in AWS Activate Credits. These credits can be used for AWS services and support, including infrastructure and emerging technologies.' Credits are claimable in one step when affiliated with an AWS Activate Provider (VC, accelerator, angel group, or other partner); the portal notes 'Home to over 350,000 startups globally.' Legacy structure (2023-2025): standard AWS Activate up to $1,000 in credits; Accelerate (VC/accelerator-backed) up to $100,000 over 12+ months. The $200K figure on the live join page indicates a 2026 headline increase; the $1,000 standard-tier copy no longer appears on the pages read today. Benefits besides credits: partner offers/exclusive deals, global events, training, technical support.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup founded within the last 10 years; funding stage at or below Series A (Series B+ explicitly rejected); most recent funding round within 12 months of application; application email domain must match the AWS account email domain (free email providers rejected); company website must be live and functional (no under-construction/maintenance/404/redirect); account in good standing; reseller-affiliated accounts need reseller confirmation; not available in every country ('AWS Activate is not available in your country' rejection exists); if you already received equal-or-greater Activate credits, a new award is the current offer minus the previous amount. No .edu requirement; no credit card to receive the credit itself (a payment method is normal for the AWS account).
  - *How to claim:* Apply at the AWS Startups portal (startups.aws.com) - if you have a VC/accelerator/angel partner, apply 'in one step' through provider affiliation (provider must confirm within 30 days or the application can stall). Processing ~5-10 business days. Alternatively, the $5,000 bootcamp credits are claimed through the same Activate application flow. As of 2026-10-22 the program is live and open to qualifying startups.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' is literal: actual awards are tiered and smaller than the headline (widget says $100K max while the join page says $200K - the $200K ceiling is not broken down on the pages read today). Promotional credits are not redeemable for data egress/inter-region transfer beyond free allowances and expire unused. Duplicate applications rejected; email-domain mismatch is the most common rejection reason; provider non-confirmation after 30 days stalls the application. Regional availability restrictions apply.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/aws-startup-credits-promotionandhttps://aws.amazon.com/startups/activate/(portalappshellreadlive2026-10-22)
- **AWS Free Tier (revamped): Free plan - up to $200 in credits, 90+ services for up to 6 months; Paid plan - pay-as-you-go plus up to $200 in credits** (high) — Current AWS Free Tier page (read live 2026-10-22) describes a restructured model: Free plan - 'Start building on AWS with up to $200 in credits. Get access to over 90 services for up to 6 months as you build and experiment with no charges and no surprise overages.' Paid plan - 'Access the full range of AWS services with pay-as-you-go pricing. New customers on the Paid plan are also eligible for up to $200 in credits.' Short-term trials - 'Try selected Paid plan services through limited free trials. Your trial begins when you activate the service, and any eligible credits automatically apply to usage beyond the trial limits.' Always Free - '30+ AWS services are always free within monthly usage limits on both the Free and Paid plans; if you go beyond these limits or access paid features, credits are automatically applied to cover the costs.'
  - *Eligibility:* All new AWS customers (including students; no .edu email or credit card documented on the page). Free plan requires simply opening a new account; a payment method is normally required for the account but the Free plan's usage up to limits is covered by the $200 credits with 'no surprise overages'.
  - *How to claim:* No application: open a new AWS account at aws.amazon.com; Free plan benefits and trial offers are automatic, and the $200 credits apply to usage within the 6-month window / beyond trial limits.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan: up to 6 months of covered access; Always Free items: indefinite within monthly limits; $200 credits: burn off over the period, unused value does not roll over as cash.
  - *Gotchas:* This is NOT the old '12-month free tier' - see sunset_notes. The $200 is a usage-credit ceiling, not a cash grant; after 6 months or when credits run out, Paid-plan usage is billed. Data transfer, inter-region egress, and paid features beyond always-free monthly limits can incur charges ('credits are automatically applied to cover the costs' - credits, not a no-cost promise, cover overage). Trial periods are per-service and only start on activation.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Educate: free education license for students and educators (no credit card)** (high) — Official AWS Educate page (read live 2026-10-22): 'AWS Educate provides students and educators with no-cost access to cloud computing skills training developed by AWS experts, plus career pathways for students.' Includes self-paced free learning ('build your cloud skills... completely for free'), hands-on labs - e.g., build a static website on S3, EC2 instance, VPC networking, RDS database, Cloud operations course with a cost-estimation lab - and a job board / employer program ('AWS Educate for employers'). Page states explicitly: 'Sign in to AWS Educate: No credit card needed.' No dollar credit pool; the value is free training + sandboxed lab access rather than account credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Students, K-12 and higher-ed educators, and employers (recruiters). No .edu email is documented as required on the current page; no credit card needed. Institution-based accounts have historically been free 12-month licenses renewable annually - the current page does not state a duration, so treat renewal terms as unverified.
  - *How to claim:* Register at https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/ (Register for AWS Educate), sign in to the AWS Educate portal, then complete courses/labs for free. No payment or card at any step per the page's 'No credit card needed' statement.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a credits offer - no cloud spend allowance for personal AWS accounts beyond the generic Free Tier. Distinct from AWS Academy, which is an institutional/licensed program (usually paid by the institution) - AWS Academy's own site was unreachable during this research, so no claim is made about it. Labs are time-boxed sandbox environments.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Skill Builder: 600+ free courses and certification exam prep** (medium) — AWS Skill Builder (skillbuilder.aws, read live 2026-10-22): 'an online learning center where you can learn from AWS experts... With access to 600+ free courses, certification exam prep, and training that allows you to build practical skills.' Free for self-directed learners, including students; no enrollment gate, no credit card. This is the successor to much of AWS's formerly free training content.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, for free; no .edu requirement, no card. Free content is the baseline; AWS has added paid professional subscription tiers in recent years (not verified in today's read - treat as a gotcha, not a fact).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at skillbuilder.aws and take courses/digital badges at no cost.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not assume everything is free if you later see 'Professional' tier marketing; today's read confirms the free 600+ course base only.
  - *Source:* https://skillbuilder.aws/
- **AWS Free Plan (new accounts, credit-based, in effect since 2025-07-15)** (high) — New accounts get $100 in credits immediately at sign-up, plus up to $100 more by completing 'Explore AWS' service-exploration activities in the Console Home widget (filtered to 'Earn AWS credits') = up to $200 total usable over 6 months. Covers 90+ services for up to 6 months, including Amazon RDS, Aurora Serverless, and DynamoDB. 'No charges and no surprise bills on the Free plan' unless you convert to a Paid plan or activate paid-only services. Account auto-closes at the earlier of 6 months after account opening or when credits run out; on expiration AWS suspends the account and retains your data 90 days (upgradable to Paid within that window), then account/content is permanently erased. Free plan is NOT eligible for promotional credits (e.g. AWS Activate).
  - *Eligibility:* One free grant per identity: 'ineligible for the Free plan or Free Tier credits if you have an existing AWS account or have previously had one'. Payment method NOT required for most new signups (AWS may still request one for identity verification). Credits are USD, apply across participating global regions; NOT available in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for a new AWS account choosing the Free plan at aws.amazon.com/free -> $100 credit applied automatically. Complete service-exploration activities in the 'Explore AWS' widget on Console Home (filter: 'Earn AWS credits') within 6 months of account creation to earn up to $100 more.
  - *Expiry:* Initial $100 credit expires when the 6-month Free plan window ends (credit validity stated as 'up to 12 months' on service pages; activity-earned credits expire 12 months after account creation per FAQ). Account auto-closes at 6 months or when credits are exhausted, whichever is first; 90-day data retention after closure; no renewal, no second grant on a new identity-linked account.
  - *Gotchas:* Auto-close at 6 months means long-lived free work must be migrated/billed; data permanently deleted 90 days after closure. Free plan excludes promotional credits (Activate) and some paid-only services; upgrading to Paid to unlock those is irreversible (cannot revert to Free plan). Credits apply across regions but China/GovCloud excluded. Upgrading converts to pay-as-you-go once credits burn.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Paid Plan new-customer credits** (medium) — New customers choosing the Paid plan get 'up to $200 USD free credits' plus full access to all AWS services (including paid-only services like Bedrock AgentCore) and eligibility for promotional credits. Selected services can be trialed via 'limited free trials'; the trial clock starts at activation and remaining credits 'automatically apply to usage beyond the trial limits'. Pay-as-you-go billing applies beyond credit exhaustion.
  - *Eligibility:* Same one-free-grant-per-identity rule; a billing/payment method is in practice expected for a Paid plan (Free plan needed none). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up choosing the Paid plan, or convert an existing Free-plan account to Paid (irreversible).
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable 'for up to 12 months' on the Paid plan per service pages (e.g. Aurora pricing page), vs the 6-month Free-plan window; trial clocks start at service activation.
  - *Gotchas:* Pay-as-you-go charges begin the moment credits run out; Free-to-Paid conversion is one-way; trials are time-boxed per service.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aurora/pricing/
- **Legacy 12-month Free Tier for RDS (accounts created before 2025-07-15)** (high) — Grandfathered pre-2025-07-15 accounts keep the service-level 12-month free tier: up to 750 hours/month of single-AZ instance databases (aggregated across instance types), engines limited to MySQL, MariaDB, PostgreSQL, or SQL Server (Express Edition only), 20 GB/month of gp2 general-purpose storage, and 20 GB/month of automated backup storage for 1 year.
  - *Eligibility:* AWS accounts created before July 15, 2025 only; not the credit-based model.
  - *How to claim:* N/A - grandfathered automatically; nothing to claim.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation, then billed.
  - *Gotchas:* No free-tier credits available in AWS GovCloud (US) regions or the China (Beijing) region; SQL Server only in Express edition; 750 hours aggregate means running multiple RDS instances splits the hours.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/rds/pricing/
- **DynamoDB always-free allowances plus credit boost** (high) — Per the DynamoDB pricing page today: 25 WCUs and 25 RCUs of provisioned capacity (per Region, per payer account, monthly), 25 GB of data storage, 25 rWCUs for global tables deployed across two regions, 2.5 million stream read requests from DynamoDB Streams, 1 GB data transfer out per month (15 GB for the first 12 months, aggregated across AWS services); plus 'up to $200 USD in credits' to use the full DynamoDB feature set for up to 6 months on the Free plan.
  - *Eligibility:* Always-free allowances apply while you are an AWS customer (Free or Paid plan); the $200 credit boost is for new accounts under the credit-based model. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Always-free allowances apply automatically; the credit boost comes with a new Free/Paid plan account (see the main Free plan finding).
  - *Expiry:* Monthly allowances recur; the 12-month egress allowance lapses at month 12; the $200 credit is within the 6-month Free plan window (or 12-month credit validity on Paid plan).
  - *Gotchas:* The page presents allowances without an explicit legacy/new split - the '15 GB first 12 months' egress language is legacy-style; request-based allowances (25 WCUs/25 RCUs) are the current always-free shape. Old (pre-2025) free tier was expressed as 25 GB + 2.5M write/5M read requests, so verify the exact current RCU/WCU mix against AWS Billing if cost-critical.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/pricing/
- **Aurora free availability (Free plan) + credit validity** (high) — The Free plan 'provides Aurora PostgreSQL serverless instances with up to 4 ACUs and 1 GiB of storage per cluster' (paid plans scale to 256 ACUs and 256 TiB). New customers receive '$100 in AWS credits at sign-up' usable 'for up to 12 months, in both Free and Paid plans' plus 'an additional $100 in credits, for a total of $200'.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS accounts (Free or Paid plan); no .edu requirement; no payment method required for most Free-plan signups.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic with new-account signup ($100); extra $100 via service-exploration activities.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable up to 12 months per the Aurora page; the Free plan account itself auto-closes at 6 months - so the 12-month credit validity practically matters only if you convert to the Paid plan before closure.
  - *Gotchas:* Only Aurora PostgreSQL Serverless is named as a free-plan offering (not MySQL, not provisioned instances, no I/O/storage free GB figures on today's page - the legacy 120 hours db.t3.medium/10 GB storage/100 GB backup tier is not listed on the current Aurora page).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aurora/pricing/
- **AWS Activate Founders (self-funded / bootstrapped startups)** (high) — 'Up to $5,000 USD in Activate Credits', with 'Start with $1,000 USD in Activate Credits' - select participants may qualify for the full $5,000. Credits offset infrastructure, data services, and AI/ML models, and are redeemable on third-party models on Amazon Bedrock. Includes mentorship, AWS Startup Advisor, and partner-network co-build/co-market access.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series B stage; founded in the last 10 years; must have an AWS account on a Paid-plan tier; must be 'new to Activate Credits or requesting more credits than previously received'; apply with an AWS Builder ID using a professional email - no .edu requirement and no country restrictions stated on the page. USD credits subject to AWS Promotional Credit T&C.
  - *How to claim:* Create an AWS Builder ID -> choose self-funded (Founders) tier at aws.amazon.com/startups/join?destination=/credits/apply -> provide product, funding stage, and company details -> link/create a Paid-plan AWS account and submit; response in 5-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page - governed by the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (typically ~12-month burn-down, unverified today).
  - *Gotchas:* Amount is 'up to' $5,000 - default grant is $1,000; must run a Paid plan (the Free plan excludes promotional credits entirely); no .edu, but a professional email is required.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Activate Portfolio (VC / accelerator / angel-backed startups)** (high) — 'Up to $200,000 USD in Activate Credits' for Pre-Series B startups, gated by an 'Organization ID (Org ID)' issued by an Activate Provider (accelerator, angel investor, or VC firm; named examples: Andreessen Horowitz, Brex, Carta, Greylock Partners, Sequoia, Y Combinator). The /startups/ page headlines 'Get up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits and expert support'.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series B; founded in the last 10 years; AWS account on a Paid plan; must be new to Activate credits or requesting more than previously received; requires provider-issued Org ID (no .edu requirement; no country restrictions stated on the page).
  - *How to claim:* Get an Org ID from your VC/accelerator/angel provider, then apply via the AWS Builder ID credits-application flow choosing the Portfolio tier; 5-10 business day review.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page - AWS Promotional Credit T&C apply.
  - *Gotchas:* The ceiling is 'up to' $200,000 (variable award); provider gating means bootstrapped founders cannot use this tier; credits are USD and promo-T&C bound.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Credits for AI Startups (invite-only add-on track)** (medium) — '$200,000+ in AWS Credits' described as 'Invite only for startups ready to scale post Activate Portfolio', accessed through an Account Manager; separate 'AI startups ready to grow may be eligible for additional credits beyond Activate' noted on the main startups page.
  - *Eligibility:* Invitation-only; aimed at AI startups that have already gone through Activate Portfolio; via an AWS Account Manager.
  - *How to claim:* No self-serve application - request via your AWS Account Manager after Activate Portfolio participation.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page - promo T&C apply.
  - *Gotchas:* Invite-only means no guaranteed path; the '+' after $200,000 is not quantified on the page.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Kiro Startup Credits (separate, smaller credit track)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — A separate, smaller AWS credit program 'available to eligible startups without current Activate Credits' from the Kiro agentic IDE program (launched 2025); also described as 'Kiro credits designed for startups'. Exact amounts, duration and eligibility criteria were not retrievable today (kiro.aws and /aws.amazon.com/kiro/ did not resolve from this environment).
  - *Eligibility:* Startups without current Activate credits, per the credits page; further criteria unknown.
  - *How to claim:* Details not retrievable in this environment; check Kiro's official site/credits page directly.
  - *Expiry:* Unknown.
  - *Gotchas:* Unquantified in official AWS pages fetched today; treat any third-party numbers as unverified.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Educate (free student/individual training, not a credit program)** (high) — AWS Educate (successor of Cloud Academy) is active and 'completely for free': free self-paced online training and hands-on labs covering Storage (S3), Compute (EC2), Networking (VPC), Databases (RDS) and Cloud Operations, with shareable digital badges. Open to any individual, 'no credit card needed'; learners as young as 13 can register with just an email address; 18+ also get job-board access; 'AWS Educate for employers' gives recruiters access to the trained talent pool. The page (copyright 2026) contains no dollar-credit amounts and no mention of granting personal AWS Free Tier accounts.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual with an email - no .edu requirement, no institutional enrollment required; age floor 13.
  - *How to claim:* Register at aws.amazon.com/educate/ with any email address; complete self-paced courses and labs; earn badges.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; free while the program is live.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a money-credit offer - value is training/badges, not AWS usage credits; if you need actual AWS usage (RDS/Aurora/DynamoDB) you still sign up for the regular AWS account (and, if brand-new, get the Free plan credits under the general program, which does not itself require .edu).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/educate/
- **AWS Free Plan (new-customer Free Tier): up to $200 credits over 6 months** (high) — New AWS customers get $100 in Free Tier credits immediately on account creation, plus up to $100 more earned by completing 5 console activities in the 'Explore AWS' widget ($20 each: launch & terminate an EC2 instance; configure an RDS database; build a Lambda function with a function URL; submit a prompt in the Bedrock text playground; create an AWS Budgets alert). The Free plan covers 90+ services for up to 6 months with 'no charges and no surprise bills'; 30+ services remain always-free within monthly limits on both plans. Credits are auto-applied to eligible usage, including always-free overages. Free-plan accounts cannot use 'certain AWS services that would rapidly consume the entire credit amount, or hardware purchases'; Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is paid-plan exclusive. Announcement: AWS 'what's new' July 16, 2025; AWS News Blog July 15, 2025. Eligible EC2 free-tier instance types for accounts on/after Jul 15, 2025: t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large (t2.micro no longer eligible). RDS free on db.t3.micro / db.t4g.micro (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, SQL Server Express); Aurora PostgreSQL serverless up to 4 ACU + 1 GiB storage; Aurora DSQL always free at 100k DPU + 1 GiB storage/month.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (any existing or previously-held AWS account disqualifies you). All customers can sign up online; no .edu or startup requirement. Excluded regions: China (ZHY) and China (BJS) regions, and AWS GovCloud (US) regions (with a carve-out for certain services such as Lambda in GovCloud). Credit card NOT required to sign up for most new customers (AWS may request a payment method to verify identity in some cases).
  - *How to claim:* Go to https://aws.amazon.com/free/ -> 'Create a free account' -> choose the Free account plan during signup. $100 credit posts automatically (may take up to ~10 min to appear on the Credits page). Complete the 5 Explore AWS activities inside 6 months to earn the remaining $100. Monitor balance via the Cost & Usage widget on console home.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan expires at the earlier of (a) 6 months from account creation or (b) credits exhausted. Unused credits stay usable up to 12 months from account creation if you upgrade to a Paid plan within the window. After the Free plan period, you have 90 days to upgrade; then AWS permanently closes the account and deletes content/resources. Earned-credit activities must be completed within 6 months of creation; eligibility to earn ends permanently if you join AWS Organizations or set up a Control Tower landing zone.
  - *Gotchas:* Free plan is NOT eligible for promotional/incentive credits (e.g., AWS Activate) - those require a Paid plan; enrolling a Free-plan account in AWS Organizations auto-upgrades it to Paid. Usage is aggregated across all regions; no monthly free-usage rollover. AWS may reclaim resources unused for 3 months after 30 days' notice. Free plan is for evaluation only - 'should not be used for processing sensitive data'. Data transfer/egress beyond always-free allowances draws on credits, then pay-as-you-go once credits run out. New sign-up experience launched October 2026 (credit expiry view moved to Settings Portal; older accounts use the Credits page in the Billing console).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/(plushttps://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/,https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2025/07/aws-free-tier-credits-month-free-plan/,https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/aws-free-tier-update-new-customers-can-get-started-and-explore-aws-with-up-to-200-in-credits/)
- **AWS Always Free offers: 30+ services with monthly free usage limits (all customers)** (high) — On both Free and Paid plans, '30+ AWS services are always free within monthly usage limits' (ongoing monthly allowances, e.g., Lambda free requests/month, Aurora DSQL 100k DPU + 1 GiB storage/month). If usage exceeds the monthly allowance, remaining Free Tier credits auto-apply to cover eligible costs (Free plan); on Paid plans you pay standard rates beyond the credit balance. Per-service numeric limits are listed on the Free Tier page filters ('Explore our offers' by offer type + product category) and on individual product pricing pages; the main page does not publish the full numeric list.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers, new and existing, on either plan. No credit card needed to keep an account. No regional restrictions stated beyond the general Free Tier exclusions.
