  - *How to claim:* Use the End User Messaging mobile push API; the first 1M pushes/month are $0 automatically — nothing to redeem. Migration users: keep API usage (APNs/FCM/WNS) intact past 2026-10-30 since campaigns/journeys/console state in Pinpoint does NOT survive the sunset.
  - *Expiry:* Standing monthly allowance, no stated expiry. Hard date to act on: 2026-10-30 Pinpoint support ends — move off Pinpoint console/journeys before that.
  - *Gotchas:* Post-1M pushes cost $0.000001 each. Push is one channel of EUM; SMS/voice/email in the same app are billed separately (email via SES; no free SMS). Inbound/egress data and any EC2/S3 behind your app add cost.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/andhttps://aws.amazon.com/pinpoint/(deprecationnotice)
- **SMS (End User Messaging): no free SMS quota published — pay-per-message with carrier surcharges** (medium) — The End User Messaging pricing page lists NO free SMS/MMS/voice message allowance (unlike push's 1M free). SMS: 'price varies by destination country and, in some cases, carrier within a country' (e.g., Canada toll-free: $0.00581 AWS + $0.00705 carrier). MMS (US/Canada outbound): $0.02 base + $0.01 carrier. Voice (outbound): US $0.013/min, requires a dedicated voice-enabled long code. OTP: $0.045 per successful verification plus the SMS price. Phone Number Validate: $0.006/request. Add-ons: SMS Protect Monitor/Filter $0.01/message; Notify managed feature $0.030-$0.045/message volume-tiered plus channel fee; phone number leasing $1-$995/mo; short codes US setup $650 + $995/mo. The legacy '100 free SMS/month in US regions' SNS free tier is no longer published anywhere on the current End User Messaging/SNS pricing pages — likely absorbed into the $200 Free Account credit model.
  - *Eligibility:* Any End User Messaging customer. No free tier to claim; new accounts' up-to-$200 Free Account credits can offset early SMS spend. No card needed during the 6-month Free plan.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim for SMS specifically — usage is metered; if you need zero-cost SMS, rely on the up-to-$200 new-account credits or an AWS Activate credit grant.
  - *Expiry:* No free tier, so no expiry question; pricing is standing. Legacy 100-msg US free tier: no longer listed as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Gotchas:* High gotcha surface: carrier fees are passed through on top of AWS fees (so 'real' per-SMS cost is higher than the AWS number); promotional SMS in many markets is heavily regulated; OTP and Phone Number Validate are separate paid features; scaling past 'Notify' requires dedicated phone numbers + brand verification (US 10DLC etc.), which cost money.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/
- **Chat APIs (WhatsApp & RCS via End User Messaging): no free tier — metered with Meta/carrier surcharges** (high) — WhatsApp: AWS fee $0.005 per outbound message globally (except India Authentication/Utility at $0.002), $0.001 per inbound globally; WhatsApp voice calling $0.005/min outbound (India $0.003), $0.001/min inbound; Meta's separate message fees are added on top and vary by country/message type. RCS (US): Rich RCS outbound $0.007/segment AWS + $0.00494/segment carrier; Rich Media RCS $0.020/message AWS + $0.00949 carrier. 'Businesses are only charged when a message is successfully delivered.' No free messages for WhatsApp or RCS.
  - *Eligibility:* Any End User Messaging customer; no free tier to claim. No card requirement during the new-account Free plan window.
  - *How to claim:* Use EUM WhatsApp/RCS APIs; expect cost from message #1 unless you hold AWS credits (Free Account $200 or Activate grants).
  - *Expiry:* Standing metered pricing; no trial stated.
  - *Gotchas:* Double billing: AWS fee + Meta fee (WhatsApp) or AWS fee + carrier fee (RCS); 'only charged on successful delivery' cuts both ways for delivery-failure accounting.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/
- **AWS Activate Founders tier: $1,000 in AWS credits (up to $5,000 over time) for early-stage, self-funded startups** (high) — Founders tier (current as of the June 2026 applying guide): $1,000 in AWS Activate Credits; 'select startups may qualify for up to $5,000 over time'. Eligibility: new to AWS Activate Credits, self-funded, fully functioning company website, founded in the past 10 years. Credits expire within 1-2 years depending on package and are applied monthly to new eligible charges only (never retroactive). Processing 7-10 business days. Credits are redeemable across 200+ AWS services, including third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta, etc.). No credit-card requirement mentioned for the program itself. Re-applying is allowed for a larger tier; you receive the difference up to a lifetime cap; submitted applications can't be edited (cancel + unlink + resubmit).
  - *Eligibility:* Startup founders (individuals applying on behalf of a company): self-funded (no institutional funding), company < 10 years old, working company website required, first-time Activate recipient, business email matching the startup domain. Applies to startups of any funding stage at or before Pre-Series B.
  - *How to claim:* Create/sign in to an AWS Builder ID (personal email) → complete one-time Activate profile (business email) → choose Founders tier → enter company + funding details → link your primary AWS account (root/admin) → verify in console → submit. Credits land on the linked AWS account and automatically offset SES/SMS/push spend.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 1-2 years from grant depending on package; no stated renewal — you can re-apply when you qualify for a bigger tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Self-funded only — a small funding round or accelerator affiliation pushes you to the Portfolio tier instead. Credits cover new usage only; pre-existing debt on the account is not credited. $5K 'over time' is discretionary/select.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/applying-for-aws-activate-credits-a-step-by-step-guide/(plushttps://aws.amazon.com/startups/)
- **AWS Activate Portfolio tier: up to $200,000 in AWS credits for provider-backed startups (pre-Series B)** (high) — Portfolio tier: 'up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits'. Eligibility: associated with an AWS Activate Provider (accelerator/VC/ecosystem org, 'thousands worldwide') and hold their Organizational ID; Pre-Series B (most recent funding round within last 12 months, if funded); founded in the past 10 years; working company website; no prior AWS Activate Credits of equal or greater value. Beyond $200K, 'AI startups ready to grow may be eligible for additional credits' via an Account Manager. Credits redeemable on 200+ services incl. Bedrock third-party models; expire within 1-2 years; applied monthly to new eligible charges. Program stats: 350,000+ startups, $8B+ in promotional credits since 2013. Note: an older marketing page (startups.aws.com LP) still advertises 'up to $100,000' — the June 2026 guide and main page confirm $200,000 as the current ceiling; also a new 'Kiro credits' offer for startups exists (kiro.dev/startups).
  - *Eligibility:* Startup companies affiliated with an Activate Provider (Org ID), pre-Series B funding cap (most recent round <= 12 months old), < 10 years old, working website. Not for individuals or non-startup businesses; no .edu involved (student startups go through their university's provider agreement — see Educate finding).
  - *How to claim:* Obtain your provider's Org ID → AWS Builder ID + Activate profile (business email) → choose Portfolio tier → submit funding/company details → link primary AWS account → wait 7-10 business days; credits apply monthly to new eligible usage.
  - *Expiry:* Grant-to-expiry 1-2 years per package; re-application gives the difference vs previously held credits up to the lifetime cap.
  - *Gotchas:* The headline number moved from $100K to $200K (some pages stale); 'most recent round within last 12 months' is a hard timing rule; credits are new-charge-only; wrong/funding details = denial (inaccurate info results in denial).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/applying-for-aws-activate-credits-a-step-by-step-guide/

Some university startup programs (e.g., AWS Activate partnerships at universities like Syracuse's Blackstone LaunchPad) extend student-startup eligibility to up to $5,000 in credits valid ~2 years plus 1 year of AWS Business Support.   - *Expiry:* Educate credits typically annual per-enrollment; university startup grants ~2 years. Verify current numbers with the school's page.

Student Builder Groups (student-led campus communities) also exist.
  - *Eligibility:* Students (or staff) of an accredited higher-education institution, verified by SheerID (most checks minutes; up to 24-48h with extra docs). No startup/funding rules.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in with an AWS Builder ID at builder.aws.com → complete SheerID student verification → complete profile (unlocks Skill Builder) → earn badges (articles, comments, streaks) to unlock the $10/$20 credits and $100 voucher. Credits apply to AWS usage (incl. messaging services) if you have/created an AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* Skill Builder benefit is 1 year; badge credits/voucher have no expiry stated on the page; program is ongoing.
  - *Gotchas:* Rewards are small ($10-$20 credits, $100 voucher) — meaningful but not a production budget; the cert voucher is a voucher, not exam free passage; no card needed but actual AWS production usage still requires a billing account.

- **AWS Free Tier — Free Plan: up to $200 credits, 6 months, no charges** (high) — As of the July 15, 2025 revamp (current structure, verified today): new customers get $100 in credits immediately on sign-up and can earn up to $100 more (5 console activities worth $20 each: launch an EC2 instance, create an RDS database, build a Lambda web app, try a Bedrock foundation model, set an AWS Budgets cost budget). Free plan = access to 90+ select services for up to 6 months with no charges and no surprise overages; you cannot exceed limits — remaining credits auto-apply. Upgrade to Paid plan any time; on the old sign-up experience unused credits stay usable up to 12 months from sign-up, but under the new sign-up experience (launched Oct 2026) the FAQ states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation. Free plan accounts auto-enroll at account creation.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (an existing or previously-had AWS account makes you ineligible). No .edu email or student status required — open to anyone worldwide. Credit card NOT required for most new customers (FAQ Q4: 'A payment method isn't required to sign up for most new customers. In some cases, we may request additional information, such as a payment method, to verify your identity'). NOT available in China (ZHY/Ningxia, BJS) regions or AWS GovCloud (US) regions (certain services such as Lambda excepted). Free plan + credits are NOT available through AWS Organizations: enrolling a Free plan account in Organizations upgrades it to Paid, and an Organization can benefit from offers via only one account (usage aggregated).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an AWS account, select Free plan (default). 2) $100 credit appears on the Credits page (up to 10 minutes to show). 3) Complete the five 'Explore AWS' activities in the AWS Management Console Home widget to earn up to $100 more. 4) Monitor balance/days remaining via the Cost & Usage widget or Settings Portal (new sign-up experience) / Credits page in Billing console (pre-Oct-2026 sign-ups). 5) Upgrade to Paid plan to continue after expiry.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan expires at the earlier of 6 months from account creation or credit exhaustion (cannot be extended). After expiry AWS suspends the account and resources; data is retained 90 days, then the account and all content are permanently erased unless upgraded within 90 days. Credits expire 6 months from account creation on the new sign-up experience (FAQ); 12 months per the July 2025 Free Tier Terms for earlier sign-ups.
  - *Gotchas:* Suspended after expiry — move data/projects before the 90-day window or lose them. Free plan is 'evaluation only' per terms (sensitive data processing disallowed). Credits can't be transferred to other accounts. AWS may reclaim resources after 3 months of inactivity with 30 days' notice. Unused monthly free amounts don't roll over. Activating paid-only services upgrades you to Paid plan (bills on pay-as-you-go beyond credits). EC2/RDS/S3/API Gateway on new accounts are no longer fixed monthly free allowances — they consume the $200 credit pool; new free-plan-eligible EC2 types: t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large (t2.micro is legacy only).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/
- **AWS Free Tier — 30+ Always Free services (all customers, indefinite)** (high) — All AWS customers (new and existing, Free and Paid plans) get over 30 'Always Free' services with monthly usage caps; usage is aggregated across all regions; excess usage automatically applies Free Tier credits (Free plan) or bills at standard on-demand rates (Paid plan). Representative always-free caps (per service docs and community verification, not all published on the /free/ page itself): Lambda 1M requests + 400,000 GB-seconds; DynamoDB 25 GB + 25 provisioned RCU / 5 WCU; SQS 1M requests; SNS 1M publishes; CloudWatch 10 custom metrics + 10 alarms + 1M API requests; CloudFront 1 TB data transfer out.
  - *Eligibility:* Any AWS customer — no .edu, no student status, no credit card required for the account itself. Available in regions where AWS operates (Always Free offers are region-broader than the Free Plan credits, which exclude China and GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Automatic once you have an AWS account; no application.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while you hold an AWS account; monthly limits reset each month with no rollover. AWS may terminate any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Data transfer out (egress) beyond CloudFront's 1 TB and inter-region traffic are billable; paid add-on features auto-bill on Paid plans; individual service caps vary — check each service's pricing page before heavy use. The per-service figures above are medium-confidence (sourced from the EC2 docs and builder-community summaries, since the /free/ page lists service tiles without caps).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Free Tier — short-term trials on selected Paid-plan services** (medium) — Selected Paid-plan services can be 'tried' with limited free trials; the trial begins when you activate the service, and any eligible Free Tier credits automatically apply to usage beyond trial limits. Some offers are limited in duration or free-usage amount; on a Free plan, activating a paid-only service upgrades the account to Paid plan.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers (per-service trials listed on aws.amazon.com/free/); no .edu required; same region caveats (Free Plan credits unavailable in China/GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Activate the trial service from the console; track the trial end date yourself — AWS does not always notify when a trial ends.
  - *Expiry:* Per-service trial windows; AWS may end any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Always Free confusion: trial periods end and pay-as-you-go billing starts without a notification in some cases — delete trial resources before expiry. Egress/data-transfer and public IPv4 charges ($0.005/hr) commonly surprise trial users.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Educate — free self-paced curriculum, hands-on labs, badges, job board** (high) — Free, self-paced online cloud training 'designed for beginners': hundreds of hours of content incl. Getting Started with Storage (S3), Compute (EC2), Networking (VPC), Databases (RDS), and Cloud Operations + cost-estimation labs, a Cloud 101 pathway, and a Cloud Literacy interactive badge. Includes free hands-on labs in a sandboxed AWS Console environment (time-boxed, unlimited retries), shareable Credly digital badges, an invitation to the AWS Emerging Talent Community, and an AWS Educate Job Board (18+) with in-demand jobs and internships. No dollar-denominated AWS cloud credits — it is a learning program, not a credit grant.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any individual regardless of education, experience, or career journey — NO .edu email or university affiliation required. Register with just an email address; no credit card needed. Minimum age: 'as young as 13' per the current page; the Educate FAQ says 14+ generally, with EU 16+; Algeria, Lebanon, Portugal 18+; not offered in China or Switzerland.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Register at the AWS Educate sign-up page (promo code 'EducateLP' referenced on the page) or sign in to AWS Educate. 2) Choose a learning path (Cloud 101 recommended for new-to-cloud). 3) Complete labs/courses. 4) Claim digital badges on Credly. 5) Job board access at 18+.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; content and lab time limits update over time; lab attempts are time-boxed but retries are unlimited.
  - *Gotchas:* Labs run on shared sandboxes, not your own AWS account (does not stack with your Free Tier account). No monetary credits. Employer direct-messaging on the job board is US-only per the FAQ. Age minimum varies by country (higher in EU and some regions than the '13+' marketing). No certification exam included — AWS certifications remain paid (see AWS Academy vouchers).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Skillbuilder — 500-600+ free self-paced digital training courses** (high) — AWS's Training FAQ: 'Self-paced digital training on AWS Skill Builder is free.' Free digital training includes 500+ on-demand courses in 17 languages (the /training/ page markets '600+ free digital courses'); free digital badges (knowledge, Cloud Quest) earned at >=80% on the associated assessment, claimable on Credly. Paid Individual/Team subscriptions add more content and labs; free webinars/Twitch training events also exist, while classroom training and certification exams cost money.

  - *How to claim:* 1) Open AWS Skill Builder and sign in or create a free AWS Builder ID. 2) Enroll in any free digital course. 3) Score >=80% on the assessment to earn a badge. 4) Claim the badge via Credly.

  - *Gotchas:* Only part of Skillbuilder is free — advanced lab-heavy content is behind paid Individual/Team subscriptions; certification exams are paid (vouchers only via AWS Academy pathways); don't confuse free digital badges with paid AWS certification badges.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/faqs/
- **AWS Activate — Founders package: $1,000 credits (select participants up to $5,000)** (high) — For self-funded, early-stage startups new to AWS Activate Credits: $1,000 USD in AWS credits (per AWS's gaming-startups page: 'Start with $1,000 USD in Activate Credits. Select participants may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000'). Credits redeem on 200+ eligible AWS services incl. third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock. Also includes technical support, business mentorship, co-build/co-market/go-to-market resources, free pre-built cloud templates, AWS Startup Advisor, AWS Support Plans eligible for credits, and access to 80+ third-party offers. New customers also get the AWS Free Tier when creating a new account.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup program — NOT an education/.edu program (student-founded startups can apply as startups). Criteria: self-funded (or pre-Series B per AWS's June 2026 guide), founded in the past 10 years, a fully functioning company website or public company profile, new to Activate Credits (or requesting more than previously received), and a business email matching the company domain. No student-specific grant exists.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a personal AWS Builder ID with a professional email. 2) Complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Founders package. 4) Submit company/product/funding details (accurate info required; inaccuracies can be denied). 5) Link an AWS account and submit; decision in 7-10 business days by email, trackable in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are subject to the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant, not re-stated on these pages); not renewable at the same value without a larger request.
  - *Gotchas:* Without a functioning startup/website you won't qualify. AWS has final say; website professionalism is weighted heavily. Credits don't cover Marketplace third-party services, Route 53 domain registration/transfer, most support tiers, or Professional Services (except Bedrock third-party model charges). AWS offers generally not available in China regions.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-activate-credits
- **AWS Activate — Portfolio package: up to $200,000 credits (provider-backed)** (medium) — For startups affiliated with an AWS Activate Provider (accelerator, VC, incubator, or partner org — e.g., Y Combinator, a16z, nVIDIA, Carta): up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits per the main /startups/ page and AWS's June 4, 2026 step-by-step guide ('The Activate Portfolio tier offers up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits for provider-backed startups'); AI startups ready to scale can get additional credits beyond $200,000 via their Account Manager. Also: 80+ exclusive third-party offers/memberships valued up to $800,000, dedicated account managers, and solutions architects. Note: the /free/offers/ comparison page still lists the range as '$1,000 to $100,000' with eligibility 'funded up to and including Series A' — the $200k / pre-Series-B figures come from AWS's newer 2026 pages, indicating the cap and funding stage were raised in 2025-2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Must hold an Organizational ID from an AWS Activate Provider; pre-Series B (most recent funding round within the last 12 months, if applicable); founded in the past 10 years; a fully functioning company website; business email; must be new to Activate Credits or requesting more than previously received. Not open to direct application — provider affiliation is mandatory. No .edu/student path.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Enroll in a program with an AWS Activate Provider and get their Org ID/invitation. 2) Create an AWS Builder ID + complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Portfolio package with your Org ID. 4) Submit details; link an AWS account; decision in 7-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Per the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant).
  - *Gotchas:* Provider gatekeeping (AWS has final say and gets pickier above $5,000). Official AWS pages conflict today on the cap: $100k on /free/offers/ vs $200k on /startups/ and the June 2026 guide — treat 'up to $200,000' as the current advertised max and verify at application. Legacy sub-tiers ($5k/$10k/$25k/$100k; YC's up to $250k) are no longer publicly listed.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/
- **AWS Academy — free participation for accredited higher-education institutions (+ exam vouchers)** (high) — AWS Academy 'provides higher education institutions with a free, ready-to-teach cloud computing curriculum' (aligned to AWS certifications) plus Learner Labs. FAQ: 'There is no cost to join.' Educators may request a 100% discount voucher on AWS certification exam cost; member institutions may request a 50% discount exam voucher that students redeem at 100% of the voucher's value; students in badge-eligible courses earn Emerging Talent Community points redeemable for rewards such as discounted AWS certification exam vouchers. This is AWS's closest university-scoped free offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional, not individual: accredited higher-education institution offering degrees, diplomas, certificates, or continuing-ed programs, with an authorized Program Administrator or Central Point of Contact (CPOC). No .edu-email-only rule, but institutional membership is required; individual students/educators can't self-enroll.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Institution applies to join AWS Academy (or is added to an existing membership). 2) CPOC nominates educators; educators complete the mandatory Getting Started course. 3) Educators teach courses; students use Learner Labs. 4) Educators/students claim exam discount vouchers per program rules.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the institution remains a member; voucher/reward programs are 'subject to change' per the FAQ.
  - *Gotchas:* Vouchers discount exam fees only — they are not AWS usage credits. Institutional onboarding can be slow; the free value to students is mainly the curriculum, labs, and exam discounts — hands-on AWS usage still comes from the regular Free Tier (which needs no Academy affiliation).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/awsacademy/faq/
- **2025–26 change:** SUNSET / MATERIAL CHANGES 2024-2026: (1) The classic AWS 12-month free tier (EC2 750 hrs/mo, S3 5 GB, 1M input + 1M output free Bedrock model-invocation tokens per month for 12 months across ~75 models, RDS 750 hrs, etc.) has been SUPERSEDED by the new Free plan / Paid plan model: $100 signup credits + up to $100 earned, 6-month window, 30+ 'always free' services, and notably no payment method required for most new customers. The Bedrock-specific free-token docs page (docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/free-trial.html) now renders empty/removed, and the new Free Tier FAQ contains zero mention of Bedrock free tokens, 750 EC2 hours, or 5 GB S3 - legacy per-service allowances no longer exist. So the 'free Bedrock tokens for students' front is effectively dead; Bedrock access now comes via (a) Free plan $100-200 credits, (b) AWS Activate credits, or (c) Kiro credits for agentic coding. (2) June 2024: AWS doubled the portfolio/accelerate credit ceiling from $100,000 to $200,000 (announced July 1, 2024, for startups that raised Series A in the past 12 months; seed stage stayed at $100,000 at the time; credits' expiry moved from 1 year to 3 years - per CNBC 2024-06-28). The current credits page (2026) shows one Portfolio tier at up to $200,000. (3) 2026: the credits page was redesigned (awslabs/startups PR #75, June 2026) renaming tiers to Activate Founders ($1,000 start, up to $5,000 - NOT the ~$100 sometimes misreported; the $100 figure matches the new Free plan signup credit, not Activate), Activate Portfolio (up to $200,000), and adding the invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier ($200,000+) plus the new Kiro Startup Credits program. (4) 'Known so far' figures from the task (~$100K Portfolio, ~$100 Founders) are outdated: Portfolio is now up to $200K and Founders is $1,000-to-start/up-to-$5,000. No standalone student Bedrock/LLM free offer from AWS exists today - the closest student fronts are the no-card Kiro Student Offer (1,000 credits/month x 12 months) and AWS Academy (training only, no credits). Verification caveat: today's live fetches of aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ and free sub-pages intermittently returned 403 via proxy, but credits-page content was captured today via the official page mirror in the awslabs/startups repo and search snippets of the live page; free-plan figures came directly from aws.amazon.com/free/ and /free/free-tier-faqs/ read today.</sunset_notes>
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes (all verified on official AWS pages fetched today, 2026-10-22; WebSearch budget was exhausted so only official-page fetches were used): (1) The legacy usage-based 12-month AWS Free Tier (750 RDS hours, 5 GB S3, etc.) was replaced by a credit-based model for accounts created on/after July 15, 2025 — $100 at sign-up + up to $100 earned, Free plan (6-month auto-closing account) vs Paid plan (12-month credit validity); a refreshed sign-up experience launched October 2026, and pre-October accounts still see the old credits console. The RDS pricing page still documents the legacy 750-hour/20 GB free database for pre-July-15-2025 accounts only. (2) Per-service 'Always Free' and legacy free-trial figures (e.g., Athena GB-per-month, Redshift RA3/RA2 1-node 12-month developer tier and 30-day standard tier, Glue 1M source-records ETL free allowance, Bedrock character-based free tiers) no longer appear on today's service pricing pages — the credit model absorbed them, so do not quote legacy numbers for new accounts. (3) Activate program structure is now three tiers: Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000), Portfolio (up to $200,000 via provider Org ID), and an invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier adding $200,000+ for AI startups scaling past the Portfolio ceiling. Credits now explicitly cover Amazon Bedrock third-party models (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta). Note a discrepancy: /free/offers/ still lists Activate as '$1,000 to $100,000' — likely stale vs the $200,000 ceiling on the credits page/FAQ. (4) New Kiro startup credits program (up to a year of Kiro Pro+, 2/10/30-user tiers, 'limited time only', excludes France/Germany/Italy/Spain/Poland/UAE and ~13 other countries). (5) AWS Academy / Cloud Career Academies: no working page found on today's main AWS site (URLs 404; cloudcareeracademies.org DNS-failed) — status unconfirmed; AWS Educate (free, 13+, no credits/vouchers) is explicitly still active with a 2026 copyright. Also of note: AWS Educate bundles no cloud credits and no free exam vouchers on any page I could read today.

- **2025–26 change:** Key 2025-2026 changes: (1) 2025-07-15 cutover - AWS replaced the legacy 12-month service-level Free Tier with a credit-based model: new accounts get a 6-month 'Free plan' ($100 signup credits + up to $100 service-exploration credits = up to $200) or a Paid plan; accounts created before 2025-07-15 are grandfathered on the old 12-month per-service free tier (e.g. RDS: 750 hours/month single-AZ, 20 GB gp2 storage, 20 GB backup). (2) Free-plan accounts auto-close at 6 months or when credits run out; data retained 90 days, then permanently erased. (3) Startup program restructuring: the /startups/credits/ page now lists 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000; starts at $1,000) and 'Activate Portfolio' (up to $200,000, was the $150,000 Founders' Program era ceiling; the previous 'Accelerate' up to $100,000 tier name no longer appears) plus two newer tracks - 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+, invite-only post-Portfolio) and 'Kiro Startup Credits'. The exact 2025-2026 rename/retirement dates could not be confirmed: WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so only the current official pages could be verified. (4) No .edu-gated student dollar-credit license was found: the education front is AWS Educate (free training/badges, no credits) and institutional 'special pricing' programs; AWS Academy pages returned 404 on fetched sub-paths, so its current status/offerings are unverified rather than confirmed active or sunsetting. (5) Free-plan credits are unavailable in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US); the Free plan also excludes all promotional credits (Activate), so startup credits effectively require a Paid-plan account.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 - the classic 12-month per-service Free Tier was replaced for NEW accounts with a credit-based model: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned via 5 console activities, a 6-month Free plan window (account closes at 6 months or when credits run out), 90+ services, and 30+ always-free services; accounts created before July 15, 2025 retain the legacy 12-month tier. Free-tier-eligible EC2 instances changed too (t2.micro dropped; t3.small, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large added). (2) Short-term free trials are now paid-plan-only; the Free plan only gets 'Always Free' offers. (4) Feb 16, 2026 - Free plan (Free Tier) accounts became ineligible for promotional credits, which affects AWS Cloud Credit for Research and similar programs (a Paid plan is required to hold awards). (5) Aug 20, 2026 - AWS launched Student Rewards on AWS Builder Center as part of a $500M+ student commitment; the old 'AWS Activate for Students' style offer no longer appears on aws.amazon.com - student benefits are now Builder Center Student Rewards + AWS Educate + AWS Academy. (6) October 2026 - new AWS sign-up experience launched; credit-expiry display moved to the Settings Portal (accounts that signed up before Oct 2026 still use the Credits page in the Billing console). No free offer listed above was fully sunset as of 2026-10-22.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 — AWS replaced the long-standing 12-month Free Tier (750 hrs/mo t2.micro/t3.micro + 30 GB EBS + 100 GB egress, 30+ always-free services) with a credit-based model for NEW accounts: $100 sign-up credits + up to $100 earned = up to $200, with a 6-month no-cost Free plan that auto-closes. Legacy 12-month benefits remain ONLY for accounts created before July 15, 2025. Also, since the revamp, a credit card/payment method is no longer required to sign up for most new customers (previously mandatory). A new sign-up experience (Settings Portal credit tracking) launched October 2026. (2) June 2026 — AWS Activate Portfolio credit ceiling doubled from $100,000 to $200,000; the Founders tier was standardized at $1,000 with an 'up to $5,000' extension for select participants; a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier (>$200K) was introduced (confirmed by the redesigned aws.amazon.com/startups/credits page © 2026 and the awslabs/startups repo content update June 16, 2026). Note: CNBC's June 28, 2024 report that Series A startups got $200K from July 1, 2024 was NOT reflected in 2025 official materials (which still said 'up to $100,000'); the cap only officially moved to $200K in June 2026. - **2025–26 change:** Sunset/changed in 2025-2026: (1) 15 Jul 2025 (announced AWS Summit NY, 15-16 Jul 2025): the 12-month Free Tier was retired for NEW accounts and replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup credit + up to $100 explore credits, 6-month auto-closing plan, 90-day post-expiry data retention); pre-cutoff accounts keep their legacy 12-month offers until each one's own expiry. (2) EC2 free-VPS offer sunset: t2.micro (and hours-based t3.micro 750 h/mo) free tiers no longer apply to new accounts; new free-tier-eligible EC2 types are t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large, consumed via credits. (3) AWS Activate was folded into AWS for Startups: /aws.amazon.com/activate/ now serves the startups program (startups.aws.com); headline credit figure is 'up to $200,000' with published tiers $1,000-$100,000, 80+ partner offers valued up to $800,000, plus undisclosed extra AI-startup credits and Kiro credits — replacing older multi-year $1M total caps and old student/faculty Activate tiers (up to $100k/$150k) which no longer appear on official pages. (4) The Free Plan explicitly cannot combine with any other promotional/incentive offer, and joining AWS Organizations/Control Tower auto-upgrades to Paid and voids remaining explore credits. (5) AWS signals a new sign-up experience in October 2026 (FAQ note); Free Tier Terms last updated 9 Jul 2025. Note: session web-search budget was exhausted, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on WebFetch of official AWS pages (free/, free/offers/, free/free-tier-faqs/, free/terms/, startups/, education/, EC2 docs, Lambda pricing, RDS free page, what's-new Jul 2025 entry, AWS blog 15 Jul 2025) rather than broader search.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes (all verified against AWS pages today, 2026-10-22): (1) Jul 15, 2025 — the legacy "12 months free" tier was replaced for NEW accounts by a credit-based Free Tier: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned, 6-month Free plan; every legacy 12-month offer on pre-Jul-15-2025 accounts (including legacy CloudWatch 12-month items) has now fully lapsed. (2) Oct 2026 — AWS launched a new sign-up experience: social sign-in (Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon), no credit card needed for most customers, and credit tracking moved from the Billing console to a Settings Portal for accounts signed up after the rollout; the Free Tier FAQ now states base credits expire 6 months (vs. 12 months in the Jul 9, 2025 Terms) — a live contradiction AWS hasn't harmonized, so treat 6 months as the safe planning horizon. (3) Dec 3, 2025 — Kiro Startup Credits v1 launched (up to 100 users, pre-seed to Series B, apply by Dec 31, 2025) and closed; relaunched Apr 7, 2026 with 2/10/30-user tiers, early-stage to Series A (VC-backed per the current promotion), apply by Dec 31, 2026; startups with active AWS Activate credits are excluded from the Kiro promo. (4) Jun 2024 (context) — AWS doubled Portfolio credits $100K to $200K and extended expiry 1 year to 3 years; still the structure in effect. (5) Feb 2023 (context) — AWS Educate stopped issuing universal/institutional credit grants; it is now free learning only, and student credits now flow through Builder Center Student Rewards (up to $579 incl. $30 credits + $100 exam voucher, launched Aug 2026). (6) CloudFront's classic "100 GB/month always-free" line is now presented as a standing $0 "Free" plan tier with 100 GB + 1M requests + 5 GB S3 storage credits and no overage charges — not sunset, but restructured. No CloudWatch/observability offer was sunset; the known limits (10 metrics, 1M API requests, 5 GB logs, 100 canaries, 3 dashboards, 10 alarms, 100K X-Ray traces) all still apply as ALWAYS-FREE allowances. There is no CloudWatch-specific startup or student grant beyond the general AWS Free Tier credits and Activate credits, which can offset CloudWatch usage on a Paid plan.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes verified on official pages today (2026-10-22): (1) The legacy 12-month Free Tier is gone — the old 'free for 12 months' language no longer appears on aws.amazon.com/free; it has been replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup + up to $100 explore = up to $200 over 6 months), after which the account auto-closes (data kept 90 days, then erased). (2) The classic 'Always Free' list was narrowed in messaging to '30+ services' with monthly limits (legacy tier had 60+ services); per-service quota numbers are no longer published on the hub. (3) SageMaker notebook free allowance shrunk to 250 hours of sc.t3.medium for the first 2 months (vs legacy 750 hours/month ml.t3.medium for 12 months). (4) AWS Activate tiers renamed/revalued: 'Activate Community' no longer appears on the current credits page — it is now 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000, start $1,000) alongside 'Activate Portfolio' (now up to $200,000, previously up to $100,000) plus a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+); a new Kiro Startup Credits track (up to 1 year of Kiro Pro+, 1/10/30 users) was added. The stale /free/offers page still shows old $1,000-$100,000 figures and 7-10 day processing, inconsistent with the current credits page ($5,000/$200,000, 5-10 days) — treat the credits page as authoritative. (5) WebSearch was unavailable during this run, so no third-party press confirmation of announcement dates was possible; all claims above are from the official AWS pages fetched today.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Amazon Pinpoint sunset (HARD DATE): AWS ends support for Amazon Pinpoint on October 30, 2026 — console access and all Pinpoint resources (endpoints, segments, campaigns, journeys, analytics) terminate that day; only the messaging-channel APIs (SMS, voice, mobile push, OTP, phone number validate) continue under the rebranded "AWS End User Messaging" service. Anyone relying on Pinpoint journeys/campaigns must migrate before 2026-10-30. 2) Legacy per-service Free Tier replaced: the old model — e.g., "SES: 3,000 emails/month free for the first 12 months, then $0.10/1K" and "SNS: 100 SMS/month free, US regions" — is no longer published; as of the October 2026 sign-up experience, new accounts instead get up to $200 in Free Tier credits over 6 months (Free plan auto-closes at 6 months or when credits run out). The "then $0.10/1K" figure survives, but as the à-la-carte SES price, while NEW SES accounts default to the more expensive Essentials plan ($0.16/1K) starting July 21, 2026. 3) The formerly permanent "3,000 emails/month free (US regions, no purchase of sending capacity)" SES allowance is not listed on today's official pricing page — treat it as superseded by the credit model for new accounts; legacy account behavior not verifiable (docs pages didn't render). 4) The legacy 100-free-SMS/month US-regions tier also no longer appears on End User Messaging pricing; SMS is now fully pay-per-message. 5) AWS Activate credit ceiling was raised to $200,000 (some pages still show the old $100,000 figure — a 2025-2026 program change, not a sunset).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes (verified against official AWS pages on 2026-10-22): (1) Classic 12-month Free Tier sunset for NEW accounts on July 15, 2025 — replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 + up to $100 earned = up to $200 over 6 months, auto-enrolled, no charges); accounts created before July 15, 2025 remain on legacy 12-month terms (750 hrs/mo t2/t3.micro etc.); legacy terms still published. (2) October 2026: AWS launched a new sign-up experience; the current FAQ now states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation (vs 12 months under the July 2025 Free Tier Terms) and moved credit tracking to the Settings Portal — AWS's own FAQ is internally inconsistent between sections (6 vs 12 months), so treat credit expiry on the new experience as 6 months and verify per-account in the Settings Portal. (3) AWS Activate restructured in 2025-2026: old tiers (Starter $5k / Portfolio up to $100k / YC up to $250k / $5k-$100k sub-tiers) are no longer publicly listed; current structure is Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000 for select participants) + Portfolio (up to $200,000, cap raised from $100,000), with eligibility shifted from 'funded up to Series A' to 'pre-Series B, most recent funding round within the last 12 months', and the application flow now goes through the AWS Builder ID. AWS pages are inconsistent today: /free/offers/ still advertises '$1,000 to $100,000' while /startups/ and the June 4, 2026 AWS blog advertise 'up to $200,000'. (4) No education-only (.edu/student-email) free credits exist at AWS: no free offer requires a .edu or student email — AWS Educate is open to anyone 13/14+ with just an email, the Free Tier is open to any new customer, and AWS Marketplace 'education pricing' is a paid discount, not free. The only university-scoped free offer is AWS Academy (free institution participation + exam vouchers). (5) Free Plan/credits unavailable in China (ZHY, BJS) and GovCloud (US) regions, and not usable via AWS Organizations (auto-upgrades to Paid plan). (6) AWS Educate availability excludes China and Switzerland and raises minimum age in the EU (16) and Algeria/Lebanon/Portugal (18).
- **Critic correction** (AWS Activate (Amazon Bedrock)): Figures are out of date. The Activate Founders self-serve package is $1,000 (select participants can reach $5,000), not about $100. The Portfolio package, for startups tied to an Activate Provider with an Org ID, is now up to $200,000; it was $100,000 before June 2026. Credits can be used on Bedrock third-party models. AI startups ready to scale can talk to an account manager about more.
- **Critic correction** (AWS Startups): Range is outdated. Current tiers are Activate Founders at $1,000 (up to $5,000 for select startups) and Activate Portfolio at up to $200,000 (raised from $100,000 in June 2026). The top of the range should read $200,000 for Portfolio, not $100,000+.
- **Critic correction** (Up to ~$100K in AWS credits (Portfolio/Partner tier; ~$100 Founders self-serve)): The current official maximum is up to $200,000 for the Portfolio tier (requires a provider Org ID; pre-Series B). The Founders (self-funded) tier starts at $1,000, with up to $5,000 for select participants, not about $100.

### Chroma Cloud Starter

- **Starter free tier ($0/mo + metered usage)** (high) — Starter plan: $0/month base + usage; 10 databases and 10 team members included. Metered rates: writes $2.50/GiB (logical, uncompressed, billed once per write — no background-index charges), storage $0.33/GiB/month prorated hourly, queries $0.0075/TiB queried (each metadata/full-text predicate counts as extra queries), network $0.09/GiB returned, forking $0.03/fork, Sync $0.04/GiB processed + $0.01/page extracted/scraped. Self-hosted open-source ChromaDB (Apache 2.0) is also $0 with no limits.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone creating a new Chroma Cloud account. No .edu email, no startup age/funding cap, no credit card required at sign-up (auth via Google, GitHub, SSO, or email). No regional restrictions published; service endpoints are aws-us-east-1 (US) and gcp-europe-west1 (EU); BYOC single-tenant only on Enterprise.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at trychroma.com/signup (Google/GitHub/SSO/email) and select the Starter plan at trychroma.com/pricing — $0 base, no application. Exceeding 10 databases or 10 team members requires the Team plan ($250/month + usage).
  - *Expiry:* No duration limit: it is a permanent $0 plan, not a trial; no commitment. Plan changes pro-rate to the calendar month. Unused usage credits 'do not expire' in most cases (per pricing FAQ).
  - *Gotchas:* Exceeding either customer-set or Chroma-set usage limits pauses the service until a limit is changed — set a billing limit after the $5 credits run out. After credits are used, a credit card must be added to keep using the service (per Chroma's Oct 2025 '5 Things to Know' video). Egress/network ($0.09/GiB returned) and hourly storage billing apply; filter-heavy queries cost more than query count suggests (each predicate adds query units). Team plan floor is $250/month; Enterprise adds single-tenant/BYOC/SLAs.
  - *Source:* https://www.trychroma.com/pricing
- **$5 trial credits for new users** (high) — One-time $5 in usage credits granted to new Chroma Cloud accounts (docs FAQ: 'We offer $5 in credits to new users'). Chroma's own video estimates $5 covers ~100K document inserts and ~100K queries. Billing is account-level, aggregated across all collections/tenants in the account.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new Chroma Cloud account (new users only; not per team member). No verification, no .edu requirement, no credit card required to receive the credits.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic at sign-up via trychroma.com/signup — no application, no code, no separate claim step.
  - *Expiry:* Credits do not expire in most cases (pricing FAQ: 'in most cases your credits do not expire'; the $100 Team-plan inclusion does not rollover — not relevant to Starter). No renewal cycle; new credits are not re-granted.
  - *Gotchas:* Small grant: $5 buys roughly 2 GiB of writes at $2.50/GiB. After the credits are exhausted, a credit card is required to continue (trial-vs-free: the $0 plan is permanent, but the trial credits are one-time — ongoing usage is metered). Hitting usage limits pauses the service until limits are adjusted.
  - *Source:* https://docs.trychroma.com/cloud/pricing
- **Partner startup perk: $5,000 Chroma credits via PostHog for Startups** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Listed on third-party directory creditbase.in (database category): accepted PostHog for Startups participants can receive $5,000 in Chroma credits as a partner perk (Chroma-issued, global). No Chroma-owned startup program, age cap, or funding cap is documented on Chroma's own pricing or docs pages.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups accepted into PostHog for Startups (i.e., PostHog's program rules apply, typically early-stage companies); no .edu requirement from Chroma.
  - *How to claim:* Via the PostHog for Startups partner perks, not through any Chroma page or form found as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated in the listing; treat as current-until-confirmed (directory is a third-party aggregator).
  - *Gotchas:* Could not verify this offer on any official Chroma page today (not on pricing, docs, or changelog); amount, availability, and whether it still applies are unconfirmed — confirm with Chroma/PostHog before relying on it.
  - *Source:* https://creditbase.in/category/database
- **2025–26 change:** No official Chroma education/student licensing or Chroma-owned startup program exists on trychroma.com or docs.trychroma.com as of 2026-10-22 — no education or startup pages, and no such offers are mentioned on the pricing page, docs, or changelog. The free offer set is: (1) the $0/mo Starter plan with 10 databases / 10 team members, (2) one-time $5 new-user credits, (3) self-hosted open-source ChromaDB (Apache 2.0, fully free, unlimited), plus one unverified third-party-listed startup perk ($5,000 credits via PostHog for Startups). History/sunset: Chroma Cloud launched 2025-08-18 (changelog 'Introducing Chroma Cloud'), superseding the earlier Chroma Cloud trial; third-party tracker AgentDeals records a pricing-model change (discovered 2026-09-07, effective date unknown) from a legacy free tier (reported ~1M embeddings, 1 collection, 10K queries/day — unverified) to the current $0 Starter + $5 credits + metered pricing; the current metered terms were confirmed on the official pricing page today, so no free offer was sunsetting recently. Beware name confusion: 'Chroma Awards' (chromaawards.com), a film/music/games competition that recruited universities and students in 2025 with 'starter packs', internships and a $3M+ credit pool, is a separate organization, not the ChromaDB vendor. Course-sponsored credits (e.g., $250 Chroma Cloud credits for students of Hugo Bowne-Anderson's 'Building AI Applications' masterclass, Nov 2025 and Mar 2026 cohorts) are time-limited, sponsor-funded, cohort-specific — not a Chroma program.

### ClickHouse Cloud trial

- **ClickHouse Cloud free trial: $300 credits over 30 days (no credit card)** (high) — Official pricing page (verified 2026-10-22): "$300 in credits over 30 days", 1 ClickHouse Credit (CHC) = $1 USD of usage. FAQ: "Is there a free tier? ClickHouse Cloud starts with $300 in credits over 30 days, no credit card required." Trial ends after 30 days or once credits are consumed, whichever comes first; account then converts to pay-as-you-go with no monthly minimum (invoiced in $0.01 increments). No permanent free tier: entry paid tier is Basic (up to 1 TB storage, 8-12 GiB total memory, 24h backups retained 1 day, 1 availability zone). Compute scales to zero when idle ($0 for idle compute time; storage still bills continuously).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone. Sign up at https://console.clickhouse.cloud/signUp via email, Google SSO, or Microsoft SSO. No .edu email, no startup status, no company size limits stated. Available on AWS, GCP, and Azure. Credit card explicitly NOT required.
  - *How to claim:* Single step: sign up at console.clickhouse.cloud/signUp; the $300 trial credit is granted to new organizations automatically (campaign page: "Get started for free... Create a free account"). No application or form.
  - *Expiry:* 30 days or credit exhaustion, whichever comes first; no renewal or re-application. After the trial the account continues on pay-as-you-go usage-based billing.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial is not a free tier: once the 30 days/credits end, usage billing begins (no card on file doesn't mean no bill - you can accrue charges after trial, so monitor console). Egress/data transfer, backups, and ClickPipes ingestion are billed separately; public internet egress is from ~$0.1152/GB since the Jan 2025 price change (egress used to be free - now a migration 'divorce fee'). Storage bills even when compute is scaled to zero. Basic plan caps (1 TB, 8-12 GiB, single AZ) apply to the cheapest tier.
  - *Source:* https://clickhouse.com/pricing
- **Open-source ClickHouse - free forever (self-hosted)** (high) — "Open source (free forever)" per the official pricing page; Apache 2.0 core, no license cost, no per-unit billing, no duration limit. Official one-liner install: `curl https://clickhouse.com/ | sh`. This is the genuine free option for students on any budget: run it on a laptop, student cluster, or own cloud VM. Certain advanced/enterprise features are reserved for the proprietary/Enterprise edition or Cloud.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone (students, academics, startups, enterprises); no .edu or application required.
  - *How to claim:* Install locally or on self-managed infrastructure via the curl one-liner or package manager; no signup.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; license is free indefinitely.
  - *Gotchas:* Self-hosted means you own ops (upgrades, replication, hardware, cloud instance costs). Open-core model: enterprise-locked features unavailable in the OSS build; OSS version lags behind Cloud features.
  - *Source:* https://clickhouse.com/pricing
- **ClickHouse Startup Program: $5,000 credits for 12 months (partner-gated)** (high) — Official startup-offer page (verified 2026-10-22): "Founders can apply for $5000 in ClickHouse Cloud credits, expert training, and promotion opportunities". Credits valid for 12 months. Package also includes ClickHouse Basic Support, expert training, a joint blog-post feature/promotion on ClickHouse channels, and speaking slots at ClickHouse community events.
  - *Eligibility:* All of: company less than 3 years old; pre-Series D ("haven't raised a Series D or later funding round"); has NEVER been a paid ClickHouse Cloud customer; belongs to a program run by one of the listed partners (Antler, Bessemer, Blackbird, Google For Startups, Lightspeed, Merantix, Station F, TechLeap, TechStars - selected via dropdown in the form); holds a valid verification code obtained from the accelerator/incubator/VC. No .edu/region limits stated on the page. Headcount limits not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Three steps on the page: (1) sign up for a free ClickHouse Cloud trial (console signup); (2) complete the application form with your ClickHouse Organization ID, company details, and the partner-program dropdown; (3) wait for approval - the team reviews and confirms the credits.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid for 12 months from approval; no renewal or re-application window described on the page. One-time, new-customer-only offer.
  - *Gotchas:* Partner-gated: you cannot apply directly - must be inside a listed partner's program and have its verification code, or the application can't be validated. New-to-ClickHouse requirement excludes any prior paid usage. Hitting Series D before applying disqualifies. Support included is 'Basic' tier only, not enterprise support. No stated extension of the 12-month window.
  - *Source:* https://clickhouse.com/startup-offer
- **ClickHouse x Y Combinator deal: $10,000 credits for 24 months** (high) — Official deals/ycombinator page (verified 2026-10-22): "$10,000 in ClickHouse Cloud credits, valid for 24 months" plus ClickHouse Basic Support, a one-hour expert training session, a joint blog-post feature/promotion, and speaking opportunities at ClickHouse community events. Audience labeled "Current & Alumni" of YC, pre-Series D, new to ClickHouse Cloud.
  - *Eligibility:* All of: "You're part of the current Y Combinator program"; pre-Series D ("You haven't raised a Series D or later round"); holds a valid YC secret code for verification; "You are not a current ClickHouse Cloud customer". No .edu email requirement (this is a startup program, not a student one). No region limits stated.
  - *How to claim:* Three steps on the page: (1) sign up for a free ClickHouse Cloud trial; (2) complete the application form including your ClickHouse Organization ID and company details; (3) wait for approval - the team reviews and confirms the credits. The YC secret code is used for verification.
  - *Expiry:* Hard 24-month expiry on the credits; no renewal, extension, or stacking language on the page. One-time offer.
  - *Gotchas:* Existing ClickHouse Cloud customers are explicitly excluded. The YC secret code is mandatory - without it verification fails. Pre-Series D cap is a hard ceiling. No stated path to re-apply for a second tranche.
  - *Source:* https://clickhouse.com/deals/ycombinator
- **Student/education license: none found (no dedicated program as of 2026-10-22)** (medium) — No free education license, academic tier, .edu discount, or student-specific credit program was found. clickhouse.com/startups and /education return 404; the official llms.txt page index (90+ entries) lists no education/student program - only the trial ($300/30 days), the two startup programs above, a paid/optional certification track (learn/certification: "ClickHouse Certified Developer Exam"), and a government self-managed offering (FedRAMP). Students' free paths are: (a) the $300 trial for any individual, (b) open-source ClickHouse free forever. Verified via official page index and 404 checks on 2026-10-22, but the whole site was not crawled, so a low-visibility program cannot be fully ruled out.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A - no dedicated student eligibility (no .edu email gate found anywhere on official pages).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim; students use the $300 trial (sign up at console.clickhouse.cloud/signUp, no credit card) or self-host open-source ClickHouse for free.
  - *Expiry:* N/A.
  - *Gotchas:* The trial is not student-specific and converts to pay-as-you-go; if the program is ever launched, it would likely live at clickhouse.com/education or similar - none exists today.
  - *Source:* https://clickhouse.com/llms.txt

### CockroachDB Cloud trial

- **30-day free trial with $400 in credits (new Cloud orgs, incl. Continuum)** (high) — Every new CockroachDB Cloud organization gets a 30-day trial with $400 in credits; all usage (compute, storage, backups, data transfer, add-ons) draws down the $400 pool. Trial ends at 30 days or when the credit is exhausted, whichever first. New organizations created on/after Sep 15, 2026 land on the new 'Cockroach Continuum' pricing (Standard and Mission Critical editions, billed per vCPU-hour: Standard from $0.057/vCPU-hr on shared infra, 99.99% SLA; Mission Critical from $0.162/vCPU-hr on dedicated infra, 99.999% SLA). After the trial, clusters pause; a 30-day grace period follows (throttled, config changes restricted) during which adding a payment method restores clusters; after that, all clusters are permanently deleted and cannot be restored. Email milestones to Org Admins/Billing Coordinators at 50% and 75% credit consumption, trial end, <24h and grace end.
  - *Eligibility:* Only a user's FIRST organization qualifies; users invited to an organization do not get additional credits; organizations billed by invoice are excluded. No .edu or startup eligibility involved. No credit card required to start on the Standard edition (legacy Basic/Standard plans also card-free); Mission Critical REQUIRES a credit card because it runs on dedicated infrastructure. Region/provider limits on the card-free tier: Standard is AWS/GCP only (shared infra); Mission Critical adds Azure. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at cockroachlabs.com (cloud console) — 'Create a free Continuum Cloud organization'. 2) The $400 trial credit is applied automatically to the new organization (no coupon or form). 3) Deploy a cluster (Standard edition = no credit card). 4) Optionally add a payment method (console, or via AWS/Google Cloud Marketplace) before the trial ends to keep running.
  - *Expiry:* Trial: 30 days or $400 exhaustion, whichever is first; then 30-day grace period, then deletion. One trial per user's first organization only; does not renew.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs free tier: this is a one-time credit, not a perpetual free tier; it ends when EITHER 30 days pass or $400 is used. Standard: no CC needed; Mission Critical: CC required. Data/egress: same-region traffic between cluster nodes is free; cross-region and public-internet egress are billed at the cloud provider's list price and will consume the $400. After grace period, cluster deletion is irreversible. New deployments after Sep 15, 2026 are on Continuum pricing, not the legacy RU plans.
  - *Source:* https://cockroachlabs.com/pricing/
- **Basic plan free tier: 50M RUs + 10 GiB storage free per month (~$15/month free resource credit) — legacy CockroachDB Cloud plans** (medium) — The Basic plan starts at $0/month and includes 50 million Request Units and 10 GiB of storage free every month; on-demand compute/storage scale to zero. Billing docs describe the same benefit as the first $15 of monthly resource consumption 'credited back on the monthly invoice'. Basic targets smaller/bursty workloads up to ~30K RU/sec (~60 vCPUs), multi-region on AWS & GCP (select regions), 99.99% SLA. This is the only recurring free tier in the product line.
  - *Eligibility:* Applies to pay-as-you-go CockroachDB Cloud organizations; customers on annual or multi-year contracts are explicitly NOT eligible for the monthly free resource benefit. A payment method (credit card or cloud Marketplace subscription) must be on the account to receive the free credit. CAVEAT as of 2026-10-22: the RU-based plan pages state these prices 'apply to pay-as-you-go and committed-use customers with clusters created prior to September 15, 2026' and 'Such pricing is no longer available for purchase' — new orgs get Cockroach Continuum, which lists no free tier. So the free tier is effectively live only for pre-cutover / existing pay-as-you-go orgs.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for CockroachDB Cloud pay-as-you-go, add a payment method in the console (or subscribe via AWS/Google Cloud Marketplace), deploy on the Basic plan; the first 50M RUs + 10 GiB each month are credited back on the invoice automatically. No coupon needed.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing while the Basic plan is active for pay-as-you-go orgs; effectively sunset for new customers at the Sep 15, 2026 Continuum cutover.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit is monthly, not cumulative; it is a credit-back on invoice, not a hard cap — going over costs money, and a payment method is required to get the benefit at all. Annual-contract customers lose it. Under the Sep 15, 2026 cutover to Continuum this plan structure is no longer available for new purchases, so its practical value in 2026 is limited to existing pay-as-you-go orgs. Backups on Basic are daily and not configurable; IOPS and data transfer are bundled into RUs.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cockroachlabs.com/docs/cockroachcloud/costs
- **Free self-hosted use: CockroachDB Software License (CSL) limited non-production carve-out + legacy MIT-licensed releases** (medium) — Since Nov 18, 2024 (v24.3 and later major series), the CockroachDB server is no longer MIT-licensed: it ships under the proprietary 'CockroachDB Software License' (CSL), a key-gated license. The CSL permits use without a paid License Key only for 'Limited Non-Production Use' (design, prototyping, testing, development in non-production environments) or a database running solely on a single node. Multi-node production self-hosting requires a purchased License Key. Older releases remain available under their original licenses (MIT), so a genuinely free production-grade self-hosted path still exists for those versions.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone (no .edu, no CC); the free carve-out covers non-production use of any size and single-node production. Region limits: none (self-hosted).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Download a legacy MIT-licensed release (pre-Nov 2024, e.g. v24.2-series) for free production self-hosting, or 2) download the current v24.3+ build for free development/testing or single-node use; no registration or key needed for the carve-out. For multi-node production on current releases, purchase a License Key from Cockroach Labs.
  - *Gotchas:* The license text forbids operating multi-node production without a key, restricts benchmarking, mandates telemetry unless a key disables it, and is non-sublicensable/non-transferable. This is a 2024 license change, not a trial: it is a permanent free tier for non-production/single-node use, not a dollar credit and it covers no managed service or support. If you need the latest release in production, budget for the key.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/cockroachdb/cockroach
- **Startup program / education license: verified absent** (high) — No startup-credits program and no education (student/.edu/academic) free-license program was found on the official site as of 2026-10-22. /startup-program/ and /company/startup-program/ return 404; /resources/startup/ redirects to the homepage; /education/ is 404; the marketing sitemap (scanned in full today) contains no 'education', 'student', 'academic' or startup-program URLs — only marketing blog posts/webinars and a free 'Unicorn Lessons' architecture ebook for startups (no credits or discount). The 'education-first' blog is a customer case study, not a program. 'CockroachDB University' (free training, launched Nov 2019) is still referenced in the site footer at learn.cockroachlabs.com (fetch bot-blocked, HTTP 403); even when active it was free education/training only, with no free Cloud license or credits for students or educators.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no offer to claim.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim; the only free access for students/startups is the generic 30-day/$400 trial and (for pre-cutover orgs) the Basic free tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Absence verified via official 404s/redirects and a full marketing-sitemap scan today, but the session's web-search budget was exhausted, so I could not cross-check press releases or partner pages for an off-site program. Startup/education deals, if any, would come only via sales (account team), not public self-serve claims.
  - *Source:* https://cockroachlabs.com/sitemap.xml
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Sep 15, 2026 cutover: Cockroach Labs moved new deployments to 'Cockroach Continuum' (Standard/Mission Critical editions, per-vCPU-hour pricing). The legacy RU plans (including the $0/month Basic free tier with 50M RUs + 10 GiB free monthly) are 'no longer available for purchase' for new orgs; existing customers stay on old pricing unless they opt in. Continuum lists no recurring free tier — only the one-time 30-day/$400 trial. 2) Nov 18, 2024 relicensing: CockroachDB server moved from MIT to the proprietary, key-gated CockroachDB Software License (v24.3+); free self-hosting is now limited to non-production use or single-node deployments, while pre-Nov-2024 releases remain MIT. 3) Sep 26, 2024: the old 'Serverless'/'Dedicated' product names (which carried the original 'free for everybody, scales to zero' serverless framing) were absorbed into CockroachDB Cloud, per a reader's note on the serverless blog post. 4) No startup program and no education license are publicly offered today; Cockroach University (free training) is still footer-linked but bot-blocked (403) and never included a free database license. 5) WebSearch was unavailable this session (shared budget exhausted), so verification was done against official pages fetched 2026-10-22 (marketing pricing, docs.cockroachlabs.com, GitHub LICENSE) rather than press/news coverage of 2025-2026 changes.

### Databricks Lakebase

- **Databricks Free Edition (perpetual, no-credit-card, student/hobbyist tier)** (high) — Perpetually free 'limited Databricks account with no expiration for personal use'. Exactly 1 serverless workspace, no access to classic compute, 'limited compute size and usage' with daily fair-use limits (no exact DBU/storage numbers published on the page; a doc link 'community-edition-limitations' exists but is not rendered publicly with figures). Sign up with a personal email; no credit card or payment required. Free access to the unified Data + AI platform: notebooks, dashboards/Genie, agents, scheduled data workflows, plus free self-paced Databricks Academy training. FAQ: 'Free Edition is built for students, hobbyists, and aspiring data and AI professionals... not for production or commercial use.' 'Get Free Edition' routes to /signup/free-edition (provider=DB_FREE_TIER).
  - *Eligibility:* Any personal email (no .edu verification, no enrollment proof). Personal use only — commercial use explicitly barred. Region: all supported Databricks cloud regions; tier is account-level.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to databricks.com/learn/free-edition (or /signup/free-edition). 2) Sign up with a personal email. 3) You get one serverless workspace instantly; no cloud provider account or card needed. Databricks Academy courses are free at customer-academy.databricks.com using the 'Customer and Prospects' sign-up.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration ('perpetually free'). Daily usage limits apply. Legacy Community Edition accounts: 'access will continue to be available for the remainder of this year' (2026) — the older CE tier is being wound down in favor of Free Edition.
  - *Gotchas:* Lakebase is LOCKED in Free Edition — the trial-page comparison lists 'Locked features (no GPUs, clean rooms, Lakebase, enterprise admin etc.)'. So the free tier does NOT include the Postgres product. Also: no production/commercial use, one workspace, no classic compute, fair-use daily caps with no published quota, no support SLA.
  - *Source:* https://www.databricks.com/learn/free-edition
- **14-day free trial with up to $400 in usage credits (includes Lakebase 'try for free')** (high) — 'Full Databricks platform access for two (2) weeks used for business evaluation', 'up to $400 in free usage' credits, 'No credit card is required to start your Databricks free trial'. Express signup (email only, immediate serverless workspace) or AWS Marketplace signup (serverless + optional classic workspace in your own account). Docs confirm credits are 'valid for 14 days after you start your trial', non-transferable, trial ends when credits are exhausted, then the account 'switches to a pay-as-you-go model' (payment method: card, Marketplace, or commit contract). Personal-email trials are capped: max one SQL warehouse per workspace at 50 DBU/hr, serverless notebooks/jobs/Lakeflow capped at 50 DBU/hr, CPU only (no GPUs), one AI Search endpoint capped at 1 search unit. Lakebase: its pricing page states 'Pay as you go with a 14-day free trial' — i.e., Lakebase Postgres is reachable during the standard 14-day trial; the Lakebase product page's 'Try for free' CTA routes to the same signup. Lakebase create-flow: New project in workspace region (12 AWS regions listed), production branch, scale-to-zero enabled by default with a 24-hour inactivity timeout.
  - *Eligibility:* Work email 'recommended for full trial benefits' (business trials are unrestricted evaluation; personal email gets the 50-DBU/hr caps). No age/funding rules; no published per-person cap or region restriction on the trial itself, but Lakebase availability is limited to supported regions (12 AWS regions incl. us-east-1, us-west-2, eu-central-1, ap-northeast-1; Azure Premium = AWS/GCP Enterprise tier; 'displayed pricing does not guarantee product availability in that region').
  - *How to claim:* 1) databricks.com/free-trial -> 'Try Databricks' (/signup/express) with an email; or AWS Marketplace signup with existing AWS account. 2) Create a Lakebase project from the Lakebase Postgres app in your workspace (Autoscaling -> New project). 3) Connect with psql/PG drivers via OAuth (postgresql://user@databricks.com@ep-....databricks.com). 4) Upgrade before day 14 to keep remaining credits (they survive an upgrade).
  - *Expiry:* 14 days, no renewal of the same trial; credits expire with the trial unless upgraded to paid. After the trial, a payment method is required to continue.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: this is an evaluation trial, not a perpetual tier — Lakebase's only 'free' on-ramp is this trial; it is NOT available in the perpetual Free Edition. $400 is 'up to', not guaranteed; after expiry you're on pay-as-you-go with storage quotas (per-branch, adjustable on request) and possible egress/transfer costs (no public free egress allowance found). Credits cannot be combined with promotions or negotiated terms.
  - *Source:* https://www.databricks.com/free-trial
- **Databricks Startup Program: up to $200K in credits for Databricks and Neon** (medium) — Official startup page: qualifying startups receive 'up to $200K in credits for Databricks and Neon' (Neon = the serverless Postgres product behind Lakebase). Credits cover the Databricks Data + AI Platform incl. 'Database – Postgres for data apps and AI agents' (Lakebase) plus four 'solution accelerators' (recommendations, onboarding, LLM search, time-series forecasting with GenAI). No credit window, per-product split, or age/funding caps are published on the page.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups 'not founded... ' — no published eligibility criteria on the official page: no stated company age, funding-raised cap, revenue limit, or region. Two paths: apply directly (Google Sites form at sites.google.com/databricks.com/startup-program-apply; form fields not publicly readable), or be nominated by an investor — listed VC partners: Andreessen Horowitz, Battery, General Catalyst, Insight Partners, Menlo Ventures, NEA, among others.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply via the 'Apply now' form (Google Sites) with company details, OR 2) ask a partner VC to nominate you into the program. No public review-timeline; no published cap on number of credits beyond 'up to $200K'.
  - *Expiry:* Not published (no credit-usage window or program end date on the page; footer copyright 2026, content dated 2026-04).
  - *Gotchas:* Amount is a ceiling ('up to'), not a guarantee; exact allocation and duration are decided per-approval. The $200K spans two product families (Databricks + Neon) — split undisclosed. Eligibility caps are hidden in the application form. Startup credits don't replace the pay-as-you-go base: unused commitment after credit exhaustion bills normally.
  - *Source:* https://www.databricks.com/product/startups
- **University Alliance / education program (educators + students)** (high) — For educators: 'perpetually free Databricks experience for learning and experimentation' (Free Edition), dedicated online community, dedicated educator/student badges, free teaching resources and sample notebooks ('Get free resources' -> /resources/teach; 'Access Free Edition' -> /learn/free-edition). For students (via /resources/student-resources and /university/student-fellows): free Free Edition access, free self-paced Databricks Academy courses, University Alliance Student badge (LinkedIn-ready), and students who complete a course 'qualify for a 25% discount on Databricks certifications' (exam fee, not platform credits). Student Fellows sub-program: by invitation only ('invited for their exceptional contributions'), grants free Free Edition access plus 'free or discounted' certification vouchers, on-campus communities, and internship pipeline; application via Airtable form with a T&C PDF; no stipend or credit hours stated.
  - *Eligibility:* No .edu email requirement or enrollment verification is stated on any page. Free Edition portion: personal email, personal use only, any region with the tier. University Alliance: faculty/educators invited via the program; Student Fellows: invitation-only, no published geography or funding limits.
  - *How to claim:* Students: 1) sign up for Free Edition (provider=DB_FREE_TIER). 2) take free Academy courses at customer-academy.databricks.com ('Customer and Prospects' login). 3) earn the badge after finishing a course; claim the 25% certification discount after course completion. Fellows: apply through the Airtable form linked from /university/student-fellows. Educators: use 'Access Free Edition' / 'Get free resources' on /university.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual for Free Edition and badges; no expiry stated for the 25% certification discount or Fellows vouchers. No renewal needed.
  - *Gotchas:* This is NOT a platform-credit grant: the education offer is Free Edition access (which locks Lakebase out) + free training + a 25% exam-fee discount, so students get no money-credits and no Lakebase under this program. .edu email is not verified, so anyone can use it; Fellows details (term, counts, countries) live only in the T&C PDF. Free Edition caps: 1 serverless workspace, fair-use daily limits, personal use only.
  - *Source:* https://www.databricks.com/university
- **2025–26 change:** Community Edition sunset/rebrand (2025-2026): Databricks' legacy 'Community Edition' (the free, Spark-learning tier) has been replaced by the expanded 'Free Edition' — 'designed and extended to include full access to the Data + AI Platform'; the Free Edition page states 'Access to legacy CE accounts will continue to be available for the remainder of this year' (page footer © 2026), i.e., legacy CE is being sunset by end of 2026 and users are steered to Free Edition. Startup program now explicitly bundles Neon credits ('up to $200K in credits for Databricks and Neon') on the 2026 page — older program versions referenced Databricks credits alone. Lakebase positioning: no perpetual free Lakebase tier exists; the only free on-ramps are the 14-day trial (Lakebase explicitly listed among Free Edition's locked features) and startup credits. No other Lakebase-specific trial/free-offer changes were visible in 2025-2026 pages; Lakebase docs (last updated Oct 21, 2026) show no GA/preview badge on the core service, with 'store Postgres changes as Delta tables' still marked Public Preview. One caveat: WebSearch was unavailable this run (session budget), so the 2025-2026 change summary relies only on official pages fetched today plus the pages' own footers/dates.

### Fauna Cloud Community

- **Offer (from mapping):** Free shared FaunaDB (document DB): ~1 GB storage, ~1M requests/mo

- **2025–26 change:** No live free offers as of 2026-10-22: the entire Fauna service was sunset. Timeline: Fauna Inc. announced on 2025-03-19 that it would stop accepting new customers and wind down the service; the service ended Friday 2025-05-30 at 12:00pm PT, after which all Fauna accounts and their data were permanently deleted (official EOL FAQ, docs.fauna.com/fauna/current/eol-faq; announcement, fauna.com/blog/the-future-of-fauna). EVERY offer closed: (1) Free/Community plan ("Free Plan — For learning Fauna"): free shared managed DB, ~1 GB storage, ~1M requests/mo, no credit card required — ended with service; (2) Free trials on paid Startup (from $150/mo, US+EU region groups, 4k reads/s, 1k writes/s) and Pro (from $500/mo) tiers — new signups disallowed from 2025-03-19, and per the EOL FAQ existing trial customers could not upgrade to paid accounts; (3) No startup-credit program, trial-credit program, or .edu education license ever existed at Fauna. Continuity: Fauna committed in the same announcement to open-sourcing its core database engine (OSS Fauna + FQL + drivers + CLI), so self-hosted open-source Fauna is the only remaining free path. Live-site verification today: direct fetches of fauna.com fail at the edge (ECONNREFUSED, CloudFront 3.33.139.32) and docs.fauna.com no longer resolves (DNS ENOTFOUND), consistent with company dissolution; search-engine cached copies still show the banner "The Fauna service will be ending on May 30, 2025". The SaaSOffers "Fauna Free Tier" page is stale filler, not an active offer. Caveats: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted before additional independent cross-checks; confidence is HIGH that the service closed on 2025-05-30 and that signups/trial upgrades were prohibited (official announcement + EOL FAQ text read today via search cache), and MEDIUM on the historical free-plan caps (~1 GB / ~1M requests/mo, carried from task context, not re-verified on a live page).
- **Critic correction** (no real free offer: hosted Fauna service ended 2025-05-30; no new accounts; accounts and data deleted): Remove the entry.
