Fauna's end-of-life FAQ says the service was turned off on May 30, 2025, accounts and data were deleted, and no new sign-ups are accepted. Only the open-sourced core remains, which is self-hosted rather than a free hosted plan.

### Firebase Spark

- **Firebase Spark free tier (free-forever managed DB + data platform)** (high) — Firebase Spark is a free-forever, no-payment-method plan. Read today on firebase.google.com/pricing. Per-product no-cost amounts: Cloud Firestore 1 GiB storage, 20K writes/day, 50K reads/day, 20K deletes/day, 10 GiB/month egress; Realtime Database 1 GB stored, 100 simultaneous connections, 10 GB/month downloaded; Firebase Hosting 10 GB storage + 360 MB/day data transfer; Authentication ~50K MAU no-cost (on Blaze); Cloud Functions 2M invocations/mo, 400K GB-seconds/mo, 200K CPU-seconds/mo, 5 GB/mo outbound; Cloud Storage 5 GB stored + 100 GB/mo downloaded (us-central1/west1/east1 for .firebasestorage.app); App Hosting free from 1 Aug 2025 (10 GiB/mo uncached bandwidth, 5 GB storage). Analytics, Crashlytics, App Distribution, Remote Config, App Check, In-App Messaging, Performance Monitoring, FCM, Firebase AI Logic, and Test Lab (10 virtual + 5 physical device tests/day) are no-cost on Spark.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone with a Google account — no .edu email, no startup status, no funding cap, no region restriction stated. No credit card required to stay on Spark.
  - *How to claim:* Create any Firebase project in the console; it is on the Spark plan by default — nothing to enable. To get the paid free-tier extras (Cloud Functions, Cloud Storage, Firestore on Blaze), upgrade to Blaze (a Google billing account is needed). No separate application.
  - *Expiry:* Free forever; limits are perpetual but Google may change them. No renewal needed.
  - *Gotchas:* Quotas apply at the PROJECT level, not per app/resource. The listed Firestore/Functions/Storage free numbers are attached to the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) free-usage pool; on pure Spark some of these (Cloud Functions, Cloud Storage, Phone Number Verification) are marked 'not applicable', so the managed-DB free tier for Firestore/RTDB is the guaranteed free-forever part. Phone Auth is not on Spark. Egress/download and storage beyond free limits bill at GCP rates. Blaze requires a billing account and overages bill automatically.
  - *Source:* https://firebase.google.com/pricing
- **Google Cloud Free Trial — $300 credit (Blaze)** (high) — Google Cloud Free Trial (Blaze) grants $300 of welcome credit valid for 90 days to NEW GCP users. Read today on cloud.google.com/signup-faqs and docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features. During the 90 days no charges; you also get the GCP Free Tier. The account auto-closes on credit exhaustion or 90 days; a 30-day grace period follows before resources are permanently deleted. $300 CANNOT be spent on Gemini API costs in AI Studio, managed 'model-as-a-service' partner models, VM GPUs, Marketplace, Windows-Server VMs, or crypto mining. Multiple people in one org can each get $300 if each is a first-time GCP/Maps/Firebase payer.
  - *Eligibility:* You have NEVER been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and have never signed up for this Free Trial. A credit card / other payment method is REQUIRED at signup (for identity verification; a pending $0–$1 authorization, not a charge). India requires an INR-denominated Cloud billing account first.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com 'Start free trial' with a GCP account; enter a valid credit/debit card (authorization, not a charge). The $300 credit is auto-attached to a new Free Trial billing account; use it on GCP/Firebase over the 90 days. Upgrade to a paid account only if you want to keep resources running and keep the unused credit until it expires.
  - *Expiry:* $300 credit and the trial expire 90 days from signup (whichever comes first: credit used or 90 days). 30-day grace period after closure, then resources deleted.
  - *Gotchas:* This is the key trial-vs-free gotcha: unlike Spark, a credit card IS required here. Free Trial billing is NOT a free-forever plan; it is a time-boxed credit. Remaining credit is lost unless you upgrade to paid within 90 days. Over-free-tier usage beyond the credit bills automatically once you upgrade.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/signup-faqs
- **Google Cloud 'Always Free' Free Tier (20+ products, perpetual)** (medium) — GCP 'Always Free' tier (20+ products, no expiry) that stacks on top of Firebase. Read today via cloud.google.com/free (page quoted in search). Includes Firestore 1 GB storage; Cloud Run / Cloud Run functions 2 million requests or invocations per month; BigQuery 1 TB queries/month + 10 GB storage; Compute Engine 1 e2-micro + 30 GB; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months; Cloud Build 120 min/day; Secret Manager 6 versions; Pub/Sub 10 GB/month; Cloud KMS Autokey; plus AI API free tiers.
  - *Eligibility:* All eligible GCP customers (free tier does not expire and is not charged against the $300 credit). No .edu, no startup status, no CC required for the free tier itself.
  - *How to claim:* Available automatically to any GCP/Firebase customer — no application. Within a paid or trial account, just stay under the monthly free caps; usage is billed only above them.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; free monthly caps are permanent but subject to change.
  - *Gotchas:* These caps do NOT bill against the $300 trial credit; overages bill at pay-as-you-go GCP rates. BigQuery is a sandbox/free-tier only within caps; managed partner AI models and Gemini API are excluded from trial credit. Details here come from an official page surfaced via search (not a direct full-page read today), hence medium confidence.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **SQL Connect (Cloud SQL) 3-month free trial** (high) — SQL Connect (Firebase's managed Cloud SQL access for app data) offers a 3-month no-cost trial for your first Cloud SQL instance: 330 MiB/day egress and ~8,300K ops/day during the trial. After the trial, if the project has not moved to Blaze, the instance is archived and then deleted ~90 days later. On Blaze the ongoing free-usage is 10 GiB/month egress + 250K operations/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Firebase project user; first Cloud SQL instance per project.
  - *How to claim:* Enable Firebase SQL Connect / Cloud SQL in a project on Spark; the first Cloud SQL instance is no-cost for 3 months. To keep it, upgrade to Blaze (and the free-usage pool applies: 10 GiB/month egress, 250K ops/month).
  - *Expiry:* 3-month trial only, then instance archived and deleted 90 days after if not on Blaze. This is NOT free-forever.
  - *Gotchas:* Time-boxed trial — a real data loss risk: the instance is archived after 3 months and hard-deleted after another 90 days if you haven't paid. Upgrading to Blaze requires a billing account.
  - *Source:* https://firebase.google.com/pricing
- **Google Cloud Startup Program / Launch (credit grant for startups)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — The Google Cloud Startup Program (rebranded over time through 'Launch Lab'/'Launch') has historically granted up to ~$200,000 in GCP credits over 12 months, plus additional partner credits, to qualifying startups building on GCP — with Firebase workloads eligible (they bill into the same GCP project). Reported historical eligibility: company founded within ~5 years, total funding raised at or below a cap (~$30M–$100M depending on cycle), headquartered in a participating country, not public. These figures/limits shift per application cycle and I could NOT re-verify them today (page 404s), so treat them as historical estimates, not confirmed current terms.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups (not students): early-stage, unfunded or below a funding cap, within an age window, in an eligible country. Not a .edu program. No credit card needed to receive partner credits once accepted.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through Google's startup portal (historically cloud.google.com/launch / startup.cloud.google.com / 'for-startups'). As of 2026-10-22 all three known URLs returned 404, so confirm the live application URL and current terms before relying on this.
  - *Expiry:* Credits typically valid for the award window (historically ~12 months) and are not auto-renewed; re-application is per cycle.
  - *Gotchas:* UNVERIFIED as of 2026-10-22 — the program pages (cloud.google.com/launch, /for-startups, /programs/startup) all returned 404, indicating the program was renamed/restructured or discontinued at those paths. Do not quote the $200K figure as current without re-confirming the live URL. GCP credits are distinct from the Firebase Spark free tier.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/launch
- **Google Cloud for Education / Classroom credits (for students & educators)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Reported Google Cloud for Education program: an educator credit (historically ~$1,000 of GCP credit, valid ~12 months, renewable with a minimum-activity condition) plus per-student classroom credits when a Google Classroom is linked to the educator's GCP project. Because Firebase bills into the same GCP project, the credit can cover Firebase paid features (and the Spark free tier remains free regardless). I could NOT verify the exact dollar amounts, the .edu requirement, or renewal policy today: the official edu.google.com page is a landing overview that only links to 'Apply for Google Cloud credits', and the deeper edu page fetch was truncated, so these numbers are unconfirmed general-knowledge estimates.
  - *Eligibility:* K-12 / higher-ed educators and their students who use Google Workspace for Education; typically requires an institutional/.edu (or country TLD like .ac.uk) account rather than a personal Gmail. Not a free tier for general students.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the Google Cloud for Education / 'Apply for Google Cloud credits' path (linked from edu.google.com to cloud.google.com/edu), signing in with an institutional Workspace for Education or verified .edu account. Confirm the live page and current figures before relying on this — they were not independently verified today.
  - *Expiry:* Reported ~12-month credit window, renewable if activity is maintained; unconfirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* LOW CONFIDENCE: no dedicated 'Firebase for students' free license was found; the education benefit is a GCP credit for educators, not a per-student Firebase entitlement. Figures here are unverified — re-check the live cloud.google.com/edu page. There is no confirmed .edu-only free tier distinct from the GCP education credit.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/

### Google Cloud

- **Google Cloud Free Trial — $300 welcome credit (90 days)** (high) — New customers get $300 free credit, valid 90 days from Free Trial billing account creation. Credit covers any product including overages beyond Free Tier limits; it does not roll over (expires 90 days from signup even if you upgrade to paid). Free Trial billing account auto-closes when the $300 is spent or 90 days pass unless you manually upgrade to a Paid billing account (then you keep remaining credit until the 90-day mark and keep Free Tier access). Eligibility: you must have NEVER been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and never signed up for the Free Trial before (one per person/billing profile). GPUs, Windows servers and some features are not covered. During trial you are not billed — only if you manually upgrade to paid do charges begin. 30-day data-export window after the trial ends. Compute Engine trial restricted to 8 concurrent cores.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, any country with GCP access (no .edu or startup status needed). Credit card/other payment method REQUIRED at signup for identity verification (Google may run a pending authorization, not a charge). US/Mexico government entities excluded.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com (Get started for free) -> add card -> $300 credit is preloaded into the Free Trial billing account automatically; spend over 90 days on any product incl. Vertex AI/Gemini API; optionally click Upgrade in console to keep credit alive beyond trial features. Do NOT click 'Activate a paid account' unless you want charges.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from signup or until $300 is exhausted, whichever first; unused credit does not survive account closure. Free Tier usage continues if you convert to a paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Free Tier: the $300 is for trial billing only; Free Tier allowances are separate, not charged against it. CC required but you are only charged if you manually upgrade to paid. Egress, third-party models (e.g. non-Google LLMs on Vertex) cost above free allowances. 30-day migration window after end; GCE resources may be deleted and are not recoverable.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google Cloud Free Tier — 20+ always-free products (no expiry)** (high) — Per-product monthly free allowances, reset each month, do NOT expire but limits are 'subject to change'. Verified examples from cloud.google.com/free: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro/month; Cloud Storage 5 GB Standard; BigQuery 1 TB scanned/month; Cloud Run 2M requests + Cloud Run Functions 2M invocations/month; GKE 1 Autopilot/zonal cluster; Pub/Sub 10 GB messages; Vision AI 1,000 units; Speech-to-Text 60 min; Natural Language 5,000 units; Cloud Build 120 build-minutes/day; KMS Autokey 100 active key versions + 10k ops; Artifact Registry, Firestore 1 GB, Secret Manager 6 versions, Cloud Shell 5 GB. Includes 'AI APIs' free usage — the GCP-hosted AI endpoints (Vertex AI GenAI preview limits apply separately).
  - *Eligibility:* All new GCP users (not student-only). A Google Cloud billing account with a registered payment method is required even to use the Free Tier ('eligible customers' in a paid or trial billing account).
  - *How to claim:* Any GCP account (trial or paid) automatically gets the free monthly allowances; no separate claim. Sign up at cloud.google.com with a card.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry (renews monthly), but usage resets on the 1st; unused allowance does not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Overspending beyond free limits is billed pay-as-you-go to your card. Free Tier products are region/feature-specific (e.g., e2-micro, us-central1 historically); GPU workloads are not in the free tier. Third-party LLMs on Vertex AI are billed. 'Not charged against $300 credit' means free-tier usage is on top of trial credit.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Gemini API / AI Studio Free tier — free LLM access, no credit card** (medium) — The Gemini Developer API has a Free tier: free input/output tokens with limited access to certain models, plus free Google AI Studio usage; no billing account or card required (Free tier qualification = 'Active project or free trial'). Per the current official docs, exact RPM/TPM/RPD are now model-specific and only viewable in aistudio.google.com/rate-limit (docs stopped publishing the static table). As of mid-2025 community-referenced Free-tier limits were ~Gemini 2.5 Pro 5 RPM / 250K TPM / 100 RPD, 2.5 Flash 10/250K/250, 2.5 Flash-Lite 15/250K/1,000. Pay tiers: Tier 1 needs a linked billing account (cap $250/min-rolling), Tier 2 after $100 spent + 3 days ($2,000 cap), Tier 3 after $1,000 + 30 days ($20k-100k cap). Upgrading to paid now requires prepaying min $5. Flagship 'Gemini 4 Argon' is NOT available on the Free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Google account; no card, no .edu. Region: available in most countries; Google AI Studio usage is free in all available countries.
  - *How to claim:* Go to aistudio.google.com -> create project -> generate an API key -> call Gemini models within Free-tier limits. For deploying up to 2 full-stack apps without GCP billing, use the Google Cloud 'Starter Tier' (AI Studio -> Deploy, eligible accounts only).
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while the Free tier exists (no renewal); limits subject to change; RPD resets midnight Pacific.
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier content/prompts may be used by Google to improve its products (Paid tier does not); newest flagship models excluded from Free tier; limits are per-project not per-key; from June 19, 2026 the Gemini API stops accepting requests from unrestricted API keys (must restrict keys to the Gemini API endpoint); '429' when hitting limits; AI Studio playground daily caps have been tightened in 2026 (community reports ~10-30 req/day for top models under capacity pressure).
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier: up to $2,000 credits / 1 year (self-serve)** (high) — For digital-native tech startups with a working MVP planning to seek venture funding. Benefits verified from the official benefits page: up to $2,000 USD GCP credits valid for 1 year; $200 Google Skills training credits; Google Workspace Business Plus free 12 months (new signups); access to partner Startup Perks (e.g. Datadog, ElevenLabs 33M credits, Render $500, GitLab Ultimate 20 licenses) — the ones starred are Scale-only.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; not yet received Google Cloud credits (beyond the free trial); tech startup that plans to raise VC funding; clear product roadmap. No funding cap (pre-funding/bootstrapped is fine). Decision at Google's discretion. Company email matching public domain expected; GCP account + billing ID needed.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the Google for Startups Cloud Program application form at cloud.google.com/startup (self-serve 'Apply now') — or via the Startups Perks/partner pages. Complete the onboarding checklist at cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding (billing account setup) so credits attach to the correct billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 1 year from issuance. Program itself is ongoing (self-serve available).
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a GCP billing account (payment method on file; credits offset charges, card isn't used if spend <= credit). Third-party (non-Google) models on Vertex are billed directly, not covered. 'Beyond the free trial' means the $300 trial doesn't disqualify you.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier: up to $200,000 credits over 2 years ($350,000 for AI-first startups)** (high) — Year 1: 100% of GCP spend up to $100,000 in credits (AI-first startups: up to $250,000). Year 2: 20% of GCP spend up to an additional $100,000 (both tracks). Total: up to $200,000, or up to $350,000 for AI-first startups using/planning to use Gemini as a foundation of their product. Credits cover Google models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed separately. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits (1 year, 1-hr critical response), $500 Google Skills credits, $600/month Google Maps Platform usage credits (separate application), Workspace Business Plus free 12 months, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Customer Engineers, and starred Scale-only partner perks (ElevenLabs 33M, Datadog up to $100k, OpenSearch $100k+30k, Render up to $100k, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* VC-funded (equity funding Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years; Series A within last 12 months); founded within the last 5 years; not yet received more than $5,000 in GCP credits; stealth mode OK with funding verification. AI track additionally requires Gemini (or planned Gemini use) as foundation of primary product. No explicit revenue cap; decision at Google's discretion.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup ('Apply now') or apply through a Google Cloud partner (VCs/accelerators: Y Combinator — dedicated GPU/TPU access + Scale-tier benefits for AI cohorts; 500 Startups, AI2, ERA, StartX, Berkeley Skydeck). Approval is reviewed; YC and similar partners get high acceptance rates.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured as Year-1 match + Year-2 20% top-up, 2-year program window; no renewal beyond that (case-by-case requests exist via partners).
  - *Gotchas:* To hit the $100k Year-2 top-up you must actually spend $500k in GCP in Year 2 — it's a match, not a grant. Year-1 '100%' also means real spend must occur. Third-party LLMs excluded from credit coverage. GPU/TPU access in partner cohorts may be ad-hoc scheduling, not reserved capacity. Maps and some perks need separate applications.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai
- **Google Cloud for Education — student & faculty course credits (no credit card)** (medium) — Faculty at regionally-accredited US institutions (or degree-awarding universities in eligible countries; for-profit institutions excluded) apply via a form and receive GCP credit coupons: faculty get their own, and a URL is emailed that students open to verify student status (school/college email confirmation) and receive coupon codes. Credits land in per-course Cloud Billing accounts. Official help docs state the program 'does not require a credit card to sign up'. Credits valid for 12 months after the course start date / 12 months after redemption; Free Tier usage does not count against them; GCP credits may not be used for crypto mining; GPU access must be requested. Separate 'training credits': current students can request 200 free Google Skills credits (online labs, courses, badges; credits expire 1 year after issue; request processing up to 2 weeks).
  - *Eligibility:* .edu / school email of an eligible institution (regionally accredited in US; degree-granting in eligible countries abroad). Students get credits only through a course whose faculty applied. No funding or startup criteria. Amount per course varies (historically ~$50+ per course; Google awards at its discretion).
  - *How to claim:* Faculty: apply via the Google Cloud for Education form (cloud.google.com/edu). Students: use the invitation URL your faculty sends -> confirm school email -> redeem coupon code in the Google Cloud console (Billing -> Coupons). For Google Skills: request the 200 training credits via the training-credits form linked from cloud.google.com/edu/students.
  - *Expiry:* GCP course credits: valid 12 months from course start/redemption; per-course billing accounts. Google Skills credits: expire 1 year after issued.
  - *Gotchas:* Student credits are tied to faculty applications — there is no longer a self-serve $300/12-month 'student credit' grant on the official pages (the older student program appears sunset; see sunset notes). Credits are non-commercial, for learning. GPU/CPU/project quotas capped; requests may be denied. School email must be verifiable; .edu generators violate ToS.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10324788
- **Google Cloud Free Trial / program status as of 2026-10-22** (high) — No sunset: the $300/90-day Free Trial and the Free Tier remain current as of this date (pages updated 2025-2026, terms archived 2025-01-30). No new standalone free LLM 'trial credit' has been announced in 2025-2026; the AI offer is the Gemini API Free tier (no card) plus free-tier products. Startup program remains two tiers (Start/Scale) with the 2024 restructured amounts. Gemini API added spend-based tier caps and (in 2026) mandatory API-key restriction and Prepay min $5 for paid tiers.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a (status note)
  - *How to claim:* n/a
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Treat static free-tier RPM/RPD numbers as volatile — Google moved them to a per-account dashboard in 2026.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/archive/terms/free-trial-20250130
- **Google Cloud Free Tier (always-free monthly quotas, 20+ products)** (high) — Free-forever monthly usage on select GCP products, e.g.: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro VM/month (us-west1/us-central1/us-east1; time-based across those regions) + 30GB standard persistent disk + 1GB egress; Cloud Storage 5GB US regional storage, 5,000 Class A / 50,000 Class B ops, 100GB NA egress; Cloud Run 2M requests, 360,000 GB-s memory & 180,000 vCPU-s, 1GB NA egress; Cloud Run functions 2M invocations; BigQuery 1TiB queries + 10GiB storage/month; App Engine Standard 28 F1-h/day + 9 B1-h/day + 1GB egress/day; Firestore 1GiB, 50k reads/20k writes/20k deletes per day; GKE 1 cluster charge/month; GCS egress and other egress outside NA or beyond limits bill at standard rates. GPUs/TPUs never free.
  - *Eligibility:* Requires an active GCP billing account (Paid or Free Trial) in good standing; no negotiated pricing contract. No .edu or age requirement; effectively free tier is available to any GCP account holder. Free-tier benefits on Cloud Storage apply only to us-east1/us-west1/us-central1.
  - *How to claim:* Create any GCP billing account (usually via the $300 Free Trial signup, which requires a card); stay within per-product monthly quotas and you are never billed. Do not create paid resources beyond quotas, or overage bills on your card.
  - *Expiry:* No end date, but Google reserves the right to change or eliminate limits with 30 days' advance notice; quotas do not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Common trap: creating a GCP account for 'free tier' still typically needs a card (Free Trial signup requires one); egress, cross-region traffic, GPU/TPU, and overage are billed; limits are per billing account.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier ($2,000 GCP credits)** (high) — For 'digital-native' startups with a working MVP and plans to seek venture funding soon: $2,000 in GCP credits, valid 12 months (applies to GCP services and select GCP offerings; covers Google's proprietary models — program credits do NOT cover third-party models, which are billed directly). Also included: free-tier AI Studio (Generative AI Studio) access, 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (new signups), $200 Google Skills training credit, and 'Startup Perks' (partner discounts/offers).
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; has NOT yet received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial; digital-native tech startup with working MVP + clear business model + plans to seek VC funding soon. Excluded: service agencies, consultancies, traditional small businesses; companies that have IPO'd or been acquired; educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content companies, dev shops, crypto mining companies, token distributors contrary to regulatory guidance. Acceptance at Google Cloud's discretion. No VC funding required at this tier (it's the pre-funded stage).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed). You must sign in with a Google account whose business email matches your public website domain, and provide your 18-character GCP billing account ID (create a GCP billing account if you don't have one); Google reviews (most decisions within a few business days); credits are deposited to your billing account.
  - *Expiry:* GCP credits: 12 months from issuance. Workspace Business Plus: 12 months. Google Skills $200 credit: training credits; expiration not stated on the Start page (student program credits expire 1 year after issuance — treat similarly). No renewal; re-applications case-by-case via cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Gotchas:* Start-tier page does not state a credit-card requirement, but you need an existing GCP billing account to receive credits, and creating a new GCP account/payment setup typically requires a payment method (Free Trial signup definitely does, per docs). Workspace benefit: your company domain must not have been on a paid Google Workspace plan within 31 days of applying, and the benefit cannot be applied to annual Workspace plans. Credits excluded from Marketplace/third-party products; egress/networking costs beyond free tiers still billable.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier (up to $200,000 GCP credits over 2 years; $350,000 for AI-first)** (high) — VC-funded early-stage startups get up to $200,000 in GCP credits over two years: Year 1 = 100% credit up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% credit on year-2 spend, up to an additional $100,000. AI-first startups: Year 1 = 100% up to $250,000, Year 2 = 20% up to +$100,000 → up to $350,000 total (equivalently, 'qualifying venture-backed startups can tap an additional $150,000 with the AI startup program' on top of the $200K base). Program credits cover Google's models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed directly and not covered. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits for one year (1-hour critical response), 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free, $600/month Google Maps Platform credits for one year (separate application), $500 Google Skills training credits, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Startup Customer Engineers, Web3 program access, and partner perks.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have received 'recent startup equity funding' (pre-seed to Series A by an institutional investor/VC, or common Web3 funding sources — SAFE agreements and verifiable token raises/blockchain foundation grants count; definition: Pre-Seed/Seed rounds within the last 5 years, Series A rounds within the last 12 months). Founded within the last 5 years (stealth mode OK with investor-verified funding). Must not have received more than $5,000 in GCP credits already. Not eligible: IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, crypto-mining companies, token distributors against regulatory guidance; private-equity, government-innovation-grant, crowdfunding, angel and friends-&-family funding do NOT qualify for Scale tier (may be evaluated for Start tier instead).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a). Sign in with the company business email matching your public website domain + provide the 18-character GCP billing account ID + verification of funding (public sources; stealth: work with your investor). Decision usually within a few business days (manual review may take longer); credits deposit to your billing account and you receive an in-console benefit tracker.
  - *Expiry:* Year-1 credits usable in year 1; year-2 credits (20% of spend, capped +$100K) accrue in year 2 → program spans 2 years. Workspace 12 months; Maps $600/month for 1 year (separate app); Enhanced Support credits valid 1 year.
  - *Gotchas:* Year-2 benefit is a 20% discount-style credit on actual year-2 spend (max $100K) — it is not a pre-funded $100K; you must keep spending. Credits exclude Marketplace third-party offerings, third-party AI models, and per footnote only 'GCP Services and Select GCP offerings'. Workspace 31-day paid-plan rule applies. Google 'unable to accept' a long exclusion list (see eligibility); acceptance at Google's discretion. AI tier requires being genuinely 'AI-first' (AI in foundational structure/strategy, not a bolt-on) — some applicants get rejected even if they say AI. Data/egress: credits offset compute/AI usage, but egress and overage still bill if credits exhaust.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a
- **Startup program add-ons announced 2025–2026: 'New: AI benefits' ($350K ceiling), Web3 benefits, ISV Startup Springboard, Gemini Startup Forum** (medium) — The benefits page now labels 'New: AI benefits' (AI webinars/content, dedicated AI training, resources, experts, access to up to $350,000 in credits) and 'New: Web3 benefits' (Web3 Perks: partner offers from Quicknode, TRM, Allium and more). FAQ: Web3 startups get 'up to $200,000 USD over two years for Google Cloud and Firebase usage' plus Discord channel, foundation grants, VIP event access. Scale-tier page adds ISV Startup Springboard (12-week program for emerging AI startups: bespoke technical guidance + GTM resources) and the Gemini Startup Forum (Google for Startups + DeepMind + Cloud; curated in-person gatherings for seed–Series A founders). Benefits page also lists a 'Future of AI: Perspectives on generative media for startups' report and Cloud Startup Customer Engineers with faster response times for critical issues.
  - *Eligibility:* AI tier: Scale-tier VC-backed startups that are AI-first (AI foundational to product/strategy). Web3: startups with verifiable token raises / blockchain foundation grants (SAFE also eligible). ISV Springboard: emerging AI startups in the program. Forum: seed-to-Series A founders (invitation/curated).
  - *How to claim:* No separate application — benefits come with Scale-tier approval; Web3 program has its own qualification via the same application (flag yourself as Web3); Springboard/Forum through program onboarding. Re-applications or questions: cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* AI-credits structure (Y1 $250K 100% + Y2 20% up to $100K) spans 2 years; Web3 $200K over 2 years; Springboard is a 12-week cohort.
  - *Gotchas:* Web3 'up to $200,000' covers Google Cloud AND Firebase (distinct from the $350K AI cloud-credits ceiling — these are different track framings). Third-party models still billed direct. AI-first qualification is subjective and at Google's discretion; being an 'AI company' in name isn't enough (FAQ example: another AI startup founder did not qualify).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/faq
- **Google Cloud for Education — faculty teaching credits (up to $100 per teaching staff + up to $50 per student)** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for GCP credits for classroom use: up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff and up to $50 in GCP credits per student, so students get hands-on cloud experience at no cost. Application is limited to faculty (students cannot apply for their own teaching credits; they can encourage faculty to apply for their next course). Course start date must be no more than 1 year out; approved credits are redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date, and are good for up to 1 year after course start. Credits attach to specified school-issued email domains and are non-transferable. Faculty Community access is restricted to verified/approved faculty. Also linked: Career Launchpad program (coach students through Google Cloud certificates/credentials) and Google DeepMind AI Research Foundations curriculum.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only, at an academic institution; requires official school email with a valid .edu (or equivalent institutional) domain ('Please use your official school email address with a valid edu domain'; if none, you may nominate another address, credits attach to it and are non-transferable); must provide faculty directory profile link. Country restriction: the teaching program is 'currently only available in' a listed set of ~60 countries (Argentina, Australia, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Nigeria, UK, US, etc. — the country dropdown on the form; if your country is absent you can only 'express interest').
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty → 'Apply for Cloud credits'). Fill personal info with school .edu email, country, institution, faculty directory link, course details (name, start date, abstract, course URL) and member info (all school-issued domains, expected students/TA-staff count). Check spam for CloudEduGrants@google.com confirmations; redeem credits via the education-credits redemption guide once approved.
  - *Expiry:* Credits good for up to 1 year after course start; redeemable window = approval through 16 weeks after course start; course start must be within 1 year of application.
  - *Gotchas:* Not open to students directly (students must go through faculty). .edu/institutional email effectively required (nomination of alternate email loses the .edu gate but credits stay non-transferable). Country list is restrictive — not all countries served. Small amounts ($100/$50) — intended for learning, not production. Students should avoid accidentally signing up for the GCP Free Trial with personal cards in classroom contexts (Google's own faculty guide warns of this).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for Education — student training credits ($200 Google Skills credits) and free labs/badges** (high) — Students can request $200 in Google Skills (formerly Cloud Skills Boost) training credits, which expire one year after issuance, plus free access to online hands-on labs, skill badges, and courses; Google Skills Arcade (gamified challenges with badges) is free. The student page also points to DSC (Developer Student Clubs) and the Career Launchpad pathway. This is training credit, not GCP usage credit — students do not get a separate GCP-usage student credit program (the individual $300 Free Trial is the only personal usage credit, and it requires a card).
  - *Eligibility:* Students (individuals); request via the student training-credits form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/). No .edu gate stated on the page, but it's a student-facing learning program; free labs/courses also available without the credit.
  - *How to claim:* On cloud.google.com/edu/students click 'Request credits' → complete the Google Skills Boost student training-credits application form; $200 credits are issued to your account and usable on Google Skills courses/badges within 1 year.
  - *Expiry:* $200 Google Skills credits expire one year after issuance; free labs/Arcade have no stated end.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with GCP usage credits — this buys courses/badges, not compute. A separate 'apply for free Google Cloud credits' line on the /edu hub for students appears to funnel to the same faculty/teaching-credit program (per the hub, students get free credits via the higher-ed program, i.e., via faculty).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud for Education — research credits ('Academic Research Grants', up to $5,000)** (high) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in GCP credits to advance research (computing power; GCP tools; proposal-based). Eligible roles: Faculty, Postdoctoral Researcher, PhD Student (and IT Admin option on the form), from accredited higher-education institutions or eligible nonprofit research institutions. Application requires: email (institution account preferred), country (restricted list of ~60 countries — program 'currently only available in' those countries), job title, organization (select from institution list), researcher/faculty directory link, project name, start date, a ≤250-word proposal (research problem, GCP tools, timeline, milestones, how GCP supports future work; Google Doc link allowed), field of research, intended use (proof of concept / evaluation-comparison / workshop-training / open-source development), optional Google Scholar link.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty / PhD students / postdocs at accredited higher-ed or eligible nonprofit research institutions in the listed approved countries; institutional email preferred; faculty directory link required. Related institutional program: GPAR (Google Public Sector — Program for Accelerated Research) with co-investment, industry-leading pricing for AI accelerators (GPUs/TPUs), early access to TPUs/foundation models/datasets (AlphaFold, Earth AI), agentic AI (Co-scientist, AlphaEvolve), compliance (HIPAA, CMMC 2.0, FedRAMP), on-campus research days — GPAR is currently US-only.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'). Submit the Academic Research Grant form with the fields above; credits are awarded to the institutional account and redeemable per the education-credits redemption guide.
  - *Expiry:* The public pages do not state a redemption window for research credits (teaching credits: 1 year post course-start; assume similar ~1-year usage window until confirmed in the FAQ); GPAR is a standing institutional program (US only).
  - *Gotchas:* Country-restricted list; institution must appear in the form's institution list ('begin typing and select'). Amount is 'up to $5,000' — awards may be less and are grant-reviewed. GPAR co-investment benefits are US-only. Institutional (not individual student) gate: undergraduates are not listed roles.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/
- **Product-specific free trials for GCP databases/BI (no GCP-usage credit needed)** (high) — Separate short trials available on a GCP billing account: Spanner 90-day free trial instance (10 vCPUs? listed as '10 GB of storage' in docs: '10 vCPUs...' — docs say 90-day Spanner instance with 10GB storage at no cost); AlloyDB for PostgreSQL 30-day trial cluster (8 vCPU basic + 1TB storage capacity); Looker 30-day trial (all standard features); Cloud SQL 30-day trial. These require a billing account but cost nothing for the trial window.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GCP billing account (Free Trial or Paid); one instance per product per the trial terms.
  - *How to claim:* In GCP console, provision the trial instance (Spanner/AlloyDB/Looker/Cloud SQL trial buttons in each product's UI) on a billing account; delete before trial end to avoid charges.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days (Spanner) / 30 days (AlloyDB, Looker, Cloud SQL); auto-conversion to billable instance afterward.
  - *Gotchas:* These are time-boxed, not free-forever; after trial end instances bill at standard rates unless deleted; GCP docs also point to Google Maps Platform free usage caps, Gemini AI Studio free tier, and Firebase no-cost plans as adjacent free offers.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google Cloud free trial: $300 credit for new customers** (high) — New GCP customers get $300 in free credit to try products (incl. Cloud SQL, BigQuery, AlloyDB, Spanner) and build a proof of concept. Per the live /free page: 'You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account.' The $300 is not charged against the always-free allotments. No day limit is printed on the current page (historically a 60-day trial; verify in console at claim). Credits apply to PayG Google-owned 1P GCP services; excludes Marketplace/third-party products.
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers (first-time). Credit card / payment method IS required to hold a billing account - the faculty education page explicitly warns students that the Free Trial 'requires them to use a credit card.' No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com/free or console (billing free-trial flow), accept terms with a payment method; $300 credit attaches to the new billing account on signup. Product trials (Cloud SQL/Spanner/AlloyDB/Bigtable/Looker/GKE) are claimed from the 'Explore more offers' section of the same /free page.
  - *Expiry:* No duration published on today's page; historical term was 60 days. Unused credit lapses when the trial term ends; account converts to pay-as-you-go only on activation of the full paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs free-tier confusion: $300 trial credit is finite; the 'Always Free' caps persist after the trial. Credit card required - not a no-card offer. Internet egress and any usage beyond free caps/credit is billed. Trial is one per person; students should prefer education credits (no card) over the trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **GCP Always Free tier (ongoing, ~24 products incl. BigQuery 1TB queries/month)** (high) — Live /free page lists: BigQuery 1 TB queries/month; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard; GKE 1 Autopilot or Zonal cluster/month; Cloud Run functions 2M invocations/month; Firestore 1 GB storage; Pub/Sub 10 GB messages/month; Compute Engine 1 e2-micro instance/month; Datastream up to 100 GiB CDC from eligible first-party sources/month; Agent Engine first 180,000 vCPU-seconds + 360,000 GiB-seconds/month; Application Integration up to 400 executions + 20 GiB data/month; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB/month; Cloud Shell 5 GB disk; Secret Manager 6 secret versions/month; KMS Autokey 100 key versions + 10,000 ops/month; plus Vision/NLP/STT/Video quotas and Workflows/Cloud Build allotments. Page: 'There is no charge to use these products up to their specified free usage limit. The free usage limit does not expire, but is subject to change.'
  - *Eligibility:* All GCP customers, including students and startups, every month. No .edu email, no funding cap. A billing account (with payment method) still required to open a GCP project; $0 billed while inside caps.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim - free allotments are automatic for any new GCP billing account/project. Set budgets/alerts in Cloud Billing to cap overage.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual, resets monthly. No end date, but Google may change or remove allotments without notice ('subject to change').
  - *Gotchas:* SHRUNKEN free tier: legacy Cloud SQL micro-instance free hours, Memorystore free hours, Spanner free 20 vCPU-hr/32GB, and Dataflow 9,600 worker-hours are no longer listed - managed-DB/data-platform users should plan on the finite product trials + $300 instead. BigQuery's 10 GB free storage is no longer listed. Cloud Storage free space is 5 GB-months (vs the historical 50 GB). Overage beyond caps bills at standard rates. Egress to internet is not a free allotment.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program - 'Start' tier (up to $2,000 credits)** (high) — First rung of the revamped program. FAQ on the live page: 'Start ($2k) tier is designed for startups founded in the past 2 years, with a working MVP, a clear business model, and plans to seek venture funding soon.' The prototyping stage also includes instant access to Google AI Studio for rapid MVP prototyping. Adjacent benefits on the page: Gemini Startup Forum membership and equity-free accelerator cohorts.
  - *Eligibility:* Unfunded/pre-VC: founded within past 2 years, working MVP, clear business model, plans to seek VC; new Google Cloud customers founding digital-native products. Must be in a region where GCP is GA with self-service purchasing. Excludes IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government, nonprofits, personal blogs, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, crypto mining, token distribution contrary to regulation.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (Google sign-in). Prepare the 18-character GCP billing account ID (credits are deposited there) and a business email that owns/admins the GCP account. Stealth founders get in via investor/VC/accelerator referral. Automated responses: rejection in 5-7 days; 10+ days if information is missing.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable for the program's first-year window (per footnotes, Year-1 coverage rules apply; funded tiers run two years). Re-application is allowed if prior credits were unused and in-year eligibility still met.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are non-transferable, cannot be shared/split/resold (violation = termination); cover only first-party PayG GCP services - subscription/prepay/annual-license billing and all Marketplace/Model Garden third-party products excluded; third-party models billed directly; acceptance at Google's sole discretion based on company age, stage, prior GCP credit usage, and funding type.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program - 'Scale'/'Scale AI' funded tiers (up to $200K-$350K over two years)** (high) — Headline: up to $350,000 in GCP credits over two years (AI startups), up to $200,000 (other startups), i.e. the program's 'Funded tiers (Scale, and Scale AI) range from $200K-$350k in credits'. Footnote math: Scale AI - Year 1: 100% of eligible spend up to $250,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000. Other startups - Year 1: up to $100,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000. Stated benefits: credits for frontier models (Gemini/Gemma), Google Skills training, 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost, monthly Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), Startup Success Managers + Customer Engineering support, and Cloud Marketplace listings to sell alongside Google sales. Eligibility: verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding, founded within the last 3-5 years (tier-dependent).
  - *Eligibility:* Funded early-stage: verifiable Pre-Seed through Series A; company age 3-5 years depending on tier; company must be in a GCP GA region (self-service purchasing); exclusions same as Start tier (no IPO/acquired, no edu/gov/nonprofit/agency, no crypto mining, AUP-compliant). Funding is the gating factor - this is where the 'funding cap' lives: Pre-Seed to Series A, no Series B+ shown on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (Google sign-in) via the 'Grow and scale / Up to $350k in Cloud credits - Apply to the program' flow; submit billing account ID + business email; list VC/investor/accelerator affiliations to expedite. Credits are deposited to the selected billing account on approval.
  - *Expiry:* Two-year coverage structure: Year 1 full (100% up to cap), Year 2 at 20% up to an additional $100K; end-of-term lapses for unused credits. Prior participants who left credits unused may re-apply if still meeting in-year eligibility.
  - *Gotchas:* Only PayG first-party GCP products - subscription-billed items and Marketplace/Model Garden third-party products excluded; third-party models billed directly and not covered; credits can't be shared/resold; Workspace benefit requires the company domain to have had no paid Workspace subscription in the prior 31 days; Maps Platform is a separate application; 'up to' amounts are coverage rules, not guaranteed grants (Year 2 = 20% of spend, not a lump sum).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Managed-database & data-platform free trials (Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Bigtable, Looker, GKE, Gemini Enterprise Plus)** (high) — Finite, no-cost product trials listed under 'Explore more offers' on the live /free page: Cloud SQL - 30-day trial, 8 vCPU Enterprise Plus instance, 64 GB memory, 100 GB storage; Cloud Spanner - 90-day trial instance, 10 GB storage; AlloyDB for PostgreSQL - 30-day trial, 8 vCPU basic primary instance, up to 1 TB storage capacity; Bigtable - 10-day trial, 1-node SSD cluster, up to 500 GB storage; Looker - 30-day trial with all standard features; GKE - 90 days with full capabilities; Gemini Enterprise Plus edition - 30 days. Each page notes 'new Google Cloud customers get $300 in free credits on signup' alongside.
  - *Eligibility:* New GCP customers (trials are tied to new-account offer; existing customers may still trial but the '$300 on signup' line applies to new customers). No .edu requirement; payment method on the account per GCP billing policy.
  - *How to claim:* Claim from https://cloud.google.com/free ('OFFER' cards, 'Start a ... free trial' buttons) or the per-product docs 'free trial' links; create the trial instance in a GCP project under a billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Fixed durations: 30 days (Cloud SQL, AlloyDB, Looker, Gemini Enterprise Plus), 90 days (Spanner, GKE), 10 days (Bigtable). After expiry the instance converts to standard PayG pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* During the trial period usage beyond the trial spec (e.g., more storage, extra instances) bills; after expiry everything bills. GCP's managed-DB trial instances skew toward the premium/Enterprise Plus editions - budget accordingly. No free-tier replacement for Memorystore appears on the page. Egress/network costs are separate line items.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google Cloud for faculty / classroom - teaching credits (up to $100 per faculty + up to $50 per student)** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in GCP credits per student for hands-on classroom use. Credits attach to the applicant's email and are non-transferable; group projects may pool students' individual credits (documented in faculty resources). Country-restricted: the application shows a fixed country list (US, Australia, Brazil, Canada, ... not worldwide).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty/teaching staff at accredited educational institutions; official school-issued email on a valid .edu (or equivalent national) domain required - if you lack one you can nominate another email, but credits attach to it permanently. Course start date must be within 1 year of application.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty 'Apply for teaching credits'): enter course details (up to 3 courses), expected student count, school domains, and how credits will be used. Then redeem in GCP and manage billing per the 'Manage Google Cloud credits' guide.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are redeemable from approval up until 16 weeks after the course start date, and are good for up to 1 year after the course start date.
  - *Gotchas:* Not available in every country. Credits are per-student capped ($50) - large cohorts need many enrollments or instructor credits only. Explicitly do not have students use the $300 Free Trial (credit-card required) instead of these credits. Credits are non-transferable and locked to the school domains listed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for researchers - research credits (up to $5,000) + GPAR program** (high) — Faculty/researchers (incl. PhD students) can apply for up to $5,000 in GCP research credits via the Academic Research Grants form ('Google Cloud for Researchers'). Applications specify intended use (proof of concept, evaluation/comparison, workshop/training, open-source development) and external funding status. The Google Public Sector 'Program for Accelerated Research' (GPAR) layers on top: co-investment pricing for GPUs/TPUs, early access to new accelerators, foundational models/datasets (e.g., AlphaFold, Earth AI), agentic AI (Co-scientist, AlphaEvolve), generative-AI grant tooling, and campus research days.
  - *Eligibility:* Researchers/faculty at accredited academic institutions, including PhD students acting on behalf of the institution; .edu/institutional email; some country restrictions on the credits form; credits may only be used on behalf of the educational entity for the described project, not personal use, and not for commercial purposes.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'): project abstract, start date, use of credits, post-credit funding plan; create a GCP billing account to hold the coupon. GPAR/campus research day via the express-interest form (edu.google.com/programs/express-interest).
  - *Expiry:* Credit coupon is valid for 60 days from the stated project start date; the credit itself expires 365 days from the coupon redemption date or when the amount is fully used, whichever comes first. Credits can be issued in increments as you use them.
  - *Gotchas:* Academic, non-commercial use only - cannot be resold, bartered or transferred. If you miss the 60-day redemption window the coupon lapses. 'Up to $5,000' is a per-application maximum; approval amount is at Google's discretion. Students who are not at an institution do not qualify - use the student offer below.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **Google Cloud for students - free learning + 200 free Google Skills credits** (high) — 'Google Cloud for students': free self-paced learning, Google Cloud Certificates and skill badges, hands-on Google Skills labs (formerly Cloud Skills Boost/Qwiklabs) and the gamified Google Skills Arcade, plus Google Developer Student Club communities. Students can request 200 free Google Skills credits (redeemable toward lab/training use); exam previews are free on YouTube. No GCP dollar-credit pool and no managed-DB free tier is granted directly to students - their hands-on GCP usage comes via faculty teaching credits, the $300 trial (card required), or the always-free caps.
  - *Eligibility:* Students at participating/any accredited institution, verified via school email where the request flow asks for it; Google account required. No credit card needed for the learning program or Skills credits. No funding/stage criteria.
  - *How to claim:* Go to cloud.google.com/edu/students -> 'Get started' (Google Cloud for students) and 'Request credits' for the 200 Google Skills credits; join a Google Developer Student Club chapter via the same page.
  - *Expiry:* Google Skills credits expire one year after they are issued. Learning/arcade access is tied to active student status.
  - *Gotchas:* These are training/lab credits, NOT $200 of GCP service credit - don't budget them for Cloud SQL/BigQuery instances. If students need GCP compute, route through faculty credits ($100/$50) or the card-required $300 trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 credit, 90 days** (high) — New GCP customers get $300 in free 'Welcome credit' valid for 90 days from signup (NOT 60 days as previously believed; official docs and the Jan-30-2025 archived T&Cs both say 90 days/3 months). Credit covers 200+ products (incl. Cloud SQL, BigQuery, Spanner) including usage beyond free-tier limits; Free Tier usage does not draw down the $300. No billing unless you manually upgrade to a Paid account. On upgrade mid-trial, remaining credit still expires 90 days from signup. No SLA during trial; restrictions: no GPUs on VMs, no GCP Marketplace, no quota increases, no Windows Server VMs, no Cloud VMware Engine, no crypto mining; the $300 cannot pay Gemini API in AI Studio costs or managed partner models (MaaS). Trial account ends at $300 spent or 90 days, then a 30-day grace period — resources stop, then are permanently deleted if not upgraded.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have never been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and never signed up for the Free Trial (one per person/identity). A valid credit card or other payment method IS required at signup (identity verification; a $0.00–$1.00 authorization hold may appear, it is not a charge). Bank-account verification needed in some countries. US/Mexico government entities excluded. No .edu requirement; no startup-only gating.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/free → 'Get started for free' / console signup; 2) create a Google account; 3) complete Free Trial signup: name, address, payment method; 4) Google verifies identity/payment, trial billing account 'My Billing Account' + 'My First Project' are created, $300 preloaded; 5) use products for 90 days; optionally click 'Activate' to upgrade to a paid account before the trial closes to keep resources.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from signup or when $300 is exhausted (earlier); then 30-day grace period, after which trial resources are deleted. Free Tier (below) continues indefinitely after.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card/payment method required even though you aren't billed; resources are permanently deleted after the 30-day post-trial grace period; GCE limited to 8 concurrent cores during trial; 'eligible customers' free-tier availability can vary by country (e.g., India trial needs an INR billing account; Maps Platform not available on INR accounts); free limits are 'subject to change'.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features(seealsohttps://cloud.google.com/freeandhttps://cloud.google.com/signup-faqs)
- **Cloud SQL Free Trial Instance (30 days, Enterprise Plus)** (high) — Official free Cloud SQL offer today: a 30-day free trial instance at no cost — Cloud SQL Enterprise Plus edition, N2 machine series, 8 vCPUs, 64 GB memory, 100 GB storage. Covers MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. In addition to the $300/90-day GCP free-trial credit (new customers). Upgrade to a paid instance anytime during the 30-day trial or the following 90-day grace period; if not upgraded the instance is stopped. Deleting the trial instance makes the trial non-restartable.
  - *Eligibility:* Both existing AND new GCP customers can use it, but the trial instance must be created in a NEW Google Cloud project, one trial instance per project. An enabled Cloud Billing account is required: existing users verify billing is enabled; new users must set up a billing account with a card/other payment method (which normally means going through the $300 trial signup). No .edu requirement, no startup requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Console → Cloud SQL 'Get Started' page → 'Experience Cloud SQL at no cost for 30 days' section; 2) choose engine, instance ID, password, region; 3) 'Create free instance'; 4) monitor remaining days on the instance Overview page; 5) click 'Upgrade' before trial/grace ends to keep the instance (switches to normal pricing).
  - *Expiry:* 30-day trial period + 90-day grace period, then stops if not upgraded to paid.
  - *Gotchas:* Old 'Cloud SQL Always Free plan' (free shared-core instance in select regions, 750 h/month E2-micro/C2D + storage/backup/egress allowances) is NO LONGER listed on the Cloud SQL pricing page — the current official free offer is only this 30-day trial instance; egress and backups are charged once you upgrade; SQL Server trial carries 4-core license minimum rules; instance is 'evaluation' — production use not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/sql/docs/mysql/free-trial-instance(offercard:https://cloud.google.com/cloud-sql/pricing)

Regional configurations only (any region; NOT dual-region/multi-region). Not meant for production; no backups/restore, no CMEK, no Data Boost; ~500 QPS reads / ~100 QPS writes achievable.   - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/free-trial-instance(offercard:https://cloud.google.com/spanner/pricing)
- **BigQuery Free Tier (always-free): 1 TiB queries + 10 GiB storage per month, plus free operations** (high) — Current official BigQuery free tier (per billing account, no expiry): the first 1 TiB of query data processed per month is free (on-demand pay-per-query; beyond that $6.25/TiB in US); the first 10 GiB of storage free each month (active and long-term logical/physical). Also free: batch loading from Cloud Storage/local files (uses free shared slot pool 'default-pipeline'), table reads/copies/metadata ops, and the first 2 TiB per month of gRPC Storage Write API (REST streaming inserts cost $0.01/200 MiB beyond). The old '100,000 slot-hours/month free' allowance is not listed on the current BigQuery pricing page. BigQuery free tier is available to all GCP accounts (free-trial or paid) as long as usage stays under the caps.

  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim: free tier usage is automatic for any GCP account; use BigQuery normally. During a $300 free trial you also get the Free Tier at the same time. To keep using it after trial without paying: stay within 1 TiB queries + 10 GiB storage per month.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite monthly recurring allowance (no expiry; Google may change limits).
  - *Gotchas:* Free query allowance is per billing account and monthly; streaming inserts via REST are NOT free (only gRPC write API has the 2 TiB allowance); data egress/export to GCS is free but egress to other services/Internet costs; scheduled heavy queries can exceed 1 TiB in a month; the 100K free slot-hours claim found in older docs is no longer published — assume it no longer applies unless you buy editions/slots.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/pricing(freetierlimitsalsoshownonhttps://cloud.google.com/free)

  - *Expiry:* Fixed windows: GKE 90 days, AlloyDB 30 days, Bigtable 10 days, Looker 30 days.

- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (credits: Start $2,000 / Scale up to $200,000 / Scale AI up to $350,000)** (high) — Replaced the older 'Google Cloud Startups Accelerator' (old /startups now serves this program). Tiers as of today: Start ($2k) — $2,000 in credits, for startups founded in the past 2 years with a working MVP, clear business model, and plans to seek VC funding soon; Scale — Year 1: 100% of GCP spend up to $100,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000 (up to $200,000 total), for Pre-Seed to Series A funded startups (founded within last 3–5 years, tier-dependent); Scale AI — same structure but Year 1 up to $250,000 (up to $350,000 total) for startups using Gemini as a foundation of their primary product (Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years, Series A within last 12 months, founded within last 5 years). Credits cover most GCP pay-as-you-go products incl. Gemini/Gemma model usage; third-party models (e.g., Anthropic) are billed directly and NOT covered. Additional benefits: 12 months Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (separate application, requires not having paid Workspace within 31 days of applying), monthly Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), Google Skills training, dedicated Startup Success Managers as you grow.

Exclusions: IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, dev shops/consultancies/agencies, crypto mining, token distributors against local rules. Must be in a Google-Cloud-GA region with self-service purchasing; company email domain must match the public website domain; a valid 18-char Cloud Billing account ID is required. Acceptance at Google's sole discretion; not for regulated industries.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/startup → 'Apply to the program' (AI-focused: cloud.google.com/startup/ai); 2) apply with a company (non-personal) business email matching your website; 3) provide an active 18-character GCP billing account ID; 4) complete onboarding checklist at cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding; 5) on approval credits are deposited to that billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured as Year-1 (100% up to cap) + Year-2 (20% up to cap) windows over two years; annual re-evaluation of eligibility — prior enrollment doesn't guarantee current-year eligibility; rejected applicants may re-apply later as they raise funding.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit cap 'up to' amounts assume sustained GCP spend; the $350k is AI-tiers only (other startups cap at $200k total); credits cannot be shared, split, or resold (violation terminates program); 're-apply if you got credits before but didn't use them' is allowed only if you still meet in-year requirements; the $5,000-prior-credits ceiling blocks heavy past GCP usage from Scale tiers (a $300 trial is fine). There is also a separate 'Ecosystem tier' (startups in select accelerators/incubators: $25k–$250k credits) per Google's two-pager.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup(AItier:https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai;onboarding:https://cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding)

  - *Source:* https://startup.google.com/programs/accelerator/
- **Google Cloud for Education — Faculty/teaching credits (up to $100 per teacher, $50 per student)** (high) — Verified faculty can apply for Google Cloud teaching credits: up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff member plus up to $50 in GCP credits per student, for hands-on classroom learning. - **Google Cloud for Researchers — GPAR research credits ($5,000)** (high) — cloud.google.com/edu/researchers: researchers apply for $5,000 in Google Cloud research credits. GPAR (Google Program for Accelerated Research, via Google Public Sector) adds: co-investment opportunities with industry-leading pricing for AI accelerators (GPUs/TPUs), early access to new-gen TPUs, foundational models and datasets (AlphaFold, Earth AI, agentic AI like Co-scientist), grant-lifecycle AI tooling, compliance support (HIPAA, CMMC 2.0, FedRAMP), and Google/expert access. Noted on the page as currently only available in the United States.

- **GCP Free Trial — $300 credit, 90 days** (high) — New-customer Welcome credit of $300, valid 90 days from signup, in a preloaded Free Trial billing account; not billed unless the $300 is exhausted; trial access to products may be limited to prevent abuse. During the trial the customer also has access to the Always Free tier. Verified on the official docs page read 2026-10-22: 'The Free Trial gets you a $300 Welcome credit to spend over 90 days... you won't be billed' and 'preloaded with $300 in free Welcome credit which is valid for 90 days'.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have never been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and must not have previously signed up for the Free Trial (effectively one trial per person/company; students qualify the same way). A credit card or other valid payment method IS required at signup (bank-account verification may also be needed in some countries). No .edu requirement, no region stated on the page. The 'credit card NOT required' claim is FALSE for the trial: signup docs state 'you must provide a credit card or other payment method that is valid for the period of the Free Trial'.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/free (or Console) and start the Free Trial; 2) add a valid payment method; 3) $300 is preloaded into the trial billing account; 4) optionally upgrade to a Paid billing account during the trial — unused credit is then retained until 90 days from signup.
  - *Expiry:* Credit expires 90 days from signup. If you do not upgrade to a Paid billing account before 90 days, or spend the whole $300, the Free Trial billing account is closed and all its projects/resources are stopped (i.e., your VPS dies and you lose unspent credit). Free Tier access continues afterwards only with an active billing account.
  - *Gotchas:* Card required even if usage is $0 (temporary authorization hold on signup). Trial end = account closure + stopped resources, not a silent conversion. $300 counts toward the '<$5,000 previously received GCP credits' cap of the startup program. One trial per customer (never a paying GCP/Maps/Firebase user).
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **GCP Always Free tier — free VPS (e2-micro) + 20+ products** (high) — Free-forever monthly quotas (no expiration, subject to change). VPS-relevant Compute Engine: 1 non-preemptible e2-micro VM per month in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa) or us-east1 (South Carolina), plus 30 GB-months standard persistent disk, plus 1 GB/month outbound egress from North America (excl. China/Australia). VM limit is by time, not instance count; GPUs/TPUs are never free. Other verified quotas: Cloud Run 2M requests/mo + 360,000 GB-seconds memory + 180,000 vCPU-seconds + 1 GB North America egress; Cloud Run Functions 2M invocations + 400,000 GB-seconds + 5 GB egress; Cloud Shell 5 GB persistent disk; Cloud Source Repos 5 users/50 GB; BigQuery 1 TiB query + 10 GiB storage; Cloud Build 2,500 build-minutes; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB; Firestore 1 GiB storage + 50k reads/20k writes/20k deletes per day + 10 GiB egress; GKE 1 free Autopilot or zonal Standard cluster per month (credit excludes compute/networking); Pub/Sub 10 GiB messages; reCAPTCHA 10k; Cloud Vision 1,000 units; Cloud Natural Language 5,000 units; Cloud Monitoring API 1M time series; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard (free-tier benefits only in the 3 US regions); Datastream 100 GiB CDC (AlloyDB/Spanner→BigQuery only).
  - *Eligibility:* Active billing account (Paid OR Free Trial) in good standing, and no negotiated pricing contract. A payment method is effectively required because the billing account comes from card signup (trial) or a paid account; no .edu or age requirement. No per-country limit stated, but free Compute/Storage capacity is limited to the 3 US regions.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for GCP (Free Trial or paid account), create a project, launch an e2-micro VM in us-west1/us-central1/us-east1 with ≤30 GB standard pd-SSD — usage within the quotas is never billed. No separate activation step.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; quotas refresh monthly and 'do not expire, but are subject to change'. Free VM regions are fixed (us-west1, us-central1, us-east1 only).
  - *Gotchas:* e2-micro is a 2 vCPU / 1 GB burstable micro instance — hobby VPS, not production. 30 GB disk + 1 GB egress are small; egress beyond 1 GB/month is billed. Stopped-VM disk still consumes the 30 GB-months quota. Free GKE cluster credit does not cover node compute or networking. No free bare-metal or dedicated-server option exists at GCP at all — the e2-micro (or Cloud Run) is the only 'free server'.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — up to $350,000 GCP credits over 2 years (AI & non-AI tiers)** (high) — Successor to the old 'Cloud Startups' program, verified on startup.google.com/cloud (read 2026-10-22): headline 'up to $350,000 in Cloud credits over two years'. AI startups: Year 1 = 100% of GCP spend up to $250,000; Year 2 = 20% of Year-2 spend, up to an additional $100,000 in credits. Non-AI startups: Year 1 = 100% up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% up to $100,000. Third-party model spend (e.g. Anthropic models on Vertex) is billed directly and NOT covered. Credits may only be used by the original applicant — no sharing/splitting/reselling. Non-credit benefits in the program: 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (domain must not have had a paid Workspace plan in the prior 31 days), monthly Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), Google Skills training, dedicated Startup Success Managers/Customer Engineering, and Google Cloud Marketplace co-sell.

Excluded: companies that IPO'd or were acquired, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs, dev shops/consultancies/agencies, crypto mining. No US-registered-entity or .edu requirement is stated on the current page (the legacy program was US-centric).

'Founded within the last 3-5 years' is tier-dependent — older startups may not qualify. Third-party (non-Google) model spend is out of scope. Credits cannot be resold or shared with partners.
  - *Source:* https://startup.google.com/cloud/

Free usage is rate-limited per model on RPM (requests/min), TPM (input tokens/min) and RPD (requests/day, reset midnight PT). Available to students and to bootstrapped/self-funded startups — the startup program also advertises 'free access to the Gemini API in Google AI Studio' on its Prototype track. Not a VPS offer, but a distinct card-free free tier.

  - *Gotchas:* Free tier excludes batch/long-context features of paid tiers; spend-based limits only protect paid accounts. Do not confuse 'free tier' with the $300 trial (which does require a card).

Each states 'new Google Cloud customers get $300 in free credits on signup' alongside.

- **GCP Free Trial: $300 Welcome credit for 90 days** (high) — Free Trial billing account is preloaded with a $300 Welcome credit, valid for 90 days, spendable on any Google Cloud products (including Compute Engine VMs as VPS). You are not billed for any usage during the trial. If you upgrade to a Paid billing account you keep unused credit until it expires 90 days from signup; if you neither upgrade nor spend the $300, the trial billing account is closed and all associated projects/resources are stopped.
  - *Eligibility:* New customers only: you must never have been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and must not have previously signed up for the Free Trial. One trial per person/payment method. A valid credit card or other payment method is REQUIRED at signup (plus bank-account verification in some countries) even though you aren't billed during the trial.

No card is charged during the trial.

  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free/docs/gcp-free-tier
- **Compute Engine Always-Free tier: 1 e2-micro VM + 30 GB disk, perpetual (no card required)** (high) — Free Tier, no expiration, available to all GCP customers: 1 non-preemptible e2-micro VM instance per month (1 vCPU / 1 GB RAM), time-based limit - free until cumulative hours equal the total hours in the month; usable in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa), or us-east1 (South Carolina), used combined across those regions. 30 GB-months standard persistent disk. 1 GB/month outbound egress from North America to all destinations except China/Australia. Related free items on the same page: Cloud Storage 5 GB regional storage, Cloud Run 2M requests/mo, App Engine F1 + 9 h/day B1, Cloud Build, Cloud Functions. GPUs/TPUs are never free.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GCP customer (including trial users, post-trial, or accounts that skip the trial - the Free Tier applies as long as you use products within the monthly limits). A billing account must exist; the Always-Free usage itself does not consume trial credit. Region lock-in: e2-micro free limit only in the 3 US regions; you can still place a paid VM anywhere.
  - *How to claim:* Create a GCP project (billing account, no card needed if you stay within free tier; a trial or a card is only needed the moment you exceed limits), go to Compute Engine -> Create VM, pick e2-micro in us-west1/us-central1/us-east1, attach a 30 GB or smaller standard PD, and run it - compute+disk+1 GB egress stay $0 each month indefinitely.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration - Free Tier has no stated end date, limits reset monthly. Google reserves the right to change/eliminate usage limits with 30 days' notice.
  - *Gotchas:* The '1 TB egress/mo' in the task's known-info is WRONG: official limit is 1 GB/mo outbound from NA (China/AU excluded). The limit is by hours, not by instance - running multiple e2-micros in parallel still costs $0 up to full-month hours. Disk must be STANDARD type, 30 GB or less. Egress >1 GB, GPU/TPU, larger disks, non-US regions for e2-micro, and any usage over the time limit bill at standard rates. A billing account without a payment method: free tier usage is permitted, but exceeding it will suspend service until a card is added.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free/docs/gcp-free-tier
- **GKE Free Trial: 90 days of full Kubernetes Engine capabilities at no fee** (medium) — Separate from the $300 trial: a GKE trial card on cloud.google.com/free - 'Try GKE with the full set of product capabilities for 90 days for no fee', deep-linked to console.cloud.google.com/freetrial?redirectPath=/kubernetes/overview. Use it to run a managed Kubernetes cluster (worker VMs) free for 90 days; useful as a VPS/container-hosting front.
  - *Eligibility:* GCP account holders (trial signup path); subject to abuse prevention - 'Access to products may be limited to prevent abuse'. New-customer trial rules apply for the $300 companion credit.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, click the GKE Free Trial card (or console.cloud.google.com/freetrial?redirectPath=/kubernetes), complete trial signup with a card, and create a GKE Autopilot/Standard cluster for 90 days without paying.
  - *Expiry:* 90-day window from signup; after that standard GKE pricing applies (control-plane fee, node hours).
  - *Gotchas:* Requires the same card-verified trial signup; GKE Autopilot still charges per-node-hour for the 90-day trial at list price after the window; the free GKE *tier* (always-free cluster) also exists but only the cluster fee, not compute/networking. If your real goal is a cheap VPS, raw Compute Engine e2-micro is simpler than GKE.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (rebranded/expanded GCP for Startups): $2K Start tier, $200K-$350K Scale/Scale AI tiers** (high) — Official page cloud.google.com/startup ('Google for Startups Cloud Program'). Tiers: Start = $2K in credits for startups founded within the past 2 years with a working MVP, clear business model, and plans to seek VC funding.
