The 12-month Cloud Academy benefit comes with paid Visual Studio Professional subscriptions, not Azure for Students.

### MongoDB Atlas Free Tier

- **Atlas Free tier ("Free cluster", formerly M0)** (high) — Free-forever managed cluster: $0/hour. 512 MB (0.5 GB) storage, shared vCPU/RAM, up to 100 ops/s (reads+writes combined), 500 max connections, 100 databases / 500 collections, 32 MB sort-in-memory, 50-stage aggregation pipelines, no temp-file-to-disk (allowDiskUse ignored). Egress: not billed, but capped at 10 GB in + 10 GB out per rolling 7-day window, with throttling + 1s cooldown when exceeded. Fixed 3-node replica set, no sharding, no private endpoints/VPC peering, no server-side JS, MongoDB 8.0 only (auto-upgrade, no manual version choice), deployable only in a subset of AWS/GCP/Azure regions. One Free cluster per project (more only via sales contract). Free cluster never expires; auto-paused after 30 days of zero-connection inactivity and may be deactivated under ToS; can be resumed unless pinned to an un-restorable old version. Auth limited to SCRAM, X.509, AWS IAM. Corrections to 'known so far': it is a 3-node replica set, not single-node; docs say daily-snapshot backups CAN be enabled on Free clusters (retains up to 4 snapshots) even though the pricing-page FAQ claims backups unavailable on Free.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone: free Atlas account, no .edu, no age/region gating stated. No credit card or payment method is documented as required for Free clusters (official pages are silent; sign-up flow does not state a card requirement).
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account at mongodb.com/cloud/atlas/register -> create cluster choosing the Free tier -> add a database user and your IP address (or use `atlas setup` CLI). No payment step.
  - *Expiry:* Never expires. Paused after 30 days of zero-connection inactivity (resume anytime); long-idle clusters may be deactivated per ToS, so export data before long inactivity.
  - *Gotchas:* Free egress is capped (10 GB out per rolling 7 days), not unlimited; ops throttled at 100 ops/s; no continuous/hourly backups (docs: daily snapshot, 4-snapshot retention; pricing FAQ says no backups at all - contradictory sources); no private endpoints; fixed MongoDB 8.0; no log download, no Performance Advisor; one Free cluster per project; Atlas Charts on Free clusters is limited (dashboard auto-refresh every 4h, one scheduled report per project per week, since the 2 Jun 2025 pricing change).
  - *Source:* https://www.mongodb.com/docs/atlas/reference/free-shared-limitations/
- **General trial credits / free trial for new users** (medium) — No general free-trial or trial-credit offer found for ordinary new users. The official pricing page (fetched 2026-10-22) lists only: the free-forever Free tier, MongoDB for Startups credits, MongoDB for Educators ($500), and the GitHub Student Developer Pack ($50). No dollar-amount trial with a time limit is offered; the Free tier itself is the perpetual on-ramp.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a (no offer found).
  - *How to claim:* n/a - no claim path because no general trial/credit offer exists on official pages.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Don't confuse with the startup/educator/student credit programs (separate findings). Historically Atlas had promotional trials that are no longer advertised; absence verified on the current official pricing page.
  - *Source:* https://www.mongodb.com/pricing
- **MongoDB for Startups program** (high) — Atlas credits in four tiers - Inspire (bootstrapped, 'baseline credits'), Grow (early-funded, 'expanded credits'), Innovate (VC-backed, 'additional credits'), Scale (rapidly scaling, 'maximum credits') - plus Voyage AI tokens (embedding/reranker), 1:1 expert sessions, and a Developer Support plan that is free for 30 days then $49/month (chargeable against credits). Exact credit dollar amounts are NOT published; they are disclosed in the acceptance email/promo code. Credits have a 12-month activation window from issuance, then must be used within 12 months of activation; they apply to future charges only; extensions case-by-case (contact startups@mongodb.com with >=3 months remaining; check balance/expiry at account.mongodb.com -> Billing -> Available Credits).
  - *Eligibility:* Company <7 years old; funding stage Series A or earlier (form allows up to 'Series B or higher', mapped to Scale tier); single software product/service; development shops and agencies explicitly excluded; first-time applicants only; active company website and LinkedIn profile; company email. No revenue cap stated.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the form on mongodb.com/startups, or get referred by your VC/accelerator/incubator. On acceptance: receive unique promo code by email -> log in at account.mongodb.com -> Billing -> 'Apply Code' -> enter Activation Code (must be Organization Owner or Organization Billing Admin). If no response in 5 business days, email startups@mongodb.com.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months to activate after issuance, then 12 months to use; extension possible case-by-case.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts undisclosed until acceptance; credits do not cover retroactive usage; Developer Support costs $49/month after the free 30 days; one-time participation only; eligibility screening uses Pitchbook valuation data (Dec 2025 footnote).
  - *Source:* https://www.mongodb.com/startups
- **MongoDB for Students (via GitHub Student Developer Pack)** (high) — $50 in MongoDB Atlas credits, free MongoDB certifications valued at $150 via MongoDB University, access to MongoDB Compass, and a student community-forum support link. Distributed through the GitHub Student Developer Pack (listed under 'Infrastructure & APIs' on education.github.com/pack).
  - *Eligibility:* Must be at least 13 years old and enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting program with a verifiable school email or documentation (per the MongoDB pricing-page card); eligibility is verified through GitHub's student-pack process (.edu email or other proof).
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to the GitHub Student Developer Pack (github.com/education), claim the MongoDB voucher -> redeem the code in your MongoDB account to receive the $50 Atlas credit and University certification access. No credit card needed for the credit itself.
  - *Expiry:* No explicit credit-expiry stated on either official page; treat as subject to MongoDB's standard credit terms.
  - *Gotchas:* $50 is modest next to the free-forever M0 tier; certification value is a nominal $150 marketing figure; pack access requires GitHub student verification, which can fail with expired .edu accounts. The MongoDB-owned landing pages (mongodb.com/mongodb-for-students, /learn/education/students/licenses/) are 404 - the offer now lives only in the pricing page and the GitHub pack.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **MongoDB for Educators** (medium) — $500 in free MongoDB Atlas credits, free MongoDB certifications valued at $150, plus access to educator community forums and events. Announced on the official pricing page (fetched 2026-10-22) and on learn.mongodb.com/educators/ (page is JS-rendered; specifics could not be machine-read, so sub-details below are lower confidence).
  - *Eligibility:* Intended for educators (instructors/faculty teaching MongoDB or using it in courses); the exact eligibility definition (K-12 vs higher-ed, required affiliation) could not be confirmed from official text today.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the MongoDB University educators page (learn.mongodb.com/educators/); upon approval receive the $500 Atlas credit and free certification via your MongoDB account.
  - *Expiry:* No credit-expiry dates published on the pages fetched; subject to standard credit terms.
  - *Gotchas:* Landing page is JS-rendered so limits/eligibility should be confirmed by hand before relying on them; $500 credit is for Atlas (managed), not for self-hosted server licenses.
  - *Source:* https://www.mongodb.com/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes found: (1) Feb 2025 - Atlas Flex tier ($8 base, capped ~$30/mo, 5 GB, burst up to 500 ops/s) launched and replaced the older Atlas Serverless and Shared tiers, which are gone; the Free tier was kept 'free forever'. (2) The 'M0' tier is now officially called the 'Free' cluster in docs and UI; docs pin Free clusters to MongoDB 8.0 with no manual version upgrades. (3) Idle Free clusters auto-pause after 30 days of zero-connection inactivity (a Mar 2025 community post cites a 60-day window, so the pause window was tightened to 30 days) and may be deactivated per ToS - a material change for 'free forever' expectations. (4) 2 Jun 2025 - Atlas Charts pricing change: M0 clusters got a restricted free Charts tier (dashboard auto-refresh every 4 hours, one scheduled report/project/week). (5) Legacy student/educator self-hosted license pages (e.g. learn.mongodb.com/.../students/licenses, mongodb.com/education which now 308-redirects to MongoDB University) are gone/redirected - the classic 'student license' free self-managed server offer appears sunset; student value is now delivered through the GitHub Student Developer Pack ($50 Atlas credits + $150 certifications) plus MongoDB University. (6) Backup availability on Free clusters is now documented (daily snapshot, up to 4 retained) despite the pricing-page FAQ still saying 'backups not available for the Free tier' - treat the FAQ as stale and verify in the console.

### Neon Free Plan

- **Neon Free plan (permanent free tier)** (high) — Verified on neon.com/pricing + FAQ today. $0/month, permanent (explicitly 'not a trial'). 100 projects/org; 10 branches/project (extra branches not available on Free); 100 CU-hours compute per project/month; autoscaling capped at 2 CU (8 GB RAM); auto-pause/scale-to-zero after 5 min inactivity (cannot be disabled on Free; suspended compute = $0); 1 GB Postgres storage per project with a 20 GB account-wide cap; 5 GB public egress per project; 6-hour instant-restore history capped at 1 GB of changes; 1 manual snapshot/project; Managed Better Auth up to 60k MAU/project; Object Storage 5 GB/project (free in beta); Functions 10 active + 400 waiting capacity-hours and 1M invocations per project; monitoring retention 1 day; unlimited org members; community support only. CU-hours and egress reset monthly; storage/branch/project counts are continuous. Hitting limits suspends compute or blocks writes — data is never deleted and there is no overage billing on Free. Suspended computes still count against storage.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, no credit card required (official pricing docs: 'The Free plan is permanent (not a trial); no credit card required'), no .edu email or company status needed. No region limits stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at console.neon.tech (neon.com signup); Free plan is default — no activation steps. Included backend primitives (auth, object storage, functions) also come automatically with each project.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry or renewal needed; it is a permanent free tier. Invoices under $0.50 are not collected (paid side).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with paid plans: Launch starts at $0.106/CU-hour, Scale at $0.222/CU-hour, storage $0.35/GB-month on paid. Hitting the 100 CU-hour or 5 GB egress cap suspends that project's compute for the month. The stale pricing.md mirror still says 0.5 GB/project — current limit is 1 GB/project since Oct 2, 2026. No instant-restore, no scheduled snapshots, no IP allow rules, no HIPAA/SOC2/SLA on Free.
  - *Source:* https://neon.com/pricing
- **$20 trial credits (paid-features trial)** (medium) — The official pricing page offers 'Claim $20 in free credits to try paid features' via a console promo link. $20 of prepaid value usable against paid-plan usage (Launch/Scale rates). Exact terms (credit-card requirement, credit expiry, one-time vs repeatable) are not documented on the pages fetched today.
  - *Eligibility:* Not stated on official pages; presented as a general trial-credits claim (console promo code 'beepboop'). No edu/startup requirement indicated.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to https://console.neon.tech/app/?promo=beepboop (linked from neon.com/pricing). 2) Sign in / create account. 3) $20 credits applied to try paid features.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on official pages today.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial credits are NOT the permanent free tier — once the $20 is exhausted, standard usage-based billing applies to whatever plan is selected. Confirm CC requirements and credit expiry before claiming; unverified as of today's fetch.
  - *Source:* https://neon.com/pricing
- **Neon/Databricks Startup Program** (high) — Verified on neon.com/startups today. Two tracks: Self-funded — up to $1,000 in Neon credits; Venture-backed — up to $200K in combined Neon + Databricks credits (hero banner says 'up to $200K in credits'). Credits valid 12 months from date of acceptance. Note a marketing discrepancy: the pricing page's 'Special programs' section still says 'up to $100k in credits', while the dedicated startup page details $1K / $200K. Application is now routed through a Databricks form (Neon is 'part of the Databricks Platform').
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups. Self-funded track: less than $1M in funding, early-stage product/MVP, no investors required. Venture-backed track: VC-backed with at least $1M in funding, or participation in a recognized accelerator (Y Combinator cited). No stated caps on company age beyond 'early-stage', revenue, headcount, or geographic region.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Submit the application at neon.com/startups (Databricks application form, 'Apply Now'). 2) Reviews take a few business days. 3) If qualified, credits are applied directly to your account. Alternative: 'apply or ask your investor' / name an accelerator (e.g. YC) in the investor field.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 12 months from acceptance; no renewal mechanism described. After credits lapse or are exhausted, normal plan billing applies.
  - *Gotchas:* Not automatic — application-based with review. Billing is set up so usage beyond the credits is billed normally (a payment method will likely be needed once credits run out, though the page does not state a CC requirement up front). The '$100k' figure on the pricing page is stale marketing copy versus the page's own $1K/$200K structure. Databricks credits in the VC track may be constrained by Databricks' own program terms.
  - *Source:* https://neon.com/startups
- **Claimable Neon (agent-provisioned no-signup projects)** (high) — Verified on neon.com/claimable-neon today. A coding agent can spin up a real Neon project before a human signs up: 'No account or payment details required.' Includes a temporary Lakebase Postgres database; optional Data API and Managed Better Auth (both stay enabled after claim). Unclaimed projects are capped at 100 MB storage and 1 GB transfer and expire in 72 hours. Provision via scoped agent credentials (auth.md/WorkOS flow, CLI, neon.ts), then a short-lived claim link (claimable.neon.tech/claim?user_code=...) transfers the project into the human's Neon org, establishing ownership with team access.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone — no account, no credit card, no sign-up required for the agent-provisioned phase; to claim into an account you need (or create) a Neon org, where the Free plan applies.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Provision: agent creates project + scoped credentials via GET https://neon.com/auth.md then POST to claimable.neon.tech. 2) Build: agent works against the live DB. 3) Claim: use the claim link to transfer the project into your org.
  - *Expiry:* Unclaimed projects expire after 72 hours (100 MB/1 GB caps until claimed). After claiming, the project lives under your normal plan (Free tier limits if on Free) with no expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* The 72-hour 100 MB cap makes it unsuitable for any sustained workload — it is an on-ramp, not a free tier. After claiming, Free-plan quotas (1 GB storage/project, 100 CU-hrs, 5 GB egress) govern. Data built in the 72h window survives claim; an abandoned project is deleted at expiry.
  - *Source:* https://neon.com/claimable-neon
- **No student/education-specific program found** (medium) — No Neon student or education license offer exists on the official site as of today: neon.com/education resolves to the generic product page (no program), the pricing page lists only Agent Plan, Startup Program and Open Source Program among special programs, and no .edu-email-verified offer or education pricing appears in the FAQ or 2025-2026 changelog entries. Students get the same permanent Free plan as anyone else (no CC, no limits on who signs up). (Out of scope but adjacent: an Open Source sponsorship program exists at neon.com/programs/open-source.md for maintainers, and an 'Agent Plan' for app-generation platforms.)
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no distinct student offer; standard Free plan eligibility (anyone).
  - *How to claim:* Students: sign up for the Free plan normally; no .edu email required or requested by Neon's published materials.
  - *Expiry:* N/A.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with generic Databricks-for-education programs (e.g. Databricks education/community tiers), which are not surfaced on Neon's site. Search budget for further confirmation was exhausted this session; if an education program exists, it is not referenced from Neon's pricing/startup/claimable pages.
  - *Source:* https://neon.com/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes (all from official changelogs/blogs): (1) Aug 15, 2025 — major pricing overhaul to usage-based paid plans with a then-$5/month minimum; Free plan storage moved from 0.5 GB total to 0.5 GB PER project; the $5 minimum was later removed (Dec 12, 2025, 'purely usage-based'). (2) Nov 7, 2025 — Free plan projects raised 20 -> 30; compute prices cut (Launch $0.14->$0.106/CU-hr, Scale $0.26->$0.222). (3) Nov 21, 2025 — Free plan projects 30 -> 50. (4) Dec 12, 2025 — Free plan projects raised to 70/80; branchable Neon Auth (Better Auth) added to all plans including Free. (5) Mar 6, 2026 — unlimited org members on Free plan. (6) Oct 2, 2026 — Free plan storage doubled 0.5 GB -> 1 GB per project (now 20 GB account-wide cap) and project count -> 100. (7) Neon is now a Databricks company: pages read 'part of the Databricks Platform / Built around Lakebase Postgres, by Databricks'; the startup program is applied for via a Databricks form and its VC track pays combined Neon + Databricks credits. Nothing was sunset: no free offer was closed in 2025-2026, only expanded. Watch item: the marketing 'up to $100k in credits' on the pricing page no longer matches the startup page's $1K self-funded / $200K VC-backed structure — likely stale copy.

### PlanetScale

- **Offer (from mapping):** No free tier; free trial only — paid plans from $5/mo (Postgres single node, 10 GB storage + 100 GB bandwidth included)

- **2025–26 change:** NO ACTIVE FREE OFFER FOR STUDENTS OR STARTUPS AS OF 2026-10-22 (verified on official pricing page fetched today + official support KB). Full detail:1) FREE TIER — SUNSETTED. The free Hobby plan (5 free Vitess DBs, historically requiring a card since Aug 2023) was announced for retirement on 2024-03-06 ("PlanetScale forever" blog) and retired on 2024-04-08; non-upgraded Hobby databases were put to sleep (data archived, wakeable for 24h). It was never reinstated. Official KB "Does PlanetScale offer any free plans?" (last updated 2025-06-02) answers "Not at this time." Not restored by the September 2025 Postgres launch. Sources: https://planetscale.com/pricing (fetched 2026-10-22), https://planetscale.com/blog/planetscale-forever, https://planetscale.com/docs/plans/hobby-plan-deprecation-faq, https://support.planetscale.com/hc/en-us/articles/32303734121239-Does-PlanetScale-offer-any-free-plans2) TRIAL CREDITS — NONE OFFICIALLY. No trial, credit, or free-eval offer appears on the official pricing, plans, or billing pages. Third-party directories (creditforstartups "up to $5,000", saasoffers "$500 in credits, verified Oct 2026", cloudroutehq "free migration for YC companies") publish unverified/unofficial numbers with no application form, credit terms, or PlanetScale-backed page; a Sep-2026 directory check (guptadeepak.com) records the startup program as discontinued, noting planetscale.com/startups is generic marketing with no credits, eligibility, or application. A May-2026 third-party guide (solomonsignal.com) claims a 14-day card-required developer trial, but no official page confirms any trial duration. Treat all third-party "credit" listings as unverified. Sources: https://planetscale.com/startups (fetched via search, marketing only), https://guptadeepak.com/startup-offers/programs/planetscale-startups, https://creditforstartups.com/companies/planetscale, https://www.solomonsignal.com/launch-school/tutorials/learn-planetscale-free3) EDUCATION/STUDENT OFFER — NONE. No .edu-verified license, student plan, or education discount exists. The only student-relevant free thing is self-paced course content at https://planetscale.com/learn/courses (free MySQL/DB-scaling/Vitess videos) — not a service freebie.4) PAYMENT GATCHE: Even the cheapest paid plan cannot be used without a valid credit or debit card on file (prepaid cards rejected; Stripe checkout); "all plans require a valid payment method" per official billing docs. No CC-free evaluation path is documented. Source: https://planetscale.com/docs/billing5) PAID ENTRY POINT (for context): Base plan self-serve; Postgres single-node PS-5 from $5/mo, Postgres HA PS-5 from $15/mo, Vitess 3-node PS-10 from $39/mo, Metal from $50/mo; Base includes 10 GB storage + 100 GB bandwidth; egress $0.06/GB beyond included; Vitess NAS overage $0.50/GB (HA production storage $1.50/GB); Enterprise = BYO-cloud with infra pass-through + percentage platform fee. Pricing page generated 2026-09-03; prices vary by region/provider. Sources: https://planetscale.com/pricing, https://planetscale.com/docs/planetscale-plans6) 2025–2026 MATERIAL CHANGES: (a) Postgres went GA Sept 2025; Neki (sharded Postgres) now available (2026). (b) Pricing restructure 2026: old Unlimited/Advanced/Scale/Team tiers removed (2026-07), Scaler Pro renamed Base, new SKU plan set added 2026-10-19 (per getpulsesignal.com/changes/planetscale + docs). (c) Drizzle team joined PlanetScale March 2026. (d) No acquisition found in official sources (one low-reliability SaaSOffers entry falsely claims "acquired by Supabase in 2024" — disregard). (e) Support still offers case-by-case help via support@planetscale.com (e.g., sleeping Hobby DBs, student-without-card requests), but that is discretionary relief, not a published offer.

### Qdrant Cloud Free Tier

- **Qdrant Cloud Free Forever tier** (high) — Permanently free 1-node cluster: 0.5 vCPU / 1 GB RAM / 4 GB disk, 'around one million 768-dimension vectors'. Free cloud-inference works on it, but only with free and external models (free models: sentence-transformers/all-minilm-l6-v2, intfloat/multilingual-e5-small, qdrant/bm25, answerdotai/answerai-colbert-small-v1). Tier is described as 'for testing, and prototypes' and 'without high availability'. Support: community, best-effort response, 99.5% uptime SLA. No HA, no VPC links, no enterprise SSO. Exceeding 1 GB RAM / 4 GB disk prompts upgrade to the Standard (paid) tier, where compute/memory/storage are billed hourly.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone; no .edu, no funding or age cap. Credit card NOT required ('No credit card, no minimum spend' per qdrant.tech/cloud). No region limit stated on the page; cloud availability depends on the chosen infrastructure/region at signup.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.qdrant.io/signup (the 'Start Free' button on qdrant.tech/pricing and qdrant.tech/cloud) -> choose Free plan and a region -> 'spin up a permanently free cluster in under 90 seconds'. No form review; instant activation.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry: explicitly 'free forever', not a trial. Free forever as long as the tier is not upgraded; on upgrade, hourly billing starts for compute, memory, storage, backups, and paid-model inference tokens. Data retention on free clusters is not documented.
  - *Gotchas:* Not high-availability; single node. Free inference is limited to free/external models - paid inference models are not free on the free tier (external models like OpenAI/Cohere/Jina are billed directly by the provider under your API key, no Qdrant markup, but not free). RAM/disk caps (1 GB / 4 GB) are the hard upgrade trigger. Community support only. The ~1M-vector figure is approximate ('around one million').
  - *Source:* https://qdrant.tech/pricing/
- **Free inference tokens on paid clusters (5M tokens/model/month)** (high) — Paid Qdrant Cloud clusters receive up to 5 million free inference tokens per model per month on paid inference models; BM25 hybrid search has no token cap. This is a recurring monthly free allotment, not a one-time trial. Paid inference otherwise billed per 1,000,000 tokens, with per-model rates shown in the Inference tab of the Cloud console. Qdrant-hosted paid models include mixedbread-ai/mxbai-embed-large-v1, qdrant/clip-vit-b-32-text/vision, prithivida/splade_pp_en_v1.
  - *Eligibility:* Any paid Qdrant Cloud cluster (Standard tier or above). No startup/student requirement, no credit cap. Note: the cap is stated per model, so different paid models each get their own 5M/month allowance.
  - *How to claim:* No separate claim step: create/upgrade a paid cluster, enable cloud inference in the console, and the free monthly tokens apply automatically. Prices per model are visible in the Inference tab of the Cluster Detail page.
  - *Expiry:* Recurring: 'per month' allowance, renews monthly with the subscription. BM25 is explicitly uncapped. No sunset date published.
  - *Gotchas:* The 5M figure comes from the FAQ on qdrant.tech/cloud (and is what 'free credits' known-so-far refers to) - it is not restated with the same number on qdrant.tech/pricing itself, so treat the exact cap as FAQ-stated. Free models (including qdrant/bm25) are free on all tiers including the free tier; the 5M free tokens specifically apply to PAID models on PAID clusters. No dollar-denominated trial credits for new customers were found on any page fetched.
  - *Source:* https://qdrant.tech/cloud/
- **Qdrant for Startups program (20% off for 12 months)** (high) — 20% discount on Qdrant Cloud for 12 months from date of acceptance; no dollar cap on savings stated. Extras: partner perks from Hugging Face, LlamaIndex, and Airbyte, expert technical-advice sessions, and co-marketing (social media, Discord talks/webinars, case studies). Reviews in 7-10 business days; after a rejection you must wait 2 months to re-apply. Billing runs through Stripe (not a cloud marketplace), so discounted usage still requires a card.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-seed, Seed, or Series A startups; under 5 years old; less than $5M in funding; must be a NEW user of Qdrant Cloud (existing customers qualify only if the cloud account was created within the last 30 days). Agencies and dev shops are explicitly NOT eligible. No employee/revenue cap and no region limit stated on the page. Credit card IS required to use the discount.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the online form on qdrant.tech/qdrant-for-startups/ (do not email submissions). Provide startup details and planned Qdrant usage. Wait 7-10 business days for review; on acceptance add a credit card to the Qdrant account to activate the discount.
  - *Expiry:* Discount lasts 12 months from acceptance, then standard pricing. Program page current as of fetch (site footer © 2026); no announced end date or 2025-2026 changes on the page.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a 20% discount, not free - ongoing spend still applies and a credit card is mandatory (unlike the free tier). 'New user' rule means most incumbent Qdrant users cannot stack it; the 30-day account window is the only exception. No stacking rules with other discounts are documented. No geographic restriction is stated, but Stripe billing applies.
  - *Source:* https://qdrant.tech/qdrant-for-startups/
- **Education: free self-paced courses + free certification exam (no .edu program)** (medium) — Qdrant's education offer is free, not an academic license: (1) 'structured, free, online courses' on qdrant.tech/course - Beginner Course (<5h, embeddings/vector search/hybrid search + multimodal capstone), Essentials Course (9-12h, indexing/optimization/API), Multi-Vector Search Course (4-6h, ColBERT/ColPali/late-interaction); upcoming Intermediate (Optimizing Performance, Scaling and DevOps) and Advanced (AI Engineer, Search Engineer) tracks. (2) Qdrant Developer Certification at train.qdrant.dev: multiple-choice quiz based on the official docs and courses, self-paced, earns a public badge; registration is email/Google/LinkedIn-only with a verification code - no price is displayed anywhere on the registration or course pages.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to everyone - no .edu email, no student status, no enrollment or funding requirements. Not an education license or classroom license; no student-specific discount exists because the material is already free.
  - *How to claim:* Courses: go to qdrant.tech/course and start a course directly ('See Courses'). Certification: train.qdrant.dev/register -> 'Continue with Google/LinkedIn/email' -> verify -> complete the quiz at your own pace -> earn the badge. For more topics, email devrel@qdrant.com.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry or renewal noted; courses and exam are self-paced with no stated sunset. Upcoming advanced courses currently only have 'Register Interest' Google Form links, not open seats.
  - *Gotchas:* The courses page explicitly says free, but the exam page never labels itself 'free' - absence of any price/payment is the evidence, hence medium confidence that the exam is truly free vs. gated later. Free cloud-inference on self-hosted/open-source Qdrant is NOT available (cloud-only feature; docs suggest FastEmbed as the OSS alternative). Web search was unavailable in this session (budget exhausted), so any 2025-2026 changes not visible on the pages fetched today could not be cross-checked against news/blog posts.
  - *Source:* https://qdrant.tech/course/

### Redis Cloud Free tier

- **Free Redis Cloud Essentials (always-free 30 MB tier)** (high) — Permanently free managed Redis Cloud database: 1 database, 30 MB memory, shared cloud deployment, 30 concurrent connections, best-effort SLA, community support. No time limit, no credit card required. Free plan is a type of Essentials plan; no replication/auto-failover and no REST API on the free tier; upgrade to paid Essentials is seamless with no data loss. (Third-party trackers add ~100 ops/sec ceiling and ~5 GB monthly bandwidth allowance to the free plan - not stated on the official pages read today.)
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Redis Cloud account. No .edu requirement, no company, no credit card. Region limits: only in cloud regions Redis Cloud supports per vendor; region+cloud fixed at subscription creation. Only one free database per account - delete the old one or upgrade to paid before creating another.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at redis.io/cloud (Google/GitHub login or form + email activation), then in the Redis Cloud console: 'New database' -> select the 'Try 30 MB for free' subscription option -> pick database name/version, cloud vendor (AWS/GCP/Azure) and region -> 'Create database'. CTA on the pricing page: /try-free/?rcplan=0.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry - always free while the plan exists; no renewal needed. Third-party (Layerbase, 2026-08) notes the agreement permits data deletion after ~14 days of inactivity. No SLA compensation on the shared deployment (best-effort).
  - *Gotchas:* 30 MB is rarely the binding constraint: 30 concurrent connections and ~100 ops/sec are hit first. No REST API, no high availability on the free tier. No card needed for the free tier, but upgrading to paid requires a payment method + business address. No replication/failover - treat data as disposable. Third-party trackers report the free plan allows only 100 ops/sec and ~5 GB monthly bandwidth.
  - *Source:* https://redis.io/pricing/
- **Pro plan 'First $200 free' (free-usage credit on paid Pro plan)** (medium) — On the official pricing page (fetched 2026-10-22), the Pro plan (dedicated infrastructure, unlimited RAM, multi-DB, Active-Active multi-region, from $0.014/hr with a $200/month minimum) advertises 'First $200 free' - the first $200 of usage is complimentary before the hourly minimum kicks in. Effectively up to ~$200 of free Pro usage. This is a trial/credit on a paid plan, NOT a permanent free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Redis Cloud account; no .edu/startup status stated on the pricing page. It is a paid plan with a usage credit, so a credit card/payment method and business address are required (card-backed billing account).
  - *How to claim:* From redis.io/pricing select the Pro plan 'Try for free' CTA (/try-free/?rcplan=plan3), sign in, add a credit card, create a Pro subscription; the first $200 of usage is covered. No separate application or form.
  - *Expiry:* The $200 credit is consumed as usage accrues; no credit expiry date is stated on the pricing page. Not renewable; billing resumes at the $200/month minimum (or hourly usage) afterwards unless the subscription is deleted. How long the 'free' window lasts depends on how fast $200 of usage is consumed.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: card required, charges begin after the $200 credit is exhausted - delete or manage the subscription promptly. Pro annual-plan variants differ (AWS/GCP only for annual). Throughput/egress limits still apply on the paid tier. 'First $200 free' is the only free-credit offer for general users found on official pages.
  - *Source:* https://redis.io/pricing/
- **Redis for Startups program (free Redis Cloud for qualifying startups)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Redis ran a startup program: 'Free Redis for 6 months - usage limits apply' plus early access to new features, dedicated support with monthly office hours, access to the applied-AI team, co-marketing opportunities, and VC/customer intros. Third-party trackers value it at up to $10,000 in Redis Cloud credits (some listings say up to $25K via Google for Startups perks) with 6-12 months free access and usage limits. Credits apply to Redis Cloud usage; monitor balance/expiration in the console.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups: pre-seed/seed/Series A, privately held, commonly cited funding cap ~$5M total raised, active company website, building real-time/AI workloads with Redis. No VC backing strictly required; approval at Redis's discretion; global. No .edu requirement; not an education offer.
  - *How to claim:* When live: apply via the form on redis.io/startups/ ('Apply here'), sign in with a company email, fill company details/website/funding stage/Redis use case, await approval (typically days to ~2 weeks), then activate credits/promo code in the Redis Cloud console. NOTE: as of 2026-10-22 the English program page no longer serves the program (see sunset_notes) - this path is only valid if Redis re-enables the form.
  - *Expiry:* When live: free period advertised as 6 months (older localized pages said 12 months); credits valid ~12 months. Inactive companies risk removal. Program currently unreachable - see sunset_notes.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit amounts ($10K vs $25K) come from third-party trackers, not a page readable today. 'Usage limits apply' means even the free months are metered. Access was discretionary. Do not plan around this offer without Redis confirming current availability - the English page now redirects to the homepage.
  - *Source:* https://redis.io/startups/
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Redis for Startups (redis.io/startups/): as of 2026-10-22 the English page serves the Redis homepage instead of the program. Two independent trackers confirm the removal (CreditBase 2026-10-02: "redis.io/startups redirects to the Redis home page... needs a check"; StartupPerks 2026-10: "Redis removed the English page in 2026; program page live in German/Spanish/French/Italian/Korean" per its last check, with 'Redis kostenlos fur 6 Monate'). The program was live as of June 2025 ("Free Redis for 6 months"). Treat as sunset/uncertain on the English site; no application form reachable today. 2) Education: no official Redis education license or .edu student program exists - redis.io/education/ is a 404, and nothing on the pricing/docs pages references an education offer; students simply use the always-free 30 MB tier. 3) Essentials restructure December 2025 (per third-party Layerbase/Upstash, not on official pages read today): Essentials is now published as 250 MB-12 GB RAM-only tiers plus "Redis Flex" SSD+RAM tiers up to 100 GB; the free 30 MB tier's shape (30 MB / 30 connections / single shared DB) was unchanged. 4) Free-plan inactivity: the agreement permits deletion of inactive free databases (~14 days per third-party), so "forever free" does not mean "forever data retention". 5) No general free-trial-credit scheme beyond the "first $200 free" on Pro (credit-card required) was found on official pages. Caveat: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task; redis.io facts above come from pages fetched today (pricing, docs) plus third-party trackers, cited per finding.

### ScyllaDB Cloud trials

- **30-day Developer Free Trial (ScyllaDB Cloud)** (high) — A 30-day free trial on small instances — the official pricing FAQ says 'A 30-day trial running on smaller instances (e.g., AWS t3.micro)', 'perfect for exploring the CQL interface and building prototypes'. Docs add: trial includes drivers and monitoring; trial clusters support Vector and Text Search at no cost but are limited to the smallest index node sizes (AWS t4g.medium, GCP e2-medium, 2 vCPU/4 GiB). 'The free trial is limited to one cluster per account.'
  - *Eligibility:* Any user who registers a ScyllaDB Cloud account (no .edu email, no startup requirements). AWS and GCP; deployments supported in most AWS/GCP regions except restricted locations like China. No credit card is explicitly required for the trial itself — the docs quick-start says payment info is only prompted when creating a regular (paid) cluster ('If not already provided, the wizard will prompt you to provide the required payment information').
  - *How to claim:* 1) Register an account at cloud.scylladb.com (auth.cloud.scylladb.com/oauth/account/login); 2) log in and click 'Free Trial'; 3) configure cluster (name, AWS or GCP, limited instance-type selection, optional Vector/Text Search); 4) launch and connect. One free trial per account.
  - *Expiry:* 30 days; not renewable — one trial per account, after which you must 'create a regular ScyllaDB Cloud cluster' on a paid plan (On-Demand, Flex Credit, or Contract).
  - *Gotchas:* No SLA and no usual support during the trial. Not for production workloads. After expiry, upgrading to paid triggers payment-info entry. Instance type on the current pricing page (t3.micro) differs from docs trial-cluster sizes (t4g.medium/e2-medium) — the docs figures refer to the smallest vector/text-search index nodes, so confirm the available storage-instance list in the UI when you claim. Web-search budget for this session was exhausted partway through, so a couple of side checks (exact trial-instance list, current 48-h evaluation instance) could not be re-verified today.
  - *Source:* https://www.scylladb.com/pricing/(FAQ)+https://cloud.docs.scylladb.com/stable/cloud-services/free-trial.html
- **48-hour Production Evaluation (ScyllaDB Cloud)** (high) — 'Production Evaluation: A 48-hour free trial of production-grade instances', 'designed for performance benchmarking and testing ScyllaDB's single-digit millisecond latency under real-world workloads' (current pricing-page FAQ). Launched April 2022 on a high-performance AWS i3.2xlarge instance with known limitations: no backup or repair, no multiple data centers, no cluster resize. Enabled for every ScyllaDB Cloud user since June 2022.
  - *Eligibility:* Any registered ScyllaDB Cloud account; aimed at DevOps teams validating performance. Same AWS/GCP coverage as ScyllaDB Cloud (majority of commercially available regions except China and other restricted locations). No credit card explicitly required for the evaluation itself.
  - *How to claim:* Register a ScyllaDB Cloud account, log in, and start the production evaluation (per the 2022 launch post: 'Create a New Cluster' and select the free trial option in instance types; solutions architects are available to help design the 48-hour scenario).
  - *Expiry:* 48 hours; no renewal. After it ends, continue on a paid plan. No backup/repair during the window, so capture data yourself before expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-grade: no SLA, no support commitment, no backup/repair, no multi-DC, no resize — data loss risk at expiry. The i3.2xlarge instance detail comes from the 2022 launch post; current docs only restate 'production-grade instances' without naming a type, so verify in the UI. Not available for actual production traffic.
  - *Source:* https://www.scylladb.com/pricing/(FAQ)+https://www.scylladb.com/2022-04-13/scylladb-cloud-updates-for-april-2022/
- **ScyllaDB Startup Program (12-month free Enterprise licensing)** (high) — Free ScyllaDB licensing 'in accordance with our Master Software License Agreement' for 12 months. Official page: 'Enterprise-grade, hardened, full-featured, no ceiling on the scale of deployment', plus partner opportunities at no additional cost (virtual events, co-marketing collateral; in-person events possible with travel costs that may apply). A 2025 LinkedIn post by a ScyllaDB sales rep frames it as 'beyond the 50 vCPU / 10 TB free-tier cap' — that cap figure is unverified on any official page and should not be relied on.
  - *Eligibility:* 'A startup with less than $20M USD in total funding'. No company-age limit and no region limit stated on the official page. It is a licensing program (self-hosted / Bring Your Own Account), not a free ScyllaDB Cloud trial — free managed hosting is not included. No credit card required to apply.
  - *How to claim:* Go to https://lp.scylladb.com/startups and submit the application (page has an 'Apply here' link / #apply form).
  - *Expiry:* 12 months; renewal available 'if still within the funding threshold' (i.e., total funding still under $20M).
  - *Gotchas:* Free license only: if you run it on AWS/GCP you still pay your cloud bill (or use BYOA). No end-of-term data policy is stated. The 50 vCPU/10 TB 'free-tier cap' referenced in marketing is not on any official page I could fetch — treat as unverified (my web-search budget ran out, so a deep-dive confirmation wasn't possible).
  - *Source:* https://lp.scylladb.com/startups
- **ScyllaDB University + free education resources (no .edu student license)** (high) — ScyllaDB University (university.scylladb.com) is a free, self-paced NoSQL training platform: courses, videos, hands-on labs, and free accounts; free live 'ScyllaDB University LIVE' virtual trainings (e.g., 2025 editions) that are interactive and not available on-demand afterwards. There is no student-specific software license, no .edu-verified discount, and no dedicated education program found — scylladb.com/education/ is a 404, and no startup/education page appears on the pricing page.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone; a free University account is enough. No .edu email requirement. No credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Register at university.scylladb.com (free account) and take courses or join the next LIVE event listed there.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; no expiry on course content. Free access to labs may use a free trial cluster (one per account, 30 days).
  - *Gotchas:* This is learning content, not a free managed-database offer — the DB itself still comes from the 30-day trial or a paid license. Do not confuse with other vendors' student programs; ScyllaDB has no equivalent student-credit program found as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Source:* https://university.scylladb.com/+https://www.scylladb.com/2025-03-25/scylladb-university-live-and-community-forum-updates/
- **2025–26 change:** No permanent free tier: the only recurring free offers are the two one-time trials (30-day dev trial, 48-hour production evaluation) and the 12-month Startup Program (licensing, under-$20M funded startups). 2025-2026 changes: (1) X Cloud (elastic, tablets-based) launched June 2025 with Flex Credit annual commitments — pricing model changes, but no free tier added; (2) Vector and Text Search became usable on free-trial clusters at no cost, with trial clusters limited to the smallest index node sizes (AWS t4g.medium, GCP e2-medium) and the trial capped at one cluster per account; (3) the 2022 trial definitions (t3.micro dev instance, i3.2xlarge prod evaluation) still appear in the pricing FAQ, while current docs no longer name the dev-trial instance type — the offer survived but its smallest-instance list now differs; (4) no student/education license program exists or was sunset — education/education-licensing offers were not found and scylladb.com/education/ returns 404; free education is via ScyllaDB University only. Note: session web-search budget was exhausted during this research, so instance-level details (current dev-trial instance types, any '50 vCPU / 10 TB' free-tier cap mentioned in a 2025 sales rep's LinkedIn post) are marked medium/unverified.

### Supabase Free Plan

- **Supabase Free Plan (free tier) — the only direct, self-serve free offer** (high) — $0/month, verified on the official pricing page today: 500 MB Postgres per active project (Shared CPU, 500 MB RAM), 1 GB file storage (50 MB max upload), 5 GB egress + 5 GB cached egress, 50,000 MAU included (50K third-party-auth MAU too, raised from 50 in March 2025), 500,000 Edge Function invocations/month, 25 Edge Functions allowed, 2M realtime messages/month, 200 realtime peak connections, 1 GB log ingestion + 100 GB log query, unlimited API requests, 2 active projects per user (limit applies across ALL orgs where the user is Owner/Admin), community support only. Quotas are organization-level. 500 MB database limit is per ACTIVE project (paused/deleted projects don't count). No trial needed: this is the permanent free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Everyone — no .edu email, no startup requirements, no funding cap, no region limit, credit card NOT required. 2 active projects cap per user, counted across every Free-plan org they own/admin.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at supabase.com/dashboard -> create an organization (plan: Free) -> create up to 2 projects. No application, no verification. 'Start for Free' on the pricing page.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite / free forever. Projects auto-pause after 1 week of inactivity (data kept; paused projects don't count toward the 2-active-project limit and can be resumed). Paid-plan behavior (no pausing) is not available on Free.
  - *Gotchas:* Free projects sleep after 7 idle days — bites students between deadlines; data persists while paused but the DB is down until resumed. Hitting quota on a Free org doesn't auto-upgrade: overages simply aren't billed on Free; you must upgrade to Pro ($25/mo + $10/mo compute credits) to get more. SSO MAU and Storage Images Transformed are unavailable on Free. Only 1-day log retention. To mix plans you must create separate orgs. No backups on Free. Third-party sites' 'up to $10K' / '$2,500' / '6 months Team plan free' Supabase startup-credit claims are unverified against any Supabase page and contradict each other.
  - *Source:* https://supabase.com/pricing
- **Student/education offers: NONE (no education license, no .edu discount, not in GitHub Student Developer Pack)** (high) — Supabase has no student program, no education pricing, and no application path. supabase.com/education returns 404; the pricing page contains no student/academic wording; Supabase does not appear in the GitHub Student Developer Pack offer list (checked today); a 2024 Supabase-maintained GitHub discussion confirms the stance is 'use the free tier.' A co-founder once said YC Summer Fellows students (a YC run, not Supabase-run, grants program) received $10K in Supabase credits (2025 cohort only). Students effectively get exactly the general Free plan.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — students get no special tier beyond the general Free plan (no .edu verification, no academic license).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim; sign up for the Free plan like anyone else. If a class project needs more, email support@supabase.io (no program exists to apply to).
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Pricing-page footer copy 'free projects for students and hobbyists' (2020 founding post) is aspirational marketing, not a program. Do not rely on third-party 'Supabase for students' deal pages — they all just restate the free tier plus paid upsells.
  - *Source:* https://supabase.com/pricing
- **Startup credits via partner channels: $300 over 12 months (AWS Activate) and $300 over 1 year (Mercury Perks), plus unpublished YC/accelerator/investor partner tiers** (medium) — No self-serve application on supabase.com. Credits are distributed through partner deal books: (1) AWS Startups/Activate marketplace offer: '$300 Supabase credits for free over 12 months' (AWS-official listing; page now sits behind login at startups.aws.com). (2) Mercury Perks (mercury.com/perks/supabase, live-verified by a third party 2026-10-20): $300 in Supabase credits over 1 year, new Supabase customers only, for Mercury business-account holders (Mercury requires US-formed company). (3) YC and 80+ accelerator/VC partner tier: amounts unpublished, delivered through the partner's own portal (Supabase blog, Oct 2025: 1,000+ YC companies on Supabase; 'Supabase YC Program' gives YC companies credits). Non-dilutive, no equity.
  - *Eligibility:* AWS route: AWS Activate membership (per third-party detail, institutional-VC-backed, pre-seed to Series A, < $5M raised — verify in the AWS portal since the offer page is login-gated). Mercury route: Mercury business customer + NEW Supabase customer. YC/accelerator route: being in that partner's portfolio. No funding cap/age cap published for the $300 tiers themselves.
  - *How to claim:* AWS: redeem the Supabase offer inside the AWS Activate/Startups portal and attach to a Supabase org. Mercury: redeem from the Mercury Perks portal. YC/investor: claim through your accelerator or VC's startup-perks portal. Credits attach to one Supabase organization (moves only via support ticket).
  - *Expiry:* Credits delivered over 12 months and reduce only FUTURE invoices (not already-issued ones); no published review SLA; partner terms can change.
  - *Gotchas:* Page-gating: AWS offer page redirects to login, so current terms must be confirmed inside the AWS portal. The '$2,500', '$3,000 over 12 months', '6 months Team plan free' figures circulating on deal aggregators conflict and are NOT published on any Supabase page — treat as unverified. No public application means bootstrapped startups with no partner affiliation get only the Free plan.
  - *Source:* https://mercury.com/perks/supabase
- **Trial credits: NONE — no free Pro/Team trial or new-signup credit** (high) — Supabase has no trial-credit program for Pro/Team. Paid plans are billed monthly up front (Pro $25/mo + $10/mo compute credits covering one Micro instance; Team from $599/mo). The only 'credits' a new user gets are partner-distributed (AWS/Mercury/YC, above). The 2026-09 OrioleDB public-beta and AI/model-gateway features carry standard Postgres pricing, no student/startup-specific credit.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* 'Pro plan trial' language on third-party perk sites (e.g. 'Pro plan free 12 months, $300 value') describes partner credit redemptions, not a genuine self-serve trial; don't expect one on signup.
  - *Source:* https://supabase.com/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** 1) SUPABASE STARTUP APPLICATION PAGE GONE: aggregator checks (Sep 2026) report supabase.com/startups 404s; today the URL serves only the generic /solutions/startups marketing page with no credit amounts, eligibility, or apply form. Supabase's direct startup-credit application (historically 'apply for credits') appears sunset as self-serve; credits now flow only through partner deal books (AWS, Mercury, YC, 80+ accelerators). Supabase hired a Startup Program Manager (job posted, removed 2025-06-17) and a 'Founder Advocate' (Aug 2025) to run the YC-focused program, so partner-channel credits are the live mechanism. 2) NO STUDENT/EDUCATION PROGRAM has ever shipped: /education is 404; the GitHub Student Developer Pack does not list Supabase; the only student-adjacent offer was YC's Summer Fellows grants (Mar–Aug 2025 cohort) which included a $10K Supabase credit from YC co-sponsors — that cohort ended 2025 and no 2026 equivalent is published (low confidence). 3) FREE-PLAN CHANGES IN 2025: third-party-auth MAU quota raised from 50 to 50,000 on Free (and 100K on Pro/Team) effective March 2025; Free Plan database limit formalized as 0.5 GB per ACTIVE project (paused/deleted projects no longer count); Edge Function count quota raised to 25 (no per-function billing). Core Free-tier numbers (500 MB DB, 1 GB storage, 5 GB egress, 50K MAU, 500K invocations, 2 projects) have been unchanged since the 2024 org-based-billing migration, which added +1 GB egress. 4) NO TRIAL CREDITS ever existed for paid plans; verify current partner terms inside the (login-gated) AWS Startups portal since the AWS offer page redirected to auth as of today.

### Turso Free Plan

- **Turso Free Plan (permanent free tier)** (high) — Free forever, $0/month, no trial window and no trial credits. Verified today (2026-10-22) on the official pricing page: 100 databases; 5 GB total database storage; 500 million rows read/month; 10 million rows written/month; 3 GB monthly embedded syncs; 1-day point-in-time restore; community support. Overage rates (apply once overages are enabled): storage $0.75/GB, rows read $1/billion, rows written $1/million, syncs $0.35/GB. Point-in-time restore and database branching add no usage charge; idle databases cost only storage.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to everyone (individuals, students, companies); no .edu email, no minimum-age, no funding requirements. Sign up with GitHub. Credit card NOT required (page states 'Start free, no credit card required'). No region restriction is published for the free plan; you choose the primary location of each database. Commercial use on Free is allowed with no per-seat or revenue cap.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to turso.tech and sign up (GitHub login). 2) The Free plan is applied automatically — no activation, no coupon. 3) Create databases via dashboard or `turso db create` CLI. 4) Connect with SDKs (@libsql/client etc.) or the stateless HTTP API. Optional: enable/disable 'Overages' in billing settings to control what happens past quotas.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — standing $0/month tier with no end date and no renewal. (Contrast: third-party pages that call it a '365-day student grant' are wrong; the grant framing is aggregator content, not Turso's.)
  - *Gotchas:* Task's pre-known numbers are stale: 9 GB belongs to the paid Developer plan, the free tier is 5 GB; '1 GB egress' no longer exists — Turso meters rows read/written and syncs, not GB of egress. The first limit you hit is 10M rows written/month (~333k writes/day). A full-table-scan SELECT costs one row-read per row, so unindexed queries burn the 500M read quota fast. If overages are disabled, hitting a cap blocks that metric instead of billing. Free plan has no audit logs, no DPA/IP allow lists/SSO (paid-only features). Edge read replicas were stopped for new accounts in Jan 2025; embedded replicas (sync to a local SQLite file, included in the 3 GB sync quota) are the replacement. No trial credits are granted to new accounts.
  - *Source:* https://turso.tech/pricing
- **Turso Startup Program (Turso for Startups)** (high) — Launched 2026-05-11. For early-stage venture-backed startups: 'generous Turso credits' (dollar amount NOT published — sized at approval), plus priority access to Turso's engineering team for onboarding and architecture reviews, early access to unreleased features, and co-marketing (blog features, case studies). Verified today on the official turso.tech/startups page.
  - *Eligibility:* All three published criteria: (1) venture-backed startup with at least one round of funding; (2) building an early-stage product or actively scaling; (3) verifiable funding from recognized investors or accelerators. No company-age limit and no funding-cap published; bootstrapped/unfounded startups do NOT qualify under published criteria (they can use the free tier). No .edu/education track — the program explicitly targets VC-backed founders. Credit card not required to apply.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Open https://turso.tech/startups and fill in the application form (full name, company email, role, startup description, year founded, major investor/accelerator, latest round). 2) Applications reviewed on a rolling basis; Turso typically responds within a few business days. 3) Once accepted, credits are applied to your account and you start building immediately.
  - *Expiry:* Credit amount and spend window are not published. Confirm the dollar value and activation/expiry dates in writing at approval; unused credit balances on programs like this are typically forfeited, and after credits run out you move to standard usage-based pricing (Developer $4.99/mo billed yearly, $5.99 monthly, then overages).
  - *Gotchas:* Amount 'set at approval' — do not plan budgets around it. Review is selective (VC-backed, verifiable funding), not automatic. Program page carries no published terms page; co-marketing is an opportunity, not a guarantee. After the 2026-10-02 Supabase acquisition, watch for the program migrating into Supabase's ecosystem — the page was live as of today, but terms may consolidate.
  - *Source:* https://turso.tech/startups
- **Student / education offer: none exists (free tier is the de-facto student offer)** (medium) — No Turso education license, no .edu-verified student program, no student discount page exists. Searches of turso.tech (pricing, startups, partners, blog) surface no education offer; the free tier described in finding 1 is what students use, and it is open to anyone without verification. Third-party 'Turso student deal' listings (e.g., The Modelverse, agentdeals) merely repackage the free tier and add fabricated '365-day grant / .edu verification' framing that Turso does not state.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no student eligibility criteria because no student program exists. Free tier requires no .edu email and no verification of any kind.
  - *How to claim:* N/A — nothing to claim beyond the free-tier signup (finding 1).
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Do not cite aggregator 'student deal' pages (thefreebie/Modelverse/agentdeals-style): they invent verification steps and expiry dates ('Oct 17, 2027 tripwire', '365-day grant') that are not in Turso's official pages. Only turso.tech is authoritative.
  - *Source:* https://turso.tech/pricing
- **Trial credits for new accounts: none** (high) — No time-boxed trial credits (e.g., $X for 30 days) are offered on the official pricing page or blog. The only credits on the table are the unpublished startup-program credits (finding 2). New accounts simply get the free tier; paid plans start at Developer $4.99/mo billed yearly ($5.99 monthly), Scaler $24.92 ($29 monthly), Pro $416.58 ($499 monthly), Enterprise custom.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A
  - *How to claim:* N/A
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* If a vendor page advertises a 'free trial with credits' for Turso, treat it as aggregator noise unless it links turso.tech terms.
  - *Source:* https://turso.tech/pricing

### Upstash

- **Upstash Free Tier (all products: Redis, QStash, Workflow, Vector, Box, Blob) — the de facto free offer for students and startups** (high) — Permanent $0 plans, open to everyone (no .edu email, no startup age/funding cap). Redis: $0/mo, 500K commands/mo, 250 MB data, 10 GB bandwidth, up to 10 free databases (1 DB per plan row in comparison table; FAQ says up to 10 free DBs, $0.50/DB after, max 100), 10K cmd/s, 1 index (10K docs/index), 10 MB request / 100 MB record size; no Multi-Zone HA, no uptime SLA, no IP allowlists, no encryption at rest, no SOC-2/HIPAA, no Prod Pack, regions on AWS/GCP/Vercel (no FLY). QStash: 1,000 messages/day, 50 GB bandwidth, 1 MB message, 1 URL group (100 endpoints), 10 queues, 10 schedules, 7-day max delay, DLQ+log retention 3 days. Workflow: 1,000 steps/day (soft limit), 10 concurrent steps, 1 MB message. Vector: 200M vectors x dims, 1536 max dims, 10K queries/day, 1 GB data+metadata, 10 indexes free, top-k default 1000; regions LIMITED to N. Virginia (AWS), Ireland (AWS), Iowa (GCP). Box (serverless compute): 10 concurrent boxes, 5 CPU hours/mo, $1 LLM token budget/mo (400 after cap; BYOK allowed), AWS us-east-1 only. Blob (S3-compatible object storage): 1 GB storage, 10K simple ops/mo, 2K advanced ops/mo, 10 GB egress/mo, global CDN (no region choice), 'free plan is never charged'; overage = requests stop until 30-day window rolls (hard cap, no bill). No credit card required on any: adding a card upgrades the resource to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone: sign up free at console.upstash.com (GitHub/Google/email). No student verification, no startup criteria, no credit card. Region limits apply per product (Vector 3 regions; Box us-east-1 only).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create free account at console.upstash.com (no card). 2) Create a database/queue/index/box/bucket — it lands on the Free plan automatically. 3) Use it; each product's free allowance renews monthly/daily forever.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry: free plans renew indefinitely (daily allowances for QStash/Workflow/Vector reset daily; monthly for Redis/Blob/Box).
  - *Gotchas:* Redis 'retry' semantics: QStash FAQ says 'each delivery attempt counts as one message', so failed-retried deliveries eat the 1,000/day; Workflow retries and parallel/context calls consume extra billed steps (context.call/invoke = 2 steps each). Blob stops serving (not billed) when caps hit. Adding a credit card silently moves you to paid PAYG ($0.20/100K Redis commands, $0.25/GB storage, 200 GB/mo bandwidth free on PAYG). Free tier lacks HA/SLA/SOC2/encryption-at-rest — fine for student projects, not production compliance. Up also offers ad-hoc free trials/PoC on request via support@upstash.com.
  - *Source:* https://upstash.com/pricing/redis.md(freeplan+FAQ);also/pricing/qstash.md,/pricing/vector.md,/pricing/workflow.md,/pricing/box.md,/pricing/blob.md,allread2026-10-22
- **start-redis instant trial database (free trial credit, no signup)** (high) — POST https://upstash.com/start-redis returns a ready, free Redis database with credentials, quickstart and a console claim-link — no signup, no credit card, no API key. Unclaimed databases are deleted after 3 days; an agent is expected to send a User-Agent identifying itself (optional Idempotency-Key makes retries return the same DB).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, including AI agents; no account or card needed.
  - *How to claim:* curl -X POST https://upstash.com/start-redis (send a User-Agent header); open the console URL in the response to claim it into an account before the 3-day deletion.
  - *Expiry:* Trial DB expires/deleted after 3 days if not claimed; once claimed it lives on the normal Free plan.
  - *Gotchas:* It is a trial, not the free tier: data is lost at 3 days unless claimed. Claiming drops it into the standard Redis free limits above.
  - *Source:* https://upstash.com/redisandhttps://upstash.com/pricing/redis.md('ForAIAgents'section)
- **Upstash Open Source Program — up to $1,000/month credits for OSS projects (CLOSED to new applications)** (high) — $1,000/month credit: 'We cover all Upstash-related costs up to $1,000 per month. Build on any Upstash product(s) without worrying about billing.' Perks: direct technical support, priority support 'just like our Pro customers', co-marketing/technical articles. Credit renews monthly with no fixed end date. Eligibility is 'open-source projects building on Upstash' — no star count or license criteria published. As of the page read on 2026-10-22: 'The open-source program is currently closed and not accepting new applications.' Questions go to program lead @joshtriedcoding.
  - *Eligibility:* Open-source project maintainers/builders (individual devs qualify, not just startups); program currently closed, so no one can claim new applications right now.
  - *How to claim:* No application URL while closed: follow Upstash / email program lead (@joshtriedcoding) to hear about reopening.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are monthly while in the program; program intake currently closed.
  - *Gotchas:* Third-party lists still advertise it as open ($1,000/mo grant) — the official page says closed today; verify before relying on it. No student-specific program exists; students get the general free tier instead.
  - *Source:* https://upstash.com/open-source(read2026-10-22)
- **Upstash Postgres / Kafka free metered tiers (previously 'free to start') — NO LONGER LISTED AS PRODUCTS** (high) — The task's premise includes Postgres and Kafka metered free tiers, but as of 2026-10-22 neither appears anywhere on upstash.com: the pricing page lists only Redis, QStash, Workflow, Box, Blob; /pricing/postgres, /pricing/kafka, /postgres, /kafka all 404; docs only mention Kafka as the open-source broker (QStash comparison page); docs search confirms no Postgres or Kafka product exists. Kafka survives only in blog content (including 'kafka-connectors-deprecation'), and QStash/Workflow now serve the messaging use case. No Postgres/Kafka free-tier limits can be quoted from official sources today.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — offers not present on the official site.
  - *How to claim:* n/a — nothing to claim.
  - *Expiry:* Sunset from the product lineup by 2026-10-22 (messaging replaced by QStash/Workflow).
  - *Gotchas:* Treat any current listing of Upstash Postgres or Upstash Kafka free tiers as stale. If you need Kafka-like streaming on free, use Upstash QStash (1,000 msgs/day free) or Workflow.
  - *Source:* https://upstash.com/pricing,https://upstash.com/sitemap-0.xml,https://upstash.com/docs(allchecked2026-10-22)

No official Upstash page substantiates it: no /startups or startup-program page exists (404s), the homepage nav and pricing pages contain no startup program link, and the Redis free-tier FAQ has no startup section.
  - *Eligibility:* Allegedly early-stage startups on a new account; criteria unknown.
  - *How to claim:* Unknown — no official application path found; would require contacting sales@upstash.com.
  - *Expiry:* Unknown.
  - *Gotchas:* Third-party aggregator claim only; possibly an old promo, possibly a confused entry (Upstash also historically ran startup perks via partner programs). Low confidence — do not plan on it without written confirmation from Upstash sales.
